What Jim Mora said after CSU football loss to Boise State

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Colorado State has lost six of its last seven games.

The latest was a lopsided 64-38 defeat to top-25 Boise State on Nov. 7 at Canvas Stadium as CSU's defense was shredded again, this time for nearly 700 yards.

Here's a look at some of the most notable things CSU coach Jim Mora said after the game.

Watch the full press conference via the video at the top of the story.

"I was proud of the way our offense fought tonight. Proud of the way our team fought, we just couldn't get them stopped. It's no secret that we can score points and we did it tonight without John Holthaus, who is one of the top tackles in the conference, should be an all-conference selection. And Juice (Vereen), Mel (Brown) and Durell (Robinson). It gave some young guys a chance to shine.

"Once again the story of the day is our inability to stop people and keep points off the board. I've been doing this a long time and I've never been involved in something that's like this. It's disappointing. I'll tell you this, our players continue to work hard, they continue to have a great attitude, they come into work every day. It is a job and they come in every day with a great mindset, energy, enthusiasm. They have good practices then we go out on the field and it has not translated well but we're not going to abandon what we're doing because I believe in it and because it's going to get better."

"That's a question I can only answer partially. I can answer it fully, but I'll decline to answer part of it just because it wouldn't be the right thing to do. But I'll tell you this, we are doing the right things defensively in terms of scheme. And I know that because I've coached defense for decades and been fortunate to be around some of the best defenses in football, quite frankly. You go back and look at the Saints and 49ers and some of those teams and we were great.

"We know what it looks like. We got a little bit of a late start on it, which is my fault. We're playing catch up. It's hard to teach a new scheme midseason. It seems so simple in practice and seems so simple in a meeting room when you have a clicker in your hand and you can run it back and forth and you can slow it down and you can teach it that way. Then the lights come on and you're playing an opponent that's going to go at warp speed and has really good players and is one of the top-ranked teams in the country, things change. We just have to have the resolve to continue to try to really emphasize the right things. I'm not going to make you a promise, but this defense here eventually is going to be something our fans will be proud of. It will be something to make the fans here and our student body and administration proud."

"You have no option (but to keep showing up). You can tap out and you can hang up your cleats and go away, but these guys aren't built like that. This will not be the hardest thing they face in their lives. Us as adults in here know that. I just expect them to keep coming back hard because that's what they've shown they will do. It's not easy. It's not easy at all."

"There's just a real inconsistency to the way we're performing right now defensively. You see some really good plays and then we're not exacting enough in our gap control in the run, we're not challenging enough in certain situations in pass. You see that third down dig route they caught and you'll go 'well it's the DBs.' Well, it wasn't. Underneath we weren't in our proper space. We were chasing ghosts, chasing decoy routes. There's a function of playing with discipline because these are disciplined players."

Sports reporter Kevin Lytle can be found on social media on X, Instagram and Threads @Kevin_Lytle and on Bluesky.

This article originally appeared on Fort Collins Coloradoan: What Jim Mora said after CSU football loss to Boise State

Dana White reacts to President Trump’s job offer

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Dana White already runs the UFC, Zuffa Boxing, and Power Slap, but President Donald Trump apparently thinks he has room for one more job. On Saturday, Trump announced that he had offered White the position of White House press secretary.

Now, White has addressed the offer for the first time. Speaking at a boxing event in London on Saturday night, the UFC boss joked about his already packed schedule, while also revealing that he's headed to Washington, D.C., to meet with the president.

The White House has been without a press secretary since October, when Katie Zacharia was fired after just five days on the job. Zacharia had replaced Karoline Leavitt, who stepped down in August to spend more time with her family.

On Saturday afternoon, Trump took to Truth Social and offered the role to White, one of his most loyal allies. "I am pleased to announce that the Great Dana White, a violent Fighter and Warrior, has been offered the job of Press Secretary of the United States," Trump wrote.

Trump also asked White to bring several UFC stars, including Islam Makhachev, Khabib Nurmagomedov, and Justin Gaethje, with him as assistants. "We need these people to defend us against the Lunatic Left. Dana will be a Press Secretary like none other. Dana, please take the job!" Trump added.

White's relationship with Trump goes back decades, and he has spoken at the last three Republican National Conventions. In June, the UFC also held its Freedom 250 event on the South Lawn of the White House, which coincided with Trump's 80th birthday.

Shortly after Trump's post, White shared an image of himself behind the podium in the White House briefing room on his Instagram story, with the caption "On to the next chapter." White House communications director Steven Cheung also chimed in, writing, "Hell yeah! Do it, @DanaWhite! It'll just be like old times!" in a post on X.

But when White was asked about the offer at the Zuffa Boxing press conference in London, White told the media that he's "flying to Washington, D.C. right now" and will meet with President Trump on Monday.

"I've got a lot of jobs already," White added.

We'll have to see what happens, but White certainly didn't shut down the possibility.

The post Dana White reacts to President Trump's job offer appeared first on The Comeback: Today's Top Sports Stories & Reactions.

Hank Brown, No. 21 Iowa defense combine to give Northwestern first loss at new stadium, 24-10

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

EVANSTON, Ill. (AP) — Hank Brown threw for a touchdown and ran for another, and Braeden Jackson returned a kickoff for a TD for the second time this season as No. 21 Iowa beat Northwestern for the fifth straight time and dealt the Wildcats their first loss at the new Ryan Field, 24-10 on Saturday night.

The Hawkeyes (7-2, 4-2 Big Ten) have allowed only two touchdowns in the past three games. They recorded six sacks and didn't allow the Wildcats inside their 26-yard line until the fourth quarter.

Brown was 16 of 21 passing for 126 yards with an interception after going 20 for 24 in last week's win over Wisconsin.

Northwestern has dropped two straight after a 6-1 start. The Wildcats (6-3, 3-3), who won twice at their temporary lakefront stadium before winning the first three games at their new $875 million home, were trying to move to 6-0 at home for the first time in 122 years.

Iowa, which scored on 98-yard drives in each of its previous two games, had a 97-yard drive Saturday.

Brown was 4 for 4 on Iowa's opening possession before sprinting for a 21-yard touchdown on a quarterback draw. He found Tony Diaz for a 24-yard TD catch early in the second quarter to cap the 97-yard drive and make it 14-0 after L.J. Phillips had a 45-yard run to get the Hawkeyes near midfield.

Phillips, who ran for 1,920 yards at South Dakota last season, had his best game in an Iowa uniform with 128 yards on 21 carries and two catches for 14 yards.

Northwestern's Nicco Marchiol, a West Virginia transfer filling in for injured starter Aidan Chiles, was 24 for 32 for 181 yards and one touchdown to go with 52 yards rushing, but Iowa held Northwestern to 2.8 yards per carry and stuffed the Wildcats twice on third-and-1 and twice on fourth-and 1.

Robert Fitzgerald picked off an underthrown pass by Brown midway through the third quarter to give the Wildcats some life, but the drive stalled and Northwestern settled for a 45-yard field goal from Jackson Kleather to break up the shutout.

Jackson returned the ensuing kickoff 96 yards to make it 21-3. He had a 99-yard touchdown return in Iowa's win at Michigan in September. Caden Buhr added a 52-yard field goal later in the third.

Marchiol connected with Joseph Hilmon II on a 2-yard touchdown pass with 1:51 to play.

Iowa: Linebacker Cam Buffington had 12 tackles to lead the Hawkeyes for the fifth time this season, his fourth game with double-digit tackles.

Northwestern: The Wildcats had been converting third downs at a 46% clip and were 5 for 7 on fourth down with the dual threat Chiles at quarterback this season, but Iowa held Northwestern to 1 for 11 on third down and 1 for 4 on fourth down.

Northwestern: At No. 11 Ohio State next Saturday.

Sophie Cunningham’s Post-Election Message Sparks WNBA Debate

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sophie Cunningham knows how to stir the pot, and she did it again right after the U.S. midterm elections. The Indiana Fever guard posted a cryptic message on her Instagram story that left fans guessing. She did not talk about the election results directly. Instead, she shared a long excerpt from entrepreneur Andy Frisella about personal excellence being the foundation of a great country. This move stands out because other WNBA players often take very direct political stances. Cunningham chose a different path, focusing on individual behavior rather than policy.

Cunningham has faced heat before for her comments on transgender women in sports. During the season, she made waves about her views. "I want to protect young girls in a locker room, or young girls in sport who shouldn't have to go against biological men," Cunningham told ESPN. She later added, "I was asked a question, and that's simply what it was. I wasn't trying to make a statement." This history makes her recent post even more interesting. People are connecting the dots between her views on sports and her views on the country.

The full text she shared from Andy Frisella was quite long and specific. It read, "A culture of personal excellence is the foundation of a great country. There is no election, politician, policy, or law that can substitute for that … and the best thing about that is the same behaviors that make you a better American also make your own life better quickly. If we want a better America … It starts with better Americans. There is no other way. Personal excellence is the Ultimate Rebellion." This message shifts the focus from what the government does to what each person does.

Steelers Hall of Fame linebacker Jack Ham hospitalized

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Pittsburgh Steelers legendary linebacker Jack Ham has been hospitalized in Washington. The Hall of Fame linebacker was getting set for Penn State vs. Washington, as he serves as the commentator for Penn State's radio broadcast. Penn State head coach Matt Campbell says Ham, 77, is in stable condition, and Ham's radio partner, Steve Jones, for playing a big part in Ham being okay.

"First of all, I think we should all know Steve Jones and the heroic effort that he did for somebody he loves dearly," Campbell said. "And if it's not for Steve and what he did, I don't know if we would have as good of news as we do right now. And then I want to also thank the University of Washington, their athletic director, their emergency team were incredible. They were outstanding. I think, hopefully, as all of you know, Jack is in stable condition right now, and obviously, the emotion of that."

Ham was part of all four Super Bowl-winning teams of the 1970s in Pittsburgh. He was a six-time All-Pro and was inducted into the Pro Football Hall of Fame in 1988.

MLB free agency: Six international players to know for the 2026-27 offseason

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Major League Baseball's free agency period opened earlier this week and teams are now clear to pursue any roster upgrades they deem necessary. That includes players coming over from Japan's Nippon Professional Baseball league, South Korea's Korea Baseball Organization, and Taiwan's Chinese Professional Baseball League. Those are high-level leagues that have sent many impact players MLB's way over the years.

Look no farther than the Dodgers to see how important international talent can be to a club. The Dodgers employed four different former NPB and KBO players this past season, including two-way star Shohei Ohtani, ace Yoshinobu Yamamoto, and starter Roki Sasaki. Ohtani was signed as an MLB free agent, but the others went to the Dodgers through the posting system.

Per the posting agreement with MLB, posted NPB and CPBL players get a 45-day window to negotiate and sign with an MLB team. KBO players get a 30-day window. Players who do not sign during that posting window return to their previous team in NPB, CPBL, or KBO. The MLB team that signs a posted player pays a fee to the player's former NPB, CPBL, or KBO team.

$5M plus 17.5% of contract value over $25M

$9.375M plus 15% of contract value over $50M

For example, the White Sox paid Munetaka Murakami's former team, the Yakult Swallows, a $6.575 million posting fee on his $34 million contract last offseason. That's $5 million on the first $25 million plus 17.5% of the remaining $9 million, which is $1.575 million. The $5 million plus $1.575 million equals the $6.575 million posting fee paid to the Swallows.

Unlike last lockout, MLB will not be pausing the signing window during the expected lockout, according to The Athletic. In other words, if owners lock players out on Dec. 1 as expected, that date will essentially serve as the same deadline for already posted NPB players unless the lockout ends before their 45-day window expires.

There are no superstar-caliber players like Ohtani or Yamamoto available in this winter's international free agent crop. There are, however, several interesting players who can improve an MLB roster in 2027. Here are the six best NPB, CPBL, and KBO players to know this offseason.

Japan's Triple Crown winner hit .306/.383/.609 with 39 home runs in 2026. He has a similar skill set to Murakami but is not exactly Murakami 2.0. He doesn't have quite as much power (but has plenty of it) and won't walk nearly as often, but he does swing and miss a bunch and is unlikely to remain at the hot corner long-term. Sato is a very aggressive hitter who ran a 66% contact rate this past season. That is below the 77% MLB average. The consensus seems to be that he will land at first base or in a corner outfield spot if not next year, then soon. Murakami successfully overcame his contact issues to slug 35 homers in only 127 games in 2026 around a hamstring injury. At minimum, that will have MLB teams reconsidering how they evaluate the Japan-to-MLB transition. Like Murakami, Sato carries risk, and like Murakami, his power makes that risk worth the dice roll. The Hanshin Tigers officially posted Sato on Nov. 1.

Listed at 5-foot-8, Taira gets outlier extension down the mound (close to seven feet despite his height), which creates a low release window. Hitters rarely see the ball come from where Taira releases it. He's not a gimmick pitcher either. Taira sits mid-90s with his four-seam fastball and backs it up with a cutter, sinker, sweeper, slider, changeup, and splitter. The sweeper and slider are distinct pitches, as are the changeup and splitter. The Seibu Lions used Taira as a reliever in 2024 and 2025, then started him in 2026, and he handled the assignment well. The breaking balls are just OK, and the fact of the matter is that pitchers this height don't often hold up under a starter's workload. I would assume the team that signs Taira will give him a chance to start. In the long run, though, he might be a reliever, perhaps even a multi-inning setup man. Taira was officially posted on Nov. 1.

A down 2026 season took some of the shine off Itoh, who won the 2025 Eiji Sawamura Award (Japan's Cy Young equivalent) after throwing 196 ⅔ innings with a 2.52 ERA. To be sure, Itoh pitched well in 2026 (3.18 ERA in 172 ⅔ innings), but he was not as consistently dominant as in the past. The 5-foot-9 right-hander is a seven-pitch pitcher with a low-90s fastball and the usual array of secondary pitches (sinker, cutter, curveball, slider, sweeper, splitter), none of which stands out as a plus offering. Itoh has thrown at least 150 innings in each of the last six seasons. He's seen as a crafty back-end starter in MLB, or perhaps even a reliever (Itoh sat 96 mph out of the bullpen in the World Baseball Classic). The Hokkaido Nippon-Ham Fighters posted Itoh on Nov. 1.

Roh has an unusual agreement with the Hanwha Eagles. He signed the richest deal in KBO history in February (11 years and roughly $23 million), and as part of the deal, the Eagles agreed to post him for MLB teams this offseason, and Roh agreed to return to the Eagles to finish his career when his big-league stint is over. This past season, Roh, 26 in November, authored a .283/.360/.505 batting line with 29 home runs. He has real right-handed power and a good approach at the plate. There are concerns about his propensity to swing and miss as well as his arm at the hot corner. The Eagles formally posted Roh on Nov. 1.

The Seibu Lions posted Takahashi last offseason, but he was unable to find a contract to his liking, so he returned to Japan for another season and threw 148 innings with a 2.64 ERA. Takahashi, 30 in February, is an international free agent now, meaning he does not have to be posted and can instead negotiate at his own pace. More of a ground ball pitcher than a bat-misser, Takahashi sits in the 91-95 mph range with his fastball and has two good secondary pitches in his slider and splitter. He also throws a cutter and curveball. Takahashi is considered a potential innings guy for the back of an MLB rotation.

Tseng pitched for Chinese Taipei in the World Baseball Classic in 2023 and 2026 and he has been one of the CPBL's top relievers the last few years. In 2026, he saved 25 games with a 1.80 ERA and 65 strikeouts in 52 ⅓ innings. Tseng's fastball typically sits in the mid-90s and has run as high as 100 mph in the past, though it has been a few years. His go-to secondary pitch is a breaking ball that needs improvement for Tseng to carve out an MLB career as more than a middle reliever.

MLB rumors: Max Scherzer mulling another season, Ty France drawing interest, top Taiwanese reliever posted

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The 2026-27 MLB offseason is a bit more than a week old and already the hot stove moves and rumors are coming in. We've ranked the top 50 free agents and top 25 trade candidates, and built you a calendar of the important offseason dates and deadlines you need to know. Here now are Saturday's hot stove happenings.

Future Hall of Famer Max Scherzer will pitch in "lockout games" this offseason to determine whether he will continue his career, reports the New York Post. The collective bargaining agreement expires at 11:59 p.m. ET on Dec. 1. Players on opposing teams work out together throughout the offseason and figure to put together games to stay sharp during the lockout.

Scherzer, 42, made 18 starts around injuries with the Blue Jays this season. If 2026 was his final season, he went out with a bang. Scherzer struck out seven in seven innings of one-run ball in Game 162. He is currently ninth all-time with 3,557 strikeouts, three behind former Tigers teammate Justin Verlander. We did not rank Scherzer among our top 50 free agents.

Multiple teams have interest in free agent Ty France, according to The Athletic. A deal could come together quickly. France had a career year in 2026 after opening his stance a bit, which unlocked more hard-hit ability. He's a terrific defensive first baseman as well. We ranked France as the offseason's 23rd-best free agent, and the second-best available first baseman.

It's unclear which teams are in the hunt for France, though there is no shortage of contenders who could use the 32-year-old. The Diamondbacks, Guardians, Mets, Rangers, and of course Padres stand out as potential landing spots. Given his career year and the surprising lack of right-handed power in the game right now, France could command a multi-year contract

The Fubon Guardians of the Chinese Professional Baseball League have posted righty reliever Jyun-Yue Tseng for MLB teams, reports the Associated Press. Officially, Tseng has until Dec. 22 to sign, though the expected lockout means his signing deadline is really Dec. 1. We did not rank him among our top 50 free agents.

Tseng, 25, pitched for Chinese Taipei in the World Baseball Classic in 2023 and 2026. He saved 25 games with a 1.80 ERA and 65 strikeouts in 52 ⅓ innings this past season. Tseng's fastball typically sits in the mid-90s but has run as high as 100 mph in the past. His secondary pitches need work to carve out an MLB career as more than a middle reliever.

Arch Manning has best game yet as Texas football pushes past No. 18 Missouri for win

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Texas football survived another strong SEC test Saturday, overcoming No. 18 Missouri 34-20 in Columbia, Mo. by winning the second half for the second straight week.

This time the Longhorns scored the final 21 points of the game, powered by a pair of Arch Manning touchdown passes and a couple of key defensive plays in the final few minutes at Memorial Stadium. It was Texas' fourth win over a ranked opponent.

Manning was 28 of 38 for 297 yards, three touchdowns and no interceptions, Raleek Brown rushed for 85 yards and a touchdown, three different Longhorns caught Manning's TDs and No. 7 Texas heads into next week's second straight road trip with some momentum.

Texas Longhorns tight end Emaree Winston (85) jumps over Missouri Tigers safety Kensley Louidor-Faustin (7) as he carries the ball in the second quarter during the game against Missouri at Faurot Field at Memorial Stadium in Columbia on Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026. (Mikala Compton/Austin American-Statesman)

Texas Longhorns defensive back Graceson Littleton (29) and Texas Longhorns linebacker Justin Cryer (28) defend Missouri Tigers wide receiver Cayden Lee (19) during the game against Missouri at Faurot Field at Memorial Stadium in Columbia on Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026. (Mikala Compton/Austin American-Statesman)

Manning said his best football was yet to come after those back-to-back poor performances in October. He's kept his word through the last two games.

Texas' quarterback turned in an efficient, 200-passing-yard performance last week against Mississippi State. He delivered greater fireworks in a difficult environment Saturday. Manning marched into Memorial Stadium and recorded his highest passing yardage total since Week 1 alongside that trio of touchdowns, the last of which gave Texas a 27-20 fourth-quarter lead it wouldn't relinquish.

Manning is a supreme talent who suffers from turnover woes. He's cleaned up his play in recent weeks with zero turnovers in his last eight quarters. He both protected the ball and recorded multiple passes of 20 yards or longer, delivering perhaps his best performance in his 23rd career start.

Texas Longhorns quarterback Arch Manning (16) throws a pass during the game against Missouri at Faurot Field at Memorial Stadium in Columbia on Saturday, Nov. 7, 2026. (Mikala Compton/Austin American-Statesman)

Texas' résumé for inclusion in the College Football Playoff isn't perfect. But the Longhorns' pair of blemishes are matched by four impressive wins, a quartet matched by perhaps no team in the nation.

The Longhorns (7-2, 4-2 SEC) defeated then-No. 1 Ohio State in September. They now have road wins over current top-20 teams in Tennessee and No. 19 Missouri, as well as a home victory last month against No. 10 Ole Miss. No other team nationwide has four wins against teams in the top-25 of the CFP rankings nor multiple wins against top-10 teams.

Look, who are we to say that David Smith's crew didn't get everything right? However, the SEC still owes Texas and Missouri an explanation since the referees became a storyline during a game that featured ESPN's game and rules analysts openly disagreeing with multiple calls.

Missouri had two touchdowns and a big first-down play taken off the stat sheet by a video review, Texas wasn't awarded a first-half fumble even though it appeared the football came loose before the whistle and then there was that Manning play that looked like an incomplete pass but was actually ruled to be a fumble that UT recovered for a pivotal first down.

Once again, maybe the officials (and the replay booth) got all of those calls right. But it didn't look like it on the screen, and that sentiment is was shared across the country - at least one social media.

GOLDEN:No comment on the CFP? Why was Texas so tight-lipped?

MORE:Maraad Watson's 61-yard TD completes 18-month journey for new Texas star

If the reports at true and running back Hollywood Smothers is indeed out for the season, Texas would be wise to have fifth-year running back Brown take up the first, second and third slots on its depth chart. Brown rushed for 85 yards and a touchdown Saturday, and the Arizona State transfer's 23-yard touchdown catch was the decisive score. Brown also set up fellow running back Derrek Cooper's touchdown catch in the first quarter by rushing for three first downs on the drive.Brown earned just one offensive touch in the second quarter, but the Longhorns wised up after halftime. In the second half, he had 12 rushing attempts and four catches. Cooper and Michael Terry III are nice depth pieces. But Brown proved two weeks ago against Ole Miss that he deserves a workhorse role, and he reiterated that point against the Tigers.

Next Saturday in Baton Rouge, La. The 14th-ranked Tigers host No. 5 Alabama this afternoon. Texas hasn't played a football game in Baton Rouge since 1953.

Jai'Von Plowden breaks free to lift Detroit Edison to MHSAA district title

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Jai'Von Plowden refused to go down until he had the wooden mitten trophy in his hands.

In a back-and-forth Division 6 district final against Clinton, the Detroit Edison senior running back made sure he produced the final play Saturday, Nov. 7.

After Clinton had taken a lead with 6:48 left, Plowden broke free for an 81-yard touchdown, giving Edison (10-1) the lead in its 26-24 win over Clinton (9-2) in a showdown between two top-5 Division 6 teams in an MHSAA district final at Edison High School.

On a shotgun handoff up the middle, Plowden burst into the second level, cut hard toward the right sideline and then outran everyone.

"Really, I was just scared to lose," Plowden said. "It was just, don't go down. I balanced myself. Usually, I'm always getting caught. But, I haven't been caught since Week 1. I don't want to be caught anymore. I want to be known as 'him.'"

[ Sign up for our new Michigan Sidelines statewide high school sports newsletter ]

Clinton picked up two first downs on the ensuing drive, but Edison's defense finally found a stop. The Redwolves scored touchdowns on their first three drives of the second half, led by great running out of the power-T from Bronson Whelan, Braxton Randolph and Gradyn Whelan.

But on the final drive, Edison defensive end London Lester had a sack and tackle for loss, and Khalil Richmond also got home for a sack. On fourth-and-forever, Clinton's deep shot fell incomplete.

"We didn't call it different," Edison coach Chanterius Brock said. "Maybe we just had to catch our second wind a little bit. Against a good, disciplined offense like [Clinton], it comes down to execution, playing harder and just wanting it more. That's what came out. We were desperate."

Edison burned the final 1:50 — kept by hand by referees on the field because of scoreboard clock issues — to win its third district in the last four years and first on its home field.

"It was excitement, tears of joy," said Plowden, who took a moment on his own to gather his emotions before joining the postgame handshake line. "We've put so much work in. It feels like we are getting what we deserve, so it made me so happy to see my team happy and see their joy."

Edison built a two-touchdown lead before Clinton whittled it away. The Pioneers struck on three long touchdowns and another big chunk before a fourth, showcasing the explosive talent all around the offense.

Watch MHSAA football playoff games on NFHS

Plowden had the 82-yard touchdown run and another long run called back in the first quarter. Senior quarterback Myles Matlock connected with senior Andre Neal Jr. (Ferris State) for an 82-yard touchdown on the first play of the second half, and connected with Mekhi James-Davis (Ferris State) on a 73-yard touchdown. Neal set up his second touchdown catch, from 10 yards, by hauling in a 37-yard catch on a contested fade down the sidelines.

"We had to be in the moment and take advantage of the moment," Matlock said. "And we executed when we had to."

Edison will now play its first home regional game in school history, next Saturday in a rematch against Ecorse. DEPSA beat Ecorse 30-20 in Week 8 to win the Charter School Conference championship behind a big performance from Neal.

"It means a lot because a lot goes into this," Brock said. "We talk about investing time into it, not spending time. You invest the time because you respect the return. Now, we are getting a return on our investment. Our kids worked extremely hard for this."

U2’s The Edge Wrote an ‘Elf’ Sequel, But It ‘Petered Out and Never Happened,’ Says Andy Richter

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

According to Andy Richter, U2’s the Edge penned a script for a sequel to “Elf” — but fans will likely never get to see (or read) it.

On a recent episode of “Obsessed: The Podcast,” the actor explained that Shauna Robertson, who’d produced the original film, once received a message saying that the band member, also known as David Howell Evans, had written a script for “Elf 2” Related Stories 'The Mosquito Bowl' Review: Football Heroes Take to the Battlefield in Peter Berg's Emptily Macho WWII Epic Netflix Names Mattel Executive Roberto Stanichi as VP of Consumer Products and Experiences

“Somebody reached out to her and said, ‘The Edge from U2 has written a sequel to ‘Elf,’ and he wants to talk to you about it,’” Richter said. “It’s the weirdest story, but apparently it was fully written. Somewhere in the world there is an Edge-penned ‘Elf 2’ out there… I would love for that to make the light of day. It never happened. It’s one of those things where she’s like, ‘All right, sure, I’ll meet him,’ and then it just petered out and never happened.”

In addition to the Edge, “Old School” and “The Hangover Part II” writer Scot Armstrong penned a separate sequel script about “the evil South Pole elves kidnapping Santa or something like that,” said Richter. (He also noted that Armstrong had helped write “Elf,” but wasn’t credited for guild and procedural reasons.)

And yet despite how beloved the holiday classic remains 23 years later, Richter is steadfast in the belief that a sequel isn’t necessary at this point. “I’m on the side of ‘Let it stand,’” he said. ”Just let ‘Elf’ be ‘Elf.’ You don’t have to use every part of the animal.”

He continued: “Now that time has passed, the movie has become so special that I think that people, rightfully so, are loath to mess with it because we all can think of movies that have been tarnished by their sequels. And this is so special and so perfect and so beloved… Why risk it?”

“Elf,” directed by Jon Favreau, originally hit theaters in 2003, starring Will Ferrell, James Caan, Zooey Deschanel, Mary Steenburgen and others. The Christmas classic followed Buddy the elf (Ferrell), who travels from the North Pole to New York City to meet his biological father, Walter Hobbs (Caan).

Ritcher appeared briefly in the film as one of Hobbs’ colleagues and co-wrote the book “Elf Unwrapped: Behind the Scenes of a Modern Christmas Classic,” released Tuesday.

Warriors rookie Yaxel Lendeborg surprises mom with new house in amazing moment

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Golden State Warriors rookie Yaxel Lendeborg has always been vocal about the love for his mother, Yissel Raposo. Together, they shed tears of joy when his name was called at the No. 11 pick in the 2026 NBA Draft to the Warriors in June.

Lendeborg has been vocal about the sacrifices that his mother made for him and one of his goals was to pay her back. He debuted a new YouTube channel showcasing "behind-the-scenes, life outside of basketball and everything in between" where a video released on Friday, Nov. 6 had a surprise reveal of a recently purchased house for his mother.

"On behalf of everybody that came to show up, welcome home," Lendeborg said to Raposo, moving her from New Jersey to California.

Raposo was speechless as happy tears trickled down her face and she took the keys out of a box Lendeborg handed her and she opened the door as they took a tour of her new home.

Lendeborg said it took him 2 to 3 months to finalize the house. To keep his mom from finding out, he and his sister would often disguise phone conversations around visiting Hawaii; instead, they were talking about how to get their mom to her new house without suspicion.

"There is no gesture big enough to properly say thank you for all the love, sacrifice, and support my mom has poured into me but this moment was pretty special and one I'll never forget," Lendeborg said in his video description. "She deserves it all. For me, it's bigger than basketball. It's about making sure the people who believed in me get to enjoy the blessings too."

Lendeborg has always discussed the bond that he and his mother share and how she's directly responsible for his achievements in basketball and finding early success in the NBA.

"I got here because of her," Lendeborg said in June after getting drafted. "She pushed a dream, forced me to go out there and step into the world and become a man. I had no choice but to go out there and do the best I could. So I vowed every day to be a better man, be a better person and just do the best I can for her."

Sharing the moments of success with his mom means everything to Lendeborg because of the sacrifices that she made for him and his sisters, having to work multiple jobs to ensure they have what they need. Raposo also battled Stage 4 appendix cancer, another thing that fuels Lendeborg.

"She put her whole life on hold for my life and I'm super proud of her for fighting so hard and I'm glad we get to share this moment together," he said in a June interview after being drafted.

Lendeborg has been a bright spot for the Warriors on the court, who are on a six-game winning streak as they face the Orlando Magic in a 5:30 p.m. PT (8:30 p.m. ET) tip-off on Saturday, Nov. 7 at Chase Center in San Francisco, California.

The rookie averages 21.0 points, 7.1 rebounds, and 4.1 assists per game through his first 9 games of the 2026–27 NBA season.

Aquilani at risk of Sassuolo dismissal: ‘I have to be judged on results’

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

There are several reports that Sassuolo are ready to fire Alberto Aquilani after five consecutive defeats. ‘I have to be judged on results and I have to question myself,’ said the coach.

Aquilani was appointed over the summer to replace Fabio Grosso and had also been in negotiations with Torino before agreeing terms with the Neroverdi.

There were some positive results, most notably the 3-2 victory over Juventus, but his overall score this season is of four wins, two draws and seven losses between Serie A and the Coppa Italia.

The 3-0 defeat to Roma was the fifth loss on the bounce, scraping one point from the last seven rounds, and that was a 1-1 against Milan.

Sky Sport Italia and Sportitalia both report this evening that Sassuolo are giving serious consideration to a change of management during the break for international duty, with Roberto D’Aversa the favourite to take over.

“It was as if a light went off,” Aquilani told Sky Sport Italia about tonight’s performance against Roma.

“I didn’t expect to see so little from my team. We have no courage on the ball, it becomes impossible this way. After we went 1-0 down, the game just stopped. I expect more in so many ways, but I cannot hide, I am the one responsible.”

This is only Aquilani’s first season in Serie A as a coach after impressing in Serie B with Pisa and Catanzaro.

“We talk about mental fragility, but it’s hard to lift the players after five defeats in a row. This is football, we need to face reality.”

Aquilani was asked if he still feels that the Sassuolo directors have full faith in him.

“I haven’t spoken to them yet. I always felt their faith, but obviously I have to be judged on results, and I have to question myself. Many things have to change.”

Man United sink to new 40-year low after Nottingham Forest beat Man City

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Ben Knapton | 07 Nov 2026 22:41

Manchester United have sunk to a new 40-year low thanks to Nottingham Forest's shock victory over Manchester City on Saturday evening.

Ahead of the start of gameweek 10, the Red Devils were just about keeping their heads above water in the Premier League table, sitting outside the relegation zone on goal difference.

Michael Carrick's men led Crystal Palace by that metric, while sitting two points clear of rock-bottom Sunderland and one better off than Forest after nine rounds.

The Tricky Trees were unsurprisingly tipped to suffer defeat to Enzo Maresca's men at the City Ground, but in an unexpected turn of events, Forest claimed a 3-1 triumph on home soil.

Jair Cunha and Liam Delap propelled the Garibaldi into a 2-0 lead before Rayan Cherki pulled one back, but the Frenchman's response was futile as Delap struck his second of the game in the second half.

Forest's win propelled Oliver Glasner's men out of the Premier League drop zone, while simultaneously forcing Man United into the bottom three, two points adrift of 17th-placed Coventry City.

The Red Devils therefore find themselves in the bottom three after nine rounds for the first time since November 1986, when they were in 21st place after 15 games of the 1986-87 season.

November 1986 was the same month that Ron Atkinson was dismissed and Sir Alex Ferguson took over as head coach, and the latter ultimately led Man Utd to an 11th-placed finish in the First Division.

The 20-time English champions will rise to 16th place if they beat Aston Villa by any scoreline on Sunday, though, while victory by three goals or more will also lift them above Fulham into 15th spot.

Nevertheless, Carrick remains a man under major pressure after Man United's new 40-year worst, in spite of Tuesday's extraordinary 3-2 Champions League comeback triumph over Roma.

Man United always intended to get to the November international break before making any firm managerial decisions, so to say Carrick bought himself more time with Tuesday's fightback may be inaccurate.

However, another pitiful Premier League performance and result on Sunday could force the hierarchy to take action, especially with the current bookies' favourites available for hire immediately.

The odds on Eddie Howe replacing Carrick are now as low as 8/13 on some operators, while Julian Nagelsmann is the second-favourite to take charge at 11/4.

Antonio Conte and Unai Emery are not far behind, the latter's links with Old Trafford representing a fascinating sub-plot ahead of tomorrow's fixture.

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

There was an unfortunate incident after Cagliari’s 1-0 victory over Frosinone, as a supporter fell from the stands trying to salute the players, who rushed to his aid.

An ambulance is always ready on the sidelines in top flight matches, and was called in take the supporter to the local hospital.

It’s reported the fan has been diagnosed with a fractured clavicle, but there are no more serious complications.

If all goes well, he should be released on Sunday to continue his recovery at home.

The Unipol Domus where the Sardinians play their home matches is a temporary structure, and was put in place while plans to reconstruct the Stadio Sant’Elia were worked through.

That arena now lies in ruins and is overgrown with vegetation just yards away from the current stadium.

Three Billy Edwards TD passes lift UNC to 38-21 win over UConn in Belichick’s return to New England

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Billy Edwards threw for three touchdowns and North Carolina scored 21 points off three turnovers as the Tar Heels defeated UConn 38-21 to snap a two-game losing streak in coach Bill Belichick's return to New England.

It was Belichick's first game back coaching in New England since his tenure with the NFL's Patriots came to an end.

"It's good to be back in New England," Belichick said. "It was an interesting game. I think UConn is a really challenging team, they put a lot of pressure on us.

"The team rebounded pretty well after a couple of tough losses."

Timir Hickman-Collins returned an interception 62 yards for a touchdown and had a fumble recovery to set up a 6-yard touchdown pass from Edwards to Jordan Shipp. Edwards had a fourth-down completion and put the game away with a 3-yard scoring pass to Jelani Thurman with 3:38 remaining after Jalon Thompson's interception gave the ball back to the Tar Heels.

"We've been really harping on (getting) turnovers, every day in practice we have been doing that," Hickman-Collins said. "Today, me and our DB (Jalon Thompson), a lot of us had turnovers."

Kalieb Osborne had completions of 23 and 28 yards and a 25-yard run to set up Cyncir Bowers' 13-yard touchdown run to pull UConn within seven points with 10:01 remaining in the third quarter. Osborne threw for 245 yards, but completed just 17 of 35 passes against a North Carolina defense that was allowing teams to complete 65% of their passes.

"I thought Carolina put us in some tough situations and won the football game," UConn coach Jason Candle said. "I didn't feel like we were at our best. We did some things that set them up in short fields. The turnovers were a big deal today."

Benjamin Hall had a 39-yard touchdown run. He finished with 81 yards on nine carries. Edwards connected with Jelani Thurman on a 3-yard touchdown with 24 seconds remaining in the first half. Thurman now has eight touchdown catches to tie the North Carolina single-season record for TDs by a tight end.

Osborne threw touchdown passes of 18 yards to Ryder Treadway and 24 yards to Cam Abshire as the Huskies trailed 28-14 at halftime.

Peyton Seelmann led North Carolina (5-4) with nine tackles and two sacks. Hayden Pegg had 12 tackles and an interception for UConn (4-5).

Belichick coached the Patriots to six Super Bowl titles. He played at Wesleyan University from 1971-74, which is about 20 miles away from Rentschler Field. Belichick nearly guided North Carolina to a win over No. 1 Miami last week.

Bob Diaco, North Carolina's defensive line coach, coached UConn from 2014-16. UConn won six games in Diaco's second season before losing to Marshall in the St. Petersburg Bowl. He was fired after a 3-9 record in 2016. Diaco went 11-26 in his three seasons with the Huskies.

North Carolina hosts Louisville on Nov. 14.

UConn is home against James Madison on Nov. 14.

Ultimate Fan: It’s a good time to open up the offense, keep the defensive pressure coming

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Ultimate Fans, life could not get better in Broncos Country right now. Denver is coming off an incredible victory over KC, currently holding court at the top of the AFC West, before a short trip out East to play the Panthers in spectacular weather just in front of a much-needed bye week.

AND on top of that, we have a fabulous UFG from another younger fan and newbie to the series who knocked it out the park just like his predecessors.

While Ky Broncos Fan wasn't even alive during the John Elway Era, his momma raised him right and turned him on to the GOAT and early Broncos' Lombardis. Watching the Tebow-to-DT thriller turned him into a fan for life and his Broncos' Mount Rushmore is testament to his appreciation for Denver's incredible history. So that gives you a hint of the treat you are in for!

I'm going to be at this game, and my track record is pretty good, so let's assume it's going to continue!

MHR – First, we have to talk about that KC game. What in the world happened? How excited/nervous were you for those final 15 seconds?Ky Broncos Fan: What happened was some Mile High Magic! I was standing in front of the TV pacing like a coach, and when that obvious false start was called (no matter what Brittany Mahomes thinks), I knew fate was on our side. The defining play of that sequence in my mind was JL Skinner stuffing Walker at the line of scrimmage after being forced into the game due to injuries; that kind of grit and determination is emblematic of the whole team. Last Sunday was my birthday, and the KC game was the perfect gift!

MHR – We used to do a post-game story at Mile High Report asking, "what did you learn about the Broncos this week?" It's a great question, so have YOU learned about the Broncos the last few weeks?Ky Broncos Fan: I've learned that the Broncos are still elite in late game situations. Over and over against some of the best teams in the league (besides the drubbing of the Cardinals), they've proved they still know how to win with their backs against the wall. The Arizona win was a nice reminder that fans can expect the Broncos to beat the teams they're supposed to beat instead of every week feeling like a coin flip.

MHR – The Broncos sit atop the AFC West at 6-2, but they rank 30th in total offense. Bo Nix has looked much better in the wins over Arizona and Kansas City. Has he turned a corner, or is this offense still living on late comebacks? As I've asked many before, is a constant comeback-style game, Ky Broncos Fan: The offense is improving, but the late comebacks are still hiding deeper issues. Establishing the run early will go a long way for more offensive consistency, and luckily for Broncos fans, Jonah Coleman seems more than ready to take that on. As for Bo, we all fell in love with his fire and gunslinging mentality last season when it mattered most. As he continues to regain confidence following the ankle injury, Denver needs him to trust his eyes and take the shots when they're there. His aversion to negative plays is one of his best traits, but Denver is at its best when he's balancing that with more risk-taking.

MHR – Carolina has been without starting corners Jaycee Horn and Mike Jackson, and Akayleb Evans is also on injured reserve. How aggressive should Sean Payton/Davis Webb be with telling Bo Nix to throw downfield on Sunday? Courtland Sutton and Jaylen Waddle have been key to moving the chains but not super explosive…any chance we get a big day from either/both?Ky Broncos Fan: This game is the perfect opportunity to unleash the passing game. Utilize the running backs to get ahead of the chains, and then open up the playbook and test their secondary. Rushing success will open up some play-action opportunities, and overall, I think pushing the ball downfield in this game is necessary to gaining confidence and setting the passing game up for more success later in the season. Waddle's speed and elusiveness are nightmares for opposing defenders, and I think he's overdue for turning a quick slant up field for a long touchdown. I also think we're overdue for a Sutton long bomb TD. When Bo puts it in the right place, Sutton turns 50/50 balls into 80/20 balls, and I want to see him get some chances to convert big plays and gain confidence this week.

MHR – On the other hand, the Panthers' run defense is atrocious (ranked 30th)…is this a game for Jonah Coleman to really break out? After that 51-yard catch-and-run + 5-yard TD last week…maybe he's poised for a big second half finish to the season? How would you suggest using him and JK Dobbins this week?Ky Broncos Fan: I'm starting Jonah Coleman in fantasy again this week. I spent a decent portion of my free agency budget to get him, and the Panthers' defense is the perfect chance for him to show the front office we don't need to spend draft capital on Jonathan Taylor. Dobbins can still be a very effective runner when healthy, and the priority has to be keeping Dobbins healthy for a playoff run. I'd make Coleman the true bellcow and spell him with Dobbins occasionally.

MHR – Nix dismissed the "trap game" talk. But it's a 1 p.m EST kickoff (11 a.m. Mile High time) right after an emotional comeback. Are you concerned the offense comes out flat? How does Nix avoid the offense getting in its own way?Ky Broncos Fan: I'm concerned. As someone who lives in eastern time, the Broncos always seem to start flat when they come to my time zone. Nix can avoid the offense getting in its own way with one word: handoff. Let Coleman and Dobbins do their jobs, and use their success to test the secondary in favorable situations. There are times when the playcalling should favor some early down aggressiveness to put Carolina on their heels too, but those playcalls have to be high-completion percentage opportunities that Payton trusts the offense to execute.

MHR – The Broncos defense has been mostly outstanding, coming up big when it needs to, even if it gives up some plays. How should Vance Joseph plan for Bryce Young, who has been having a decent season getting the ball in the hands of reigning Offensive Rookie of the Year Tetairoa McMillan and Jalen Coker, an equally dangerous receiver?Ky Broncos Fan: The secondary can trust the front line to get home. Young is easily overwhelmed by pressure and possibly dealing with an undisclosed knee injury that is altering his mobility. Joseph doesn't need to blitz more; just changing up coverage looks enough to keep Young guessing will buy the front line the time it needs to make game-changing plays. McMillan and Coker are great young receivers, but Denver's secondary has stymied greater, more experienced players already this season.

MHR – How should Vance Joseph take advantage of a depleted offensive line standing in front of Bryce Young…does the QB even have a chance with Zach Allen, Nik Bonitto, Jonathan Cooper, Malcolm Roach coming at him? With 23 sacks as a team this season, how worried should Young be on Sunday?Ky Broncos Fan: Young should be terrified. Carolina's path to success is a quick short passing game to get the ball out and mitigate the pass rush, but as long as the secondary and linebackers stay disciplined, they should face 3rd and longs routinely that let Allen, Bonitto and the others feast. Young has issues with the ball getting batted down at the line of scrimmage too, and the defense should be able to exploit him and create some chaos. They've made better quarterbacks than Young look worse this year, and I'm excited to see what they can pull off tomorrow.

MHR – Alex Singleton was named AFC Defensive Player of the Month. He's not always the best in coverage, but he does fly to the ball. What kind of impact do you think he has on the defense?Ky Broncos Fan: He's the emotional heart of the defense. His passion and elite tackling fuel everyone else's fires, and while he's not the most agile speedster of a linebacker we've seen, he mostly keeps himself out of bad situations by reading plays well. Carolina should try to isolate him in coverage, but that's easier said than done.

MHR – Denver ranks 25th against the run, allowing 127.9 yards per game. Carolina is coming off a 10-day "mini-bye." Is the run defense a weakness the Panthers can exploit?Ky Broncos Fan: It unfortunately is. Carolina's running backs aren't world-beaters, but a lack of disciplined tackling will allow their run game to take off quickly. I'm no defensive guru, but I'm worried about playoff matchups with better running backs come January if Denver can't clean up their run defense by then.

MHR – We are halfway through the season and just before a bye. Who are your midseason MVPs – offense, defense, ST?Ky Broncos Fan: Offense: Garret Bolles. PFF has him as 3rd best tackle in football this season, and he hasn't given up a sack yet. His growth over his career is amazing, and Denver's lucky to have him. I hope he retires here. Defense: Alex Singleton. Leading the league in tackles is amazing, but time and time again, we see him make big plays when it matters most. ST: Will Lutz. He's a fantastic kicker who seems more accurate in bigger moments. Lutz is exactly the kind of kicker you want on your roster for a postseason run.

Stats for each Broncos RB? Coleman: 20 carries, 87 yards, 2 TD. Dobbins: 6 carries, 15 yards.

Number of yards receiving/TDs for each WR/TE? Sutton: 5 catches, 68 yards, 1 TD. Waddle: 6 catches, 82 yards, 1 TD. Mims: 2 catches, 46 yards. Franklin: 1 catch, 8 yards. Engram: 2 catches, 12 yards. Trautman: 1 catch, 18 yards.

Number of times Nix barely misses getting sacked? 4

Broncos player with the most tackles? Singleton

Who gets the first sack of the game for the Broncos? Allen

Who gets any sacks? Allen, Bonitto, Cooper, Singleton

How many INTs/FF/FR for the Panthers' D? 0 INTs, 1 FF, 1 FR.

How many INTs/FF/FR for the Broncos' D? 1 INT, 2 FF, 1 FR.

How many completely wrong DPI penalties called Sunday? 2 on Moss, 0 on others.

Favorite John Elway win outside of the Super Bowls? The Drive, I can't rewatch it without getting chills.

Favorite Broncos win ever? Super Bowl 50. This one's for Pat!

Favorite Peyton Manning win? His 7-TD decimation of the Ravens to open 2013.

Toughest Peyton Manning loss? The Super Bowl 48 beat down. I couldn't dream up a worse ending for the best offense in the history of the league. That it gave a young QB named Russell Wilson a Super Bowl ring and contributed to Denver selling the farm for him years later is a cruel twist of fate.

Team you love to beat the most in the AFC West? The Chiefs. When the Raiders are relevant again it will be them.

Coach you laugh at the most in the NFL? Brian Schottenheimer. It's Jerry's world, Brian's just living in it.

Coach you most want to "bench?" Zac Taylor. Joe Burrow and that roster with a better head coach would turn some heads. Burrow has helped him keep his job, but Taylor is holding the team back.

Team you hate to lose to the most in the rest of the AFC? The Patriots.

NFC team you could cheer for in the Super Bowl? The Giants. John Harbaugh is a first class coach and human being. My Baltimore-native wife wishes they hadn't run him off to New York!

NFC team you want to face more than any other in the Super Bowl? The 49ers. A rematch with them and beating a Shanahan for Denver's 4th championship would be poetic.

Favorite game looking forward to this season? The Bills on Christmas Day.

Easiest game left on the schedule? Week 18 against the Chargers.

Toughest game left on the schedule? Week 17 at New England, but thank goodness the Bills are in Denver.

Favorite Broncos player on the current roster? Pat Surtain II.

Favorite Broncos player of all time not named John Elway? Demaryius Thomas.

Favorite new guy/rookie on the team? Jonah Coleman.

Food/Snack you have to eat on game day? Popcorn.

Favorite game analyst/commentator? Mike Tirico is always excellent.

If YOU were choosing your Broncos' Mt. Rushmore, who would be included? Floyd Little, John Elway, Peyton Manning, Randy Gradishar.

How did you become a Broncos fan? I didn't grow up particularly close to any NFL teams, but my mom had liked the Broncos since the 90s due to Elway. The first season I remember paying close attention to was the Tebow year (I was born in 2000). I vividly remember watching the entire Broncos-Steelers playoff game, and as I watched Demaryius Thomas fly down the sideline as Mile High erupted, I fell in love with the Broncos and have watched every game religiously ever since.

Former Serie A player takes up MMA

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

At 34, Diego Demme found a new field of expression after hanging up his boots last summer. The former Naples midfielder, crowned Italian champion with the club in 2023, started practicing MMA in Berlin. Having played for RB Leipzig and Hertha Berlin, the former German international now trains four to five times a week at the Choke Athletics club. A radical change of discipline for someone who has played for a long time at the highest level of European football.

However, the beginnings were not easy. Demme admitted to having suffered from the holds of his partners and the physical intensity of this new practice. But his progress is already visible: he recently completed a course without being forced to drop out, a first for which he was delighted. His coach, Saman Darvish, highlights his ability to learn, his endurance and his determination. If the German now plans to join intermediate to advanced level grappling sessions, he does not plan to become a professional fighter. Above all, he sees this experience as a new personal challenge and a way to learn to better defend himself.

Roquan Smith hit with 2 NFL fines for his play in Ravens vs. Bills

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Ravens linebacker Roquan Smith was hit with two fines for his play in Week 8's loss to the Buffalo Bills. In total, Smith will pony up almost $36,000 for two penalties last Sunday.

One fine was connected to unnecessary roughness for this play, where Smith not only grabbed Bills quarterback Josh Allen's facemask, but twisted it almost 180 degrees.

The second fine stemmed from unnecessary roughness penalty for a hit on a defenseless player not even a full minute into the game.

Week 8's fines make three total for Smith during the 2026 season. He was also fined $11,941 for unnecessary roughness stemming from a face mask in Week 2. That's why Week 8's fines were almost $18,000 each — because they weren't the first this season.

No other Ravens players were fined in Week 8. No Bills players were fined for that contest.

The NFL donates half the fines it collects to the NFLPA's efforts to support retired players, and the other half to the NFL Foundation and organizations supporting youth football programs, community health and wellness, and athlete safety.

Rodrigo Mora: ‘Football is crazy sometimes, Cristiano Ronaldo my idol’

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Rodrigo Mora concedes ‘football is crazy’ after scoring his third consecutive Serie A goal for Roma and looks forward to a senior Portugal debut even without ‘idol’ Cristiano Ronaldo.

The 19-year-old talent was a big investment this summer from FC Porto, secured for €25m for effectively only half his contract.

It took a while for him to make his mark in the Giallorossi jersey, but this evening he scored in the 3-0 win over Sassuolo, his third consecutive Serie A match on target.

“It was a very good pass from Malen, I tried my luck, and I was happy to score another important goal, so Roma keep pushing with another win,” Mora told Sky Sport Italia.

He now has four goals in nine Serie A games, plus two assists in four Champions League appearances under Gian Piero Gasperini.

“When I arrived here, I thought we were strong. Every time I training I thought we were a very good team, we are proving that in the match and need to keep this momentum going.”

Mora seems to have really hit his stride in the last month, as after struggling to even get into the starting XI, he has flourished in recent weeks.

“Football is crazy, because one day you don’t play, the next you play. I was ready, now it’s my third goal in a row, so it’s important to always be ready when the moment comes.”

Rodrigo Mora has also received a call-up for the senior Portugal squad, which will be a very different team now that Cristiano Ronaldo has retired from international duty.

“It’s a very proud moment for me and my family, it is always a pleasure to play for Portugal. Now Cristiano is not there, but is an idol for everyone in Portugal, including me. I learned a lot when I came to the national team, I am very happy to be there.”

Tottenham to lodge 'formal complaint' over Jayden Bogle VAR decision after Mathys Tel injury update

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Ben Knapton | 07 Nov 2026 22:23

Tottenham Hotspur reportedly intend to complain to Pro Ref after Leeds United's Jayden Bogle controversially escaped a red card in Saturday's Premier League clash.

Spurs' top-flight revival was nipped in the bud in a 1-0 loss to Leeds at Elland Road, where Noah Okafor struck the decisive goal for Daniel Farke's side in the second half.

However, Tottenham were aggrieved that the game finished 11 versus 11, as Leeds right-back Bogle caught Mathys Tel on the back of his ankle with a dreadful studs-up challenge in the first half.

Referee John Brooks did not deem the incident punishment-worthy at the time, though, while VAR official Tony Harrington elected not to send the on-field official to the monitor either, determining that Bogle had not committed serious foul play.

The PL Match Centre on X released a transcript of the audio conversation between Harrington and Brooks, the former declaring that Bogle's tackle amounted to reckless rather than dangerous.

"I'm confirming the on-field decision of no red card. There’s a lack of speed going into the challenge and he’s stepping into the tackle. Confirming a reckless challenge," Harrington said.

However, BBC Sport states that Tottenham will lodge a formal complaint with the referees' body, as the Lilywhites unsurprisingly believe that Bogle should have been made to take the walk of shame.

Spurs will seek an explanation from Pro Ref as to why Bogle was allowed to stay on the pitch, although De Zerbi refused to criticise the officials and technology in his post-game interview.

"We have full confidence in the referee and VAR. I cannot say nothing more," De Zerbi told BBC Match of the Day, as Spurs suffered their fourth loss of the Premier League season.

The North London giants nevertheless remain in the top half of the Premier League table, although they will drop back down into the bottom 10 if either Ipswich Town or Aston Villa collect a point on Sunday.

Tel was unable to carry on after feeling the full force of Bogle's studs, and the Frenchman had to be replaced by Dominic Solanke with 43 minutes gone at Elland Road.

De Zerbi had no positive news to share on Tel's condition at full time, suggesting that the Frenchman is set for a spell on the sidelines.

"Bad injury and now we have to make the medical check, and the next day we will know what is the problem," De Zerbi told reporters in his post-game press conference.

Tel was called up to the France Under-21 squad for November's fixtures against Italy and England, but the former Bayern Munich man will now almost certainly have to pull out of the selection.

Tel has scored one goal in 10 games so far this season for Tottenham, whose first game back after the international break comes at home to Ipswich Town on November 21.

The midfielder, 23, latched on to a loose ball on the edge of the Peterborough area and fired home, with the aid of a slight deflection, before racing off towards the visiting fans and celebrating with a knee slide.

Middlesbrough, QPR & West Brom have all dodged Bamba Dieng transfer bullet

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Middlesbrough, Queens Park Rangers, and West Bromwich Albion were all linked with Senegal international Bamba Dieng during the summer transfer window, but none ultimately pursued a deal.

The 26-year-old instead joined Serbian giants Red Star Belgrade on a free transfer, where he has enjoyed some encouraging moments in front of goal after not joining Middlesbrough, QPR, or West Brom.

However, there is a growing argument that the Championship trio might have been wise to steer clear, particularly when looking beyond Bamba Dieng's domestic scoring record and assessing how he has performed on the European stage.

Dieng has certainly found the net a reasonable number of times since arriving in Serbia, but there is a significant difference between scoring in the Serbian SuperLiga and delivering consistently in the Championship.

More importantly, his performances in the UEFA Conference League have hardly suggested he would have been a transformative signing for the likes of Middlesbrough, West Brom, or QPR this season. Boro were even in talks with Dieng at one stage of the transfer window.

All three clubs should arguably be targeting promotion and, given their size and stature, would surely fancy their chances of competing for glory in a European competition of that nature if they were playing at that level.

FLW have ranked all 24 Championship clubs on how far they have fallen from their historical glory days, including Blackburn and West Ham.

A high-wage arrival such as Dieng should be demonstrating genuine upside against continental opposition, rather than simply padding his statistics and goal involvements in domestic fixtures for a club the size and stature of Red Star Belgrade in Serbia's top flight.

Red Star have faced Lugano, Copenhagen, and Inter Club d'Escaldes, with that trio being opponents that Championship sides would reasonably expect to beat or at least give a very good account of themselves against.

Dieng has failed to register a goal or assist in any of those three matches. Perhaps all three English clubs had the right idea by ultimately looking elsewhere or sticking with what they had in the end. The fact he limped off the pitch assisted by medial staff in their most recent fixture underlines this, too.

Dieng began his professional career in Senegal with Diambars before earning a move to Marseille in 2021, where he scored eight goals in 40 appearances. He subsequently joined Lorient permanently in January 2023, spending time on loan at Angers before enjoying his most productive campaign yet in 2025/26.

The 24-cap Senegal international may well have made the move to the Championship over the recent summer but he has also experienced missing out on a Premier League move, having agreed to join Leeds United from Marseille in September 2022 before a dramatic deadline-day change of plans saw Nice attempt to hijack the deal (BBC Sport).

Are you happy with your club's striking options with January just around the corner? Comment below!

Since joining Red Star on a free transfer in August, he has scored five goals in seven Serbian league appearances. However, he has no goals or assists in three Conference League outings, totalling just over 200 minutes, against opposition of the stature and quality of Lugano, Copenhagen, and Inter Club d'Escaldes.

Dieng would not have been somebody who could comfortably arrive as an expensive substitute at any of these three clubs, meaning his signing would have carried significant consequences for their existing attacking options.

At Middlesbrough, he could have been brought in instead of Will Lankshear, which would have been a considerable mistake given the former Tottenham Hotspur youngster's extraordinary goalscoring form in the Championship.

Type the top scorer next to each season. Surnames count. Two seasons had joint winners, so they need two names.

Top league scorers in the regular season, per season since the second tier became the Championship in 2004–05.

QPR might have been tempted to install him ahead of Richard Kone, potentially stunting the development of one of their most exciting young attackers. Meanwhile, West Brom already have Jimmy-Jay Morgan performing well, with Aune Heggebo also contributing positively.

All three clubs have younger forwards worth developing rather than blocking with an expensive, high-wage alternative. Dieng might yet prove to be a success in Serbia, but the Championship trio arguably made the right decisions for their respective squads.

Reba McEntire Reveals Dolly Parton’s Touching On-Set Gesture: ‘She Didn’t Have To Do That’

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Reba McEntire was determined to make Dolly Parton feel welcome when the country legend joined her sitcom for a guest appearance—but it was Parton who ended up making everyone on-set feel special.

While speaking at NAR NXT in New Orleans alongside her manager and former real estate agent, Justin McIntosh, McEntire recalled the thoughtful gesture Parton made for every crew member behind the scenes of her hit sitcom “Reba”—a small act of kindness that left a lasting impression.

Instead of simply showing up and delivering her lines, Parton made a point of personally greeting the people working behind the scenes, bringing along Valentine’s Day candy and a message for every crew member.

For McEntire, the gesture remains a vivid example of the warmth that made her fellow singer so beloved.

“I was so nervous with Dolly being on the set. I wanted everyone to be nice. What I didn’t expect was how nice she was going to be on the set," she said.

McEntire recalled that Parton took the time to make each person feel appreciated, turning a guest appearance into a chance to connect with the crew.

“She had Valentine’s Day candies, and she went to every crew member individually and said how happy she was to be there,” she said. “She didn’t have to do that.”

The memory has stayed with McEntire, along with a conversation the two shared while sitting together on the iconic show's couch.

“I asked her if she would ever retire,” McEntire recalled. “And she said, ‘What would I do that I could have so much fun doing?’”

It was a simple answer, but one that reflected the enthusiasm McEntire remembers so fondly.

“I’ve never met a person or known a person that’s so universally loved like Dolly Parton,” she said. “I’ve never heard anything bad about her. She was that special of a person.”

That sense of gratitude for a career filled with opportunities to connect with people is something McEntire shares.

“I’m just really grateful and thankful for doing what I do at the level I do,” she said.

For McEntire, keeping her work exciting means giving herself room to try new things—and inviting her audience along for the experience.

“When I change things up, my fans stay interested with me,” she explained. “If it looks like I’m having fun, then they’re having fun.”

The actress revealed that being able to enjoy her work is a gift.

“If you are doing what you love, then that’s how blessed you are,” she said. “That’s one of the greatest gifts God can give, to give you a job you love. I’m very grateful for my job.”

The connection she has built with her audience also rests on the principle that people can recognize sincerity principle—something she believes applies well beyond entertainment.

“I try to sing the songs from my heart so when you listen to it, it touches your heart,” she said.

That emphasis on warmth and connection carries into the way McEntire searches for a home, too.

Long before her career brought her to stages and television sets, she grew up on an 8,000-acre ranch in Oklahoma.

Despite the expanse of land outside, her family of six shared a small house with three bedrooms and one bathroom.

Asked what she first thinks of when she remembers the ranch, McEntire named the people and feelings that filled it.

“Love, family, unity, closeness,” she said. “One itty-bitty bathroom, three bedrooms. We were a close family.”

Gathering around the dinner table was part of life, and the atmosphere of the house remains easy for her to recall.

“We all sat at the dinner table together,” she said. “It was earthy. I can still smell how the house smelled and the energy.”

The land remains a family home. McEntire says it stayed in the family after her parents died, and her sister and her sister’s family now live on the property.

Those early memories offer a glimpse of what McEntire still values when she walks into a house: a feeling of comfort and a connection that goes beyond its appearance.

“A house has energy, and when you come in and when you bond with that energy, it makes it a home,” she said. “So I always look for good, positive energy when I come in.”

The country icon reflected on the moment she experienced that pull while looking for property in Tennessee.

A friend who was vacationing in Florida with a real estate agent spotted a listing the agent was viewing and urged that it be shown to McEntire.

“My friend said, ‘You gotta show Reba that place,’” she recalled.

The approach to the property and a creek at the back helped capture her attention. Soon, she was returning for visits and spending time with the owners.

“The love I had for that house, it took my breath away,” she said. “I went a couple times, and I watched football games with the owners.”

When it comes to her priorities in a home, being comfortable and enjoying the surroundings rank high.

“I love a home with a view,” she said. “When I lived in Oklahoma, you could see sunrise and sunset. That’s important to me.”

McEntire said that focus on people and their needs also helped draw her to her partnership with Realtor.com®.

“What y'all do is very important, you care so much, that's why I like Realtor.com. I love your organization because you have heart...You really do dive into the family and what their needs are. You love people and you care about people, that's why I wanted to be with you guys," she said.

Whether she is discussing finding a home or maintaining the trust of an audience, McEntire returns to the importance of treating people well—and continuing to do so over time.

“You have to stay consistently honest, because when you look someone in the eye, they know how you treated them last time,” she said. “You have to stay consistent with it.”

Kelsi Karruli is a senior celebrity reporter who joined Realtor.com in 2025. She writes about trends encompassing real estate and celebrity properties. She previously wrote for Daily Mail, True Urban Culture, and JMedia Corporation, where she specialized in covering celebrities, relationships, psychology, and gender issues. She has a bachelor's degree in journalism from Pace University, where she received the James Rose Award for excellence in journalism. When she isn’t updating readers on A-listers' moves, she is spending time with her cat, Mary, and cheering on the Albanian soccer team.

City slump again as Arsenal extend Premier League lead

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manchester City suffered a third consecutive Premier League defeat at the hands of Nottingham Forest on Saturday, allowing Arsenal to stretch their lead at the top of the table to six points.

Enzo Maresca's men desperately needed a victory at the City Ground to keep pace with the in-form champions, who have taken 27 points from a possible 30, but lost 3-1.

City had a mountain to climb after finding themselves 2-0 down in the 18th minute following goals from defender Jair Cunha and forward Liam Delap.

The visitors were much improved after the break and Rayan Cherki cut the deficit with an emphatic strike into the roof of the net.

But Delap restored Forest's two-goal advantage in the 73rd minute, rifling home with his left foot past the outstretched hand of Gianluigi Donnarumma to earn his side breathing room.

Forest manager Oliver Glasner sprinted down the touchline to celebrate with his team, who had not managed to score a single Premier League goal at home before Saturday.

City, who won their first seven league games under new manager Maresca, know that even if they close the gap to Arsenal, it might ultimately all be in vain.

The Etihad club are still waiting to discover their fate after being found guilty of more than 100 breaches of Premier League financial rules -- with a huge points deduction one potential punishment.

Earlier, Arsenal made hard work of beating struggling Hull to underline their title credentials on the eve of the international break.

Captain Martin Odegaard finally broke the deadlock for the champions and title favourites in the 72nd minute.

Arsenal, who bossed possession at the Emirates, had a claim for a penalty in the closing minutes of the first half after Kai Havertz had his shirt pulled by Nobel Mendy.

But VAR ruled that the German forward was in an offside position in the build-up.

Frustration mounted as the second half unfolded but Odegaard came to Arsenal's rescue, scoring his first goal since early September with a poacher's finish.

Noni Madueke cut in from the right before unleashing a left-footed shot that Hull goalkeeper Konstantinos Tzolakis spilled and Odegaard fired home from close range.

Arsenal manager Arteta praised his players but wants them to be more clinical after they recorded a third 1-0 win in four league games.

"We can still be much better, and we will improve in many phases, and we will get certain relationships to a different level that will be even more efficient," he said.

"That's the next step, to have bigger margins and when we have them, the opportunities, put them away to make sure that we don't have to hang in there the last few minutes."

In the early kick-off, Leeds struck in the 79th minute through substitute Noah Okafor to end Tottenham's three-match winning run.

The major talking point in the first half was a challenge by Leeds defender Jayden Bogle on Spurs winger Mathys Tel, which was reviewed for a potential red card by VAR officials before eventually being cleared.

Tel had to leave the field shortly before half-time.

Spurs boss Roberto De Zerbi, disappointed by his team's second-half showing, refused to be drawn on the incident, which was not reviewed on the pitchside monitor by referee John Brooks.

"In the first situation of Mathys Tel I didn't see, I can't say nothing," he told TNT Sports after the 1-0 defeat.

He added: "I have big confidence in the referee. Nothing changes if he goes to watch the clip or not. I think there was the VAR and if they didn't take the right decision, I don't know, but we have confidence."

Elsewhere, Jacob Murphy put Newcastle on course for a much-needed win with a spectacular strike in the 80th minute at Craven Cottage but substitute Rodrigo Muniz levelled just three minutes later to earn a 1-1 draw.

Forest's win against City means Michael Carrick's Manchester United will kick off against Aston Villa on Sunday in the relegation zone.
