D’Amico: ‘Roma learning experience at Manchester United, need new resources’

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Roma director Tony D’Amico admits the defeat to Manchester United was ‘a learning experience’ for them, confirming the need to sign ‘a forward who can do damage on the counter-attack’ when leading.

The match with Sassuolo kicks off at the Stadio Olimpico in Rome at 19.45 GMT (20.45 CET).

You can follow all the build-up and action as it happens from today’s four Serie A games on the Liveblog.

The Giallorossi are still unbeaten this season in Serie A, but have twice thrown away massive leads in the Champions League for defeats to Real Madrid and now Manchester United.

That Old Trafford collapse from 2-0 up to lose 3-2 confirmed this is a recurring problem, so this evening the pressure is on to get a result against struggling Sassuolo.

“It is a problem we realised towards the end of the transfer session, that we lack a wide forward who has pace and can do damage in those situations on the counter-attack,” D’Amico told Sky Sport Italia.

“We will try to correct that when the January window comes around, so will try to give the coach this extra characteristic that we don’t currently have.”

It has been said that Roma lack experience in the Champions League and that cost them when their heads dropped in those situations.

“In an atmosphere like Old Trafford, the fans make a big difference and they helped turn the tide of that game,” insisted D’Amico.

“What I saw in both the games against Manchester United and Real Madrid was a great Roma side that imposes its style of play on the strongest opponents. There are going to be moments of difficulty in fixtures like this.

“I don’t think enough has been said, partly because we don’t like to court controversy, but the penalty for Manchester United was more than generous. So we did have a moment of difficulty, but that penalty sparked the comeback.

“As I said after the Real Madrid defeat, these are learning experiences for a club that has not been in the Champions League for seven years, it also teaches us what resources we still need to give to the coach in order to continue this progress.”

Gianluca Mancini is the latest absentee with a flexor issue that also ruled him out of the Italy squad for the Nations League games with France and Belgium.

He joins Devyne Rensch, Pierluigi Gollini, Mile Svilar, Paulo Dybala and Lorenzo Pellegrini on the treatment table, but the good news is the return of Manu Koné for the first time since his muscular injury against Real Madrid on October 14, even though he wasn’t called up for France.

“We speak with all the international coaches, Koné returned to training this week, we are pleased he is back and it gives us the chance to rest someone who played nine games in 28 days.”

Joe Burrow can become third quarterback with four or more TD passes in four straight games

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bengals quarterback Joe Burrow had seven touchdown passes in his team’s first four games of the season. In the last three, he has had 13.

With four against the Dolphins, four against the Ravens, and five against the Titans, Burrow has the chance to become only the third quarterback in NFL history to throw four or more touchdown passes in four straight games.

Via NBC Sports research, the two others were Colts quarterback Peyton Manning in 2004 and Dolphins quarterback Dan Marino in 1984. Both won the league MVP award in their seasons of four straight four-or-more touchdown games.

Burrow’s odds of winning the MVP for 2026 are currently at +475, behind only Josh Allen and Brock Purdy.

Burrow currently leads the league with 20 touchdown passes. And the Bengals, after starting 2-2, are now 5-2.

They face the 3-5 Falcons on Sunday morning in Madrid at 9:30 a.m. ET on Sunday.

Charlie Austin reacts to awkward Derby County, Sammie Szmodics interview

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Former QPR, Burnley and Southampton striker Charlie Austin has backed Derby County forward Sammie Szmodics after his tense interview with a local reporter last weekend.

It has been a turbulent start to the season for Derby, and Szmodics made his frustration clear after the 4-1 defeat at Millwall last Saturday when he was involved in a heated exchange with BBC Radio Derby's Ed Dawes.

It was another day to forget for Derby last Saturday as they were comprehensively beaten by Millwall at The Den, with three of the Lions' goals coming in the opening 17 minutes, and Szmodics fronted up to the media post-match.

However, when Dawes suggested to Szmodics that he would be particularly angry about the situation as he was 'John Eustace's boy', it is fair to say that the 31-year-old was less than impressed, responding: "Yeah, of course, because I'm a professional footballer that wants to win. I'm not John Eustace's boy."

Dawes later attempted to clarify his comments, stating that he was referring to the fact that Szmodics had played for Eustace previously, to which he replied: "Yes, I've played under the manager, I'm not his boy, but we're all in it together."

After spending the second half of last season on loan at Pride Park, Szmodics returned to Derby on a season-long loan in August, and the move will become permanent in the summer for a fee of around £2.7 million — or £5 million if the club win promoted to the Premier League — after an appearance-related clause in the deal was triggered.

Szmodics has scored four goals and provided one assist in 14 appearances this season, but he is set to be out for six weeks after aggravating a knee injury that had been troubling him for much of the campaign in the 4-0 defeat to Norwich City on Tuesday night.

Szmodics' interview has caused plenty of debate over the past week, and Austin has become the latest pundit to give his opinion on the saga, praising the Derby forward for his reaction to what he believes was an inappropriate question.

"They know they're not performing," Austin said on his Chaz Kicks Off! podcast.

"Eustace was the assistant manager at QPR when I went back there, but he was also in the Derby starting XI when Bobby Zamora scored in that play-off final to beat them 1-0 and Richard Keogh cried.

"Hear me out here, Sammie Szmodics saying to that reporter 'I'm not his boy', fair play to you mate, I've got a lot of time for it.

"The reporter was being an absolute one of them mate, let me tell you, suggesting 'you're getting picked when you're not performing because you're his boy'.

"Fair play to Sammie Szmodics for giving him a little bit back.

"Players know they're underperfoming, they don't need someone from the radio coming out with nonsense questions.

"He's had a little nibble back, fair play to him.

"The problem you've got is, you can only do the talking on the pitch, and that's what matters.

"At the moment, Derby are not talking, they're not performing, and they are in trouble."

Szmodics is one of a number of Derby players who have worked under Eustace at one of his previous clubs, and while the pair have a strong relationship, it is understandable that he took exception to Dawes' question after the defeat at Millwall.

After a 10-game winless run, the Rams lifted some of the pressure on Eustace with a 3-1 home victory over Bolton Wanderers on Friday night in Szmodics' absence, but they will still head into the international break sitting in the Championship relegation zone.

Should Derby County part company with John Eustace during the upcoming international break? Tell us in the comments below!

It seems likely that Eustace will now be given more time to turn Derby's fortunes around, and he will be hoping that he is still in charge at Pride Park once Szmodics returns from injury in the New Year.

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The recruitment team at Wolves will already have one eye on the January transfer window as they look to strengthen Cesar Peixoto’s squad to help their promotion push.

It seems inevitable that Wanderers will try to bring in a new striker, but, aside from the lack of depth up top, the Portuguese head coach will be pleased with the overall state of the squad.

However, one problem facing Wolves is the size of the squad, as they are already at the 25-man limit. Therefore, to secure new additions, players will have to leave.

And, one man who may be open to a move is Tommy Doyle, with the midfielder having only played 281 minutes in the Championship this season heading out of the midweek fixtures.

As well as that, Peixoto doesn’t seem to know Doyle’s best position, as he had operated higher up the pitch before coming on as a number six in the recent win over Bristol City.

So, the £5m signing may feel he is at a stage in his career where he can play regularly and nail down a permanent home.

With that in mind, we look at TWO Championship clubs who should be targeting the 25-year-old in the New Year…

The Bluebirds have had some fantastic moments back in the Championship under the guidance of Brian Barry-Murphy, who has implemented a clear style of play with the Welsh side.

Yet, the one major problem Cardiff have right now is a lack of depth, and, in truth, they could do with strengthening in various areas in the January transfer window.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

Again, he should be able to flourish in a possession-based approach, as Doyle can help control the tempo, and he would get the ball forward quickly. Plus, his mentality and experience at this level should help a relatively young Bluebirds squad.

After a slow start to the season, Norwich look to have finally turned a corner, and there will be a belief that they can still win promotion this season, which was always the aim under Philippe Clement.

But, the first few months of the season have shown that Norwich are still lacking depth, even if they have been very unfortunate with injuries.

With Kenny McLean missing too many games through injury, Norwich are in need of a new midfielder who can hold the ball, move it quickly, and help control games - which is what Doyle is all about.

Flick and Barcelona aiming to cement top spot against ‘excellent’ Atletico

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Hansi Flick has stressed the importance of going into the international break at the top of LaLiga as Barcelona prepare to lock horns with Atletico Madrid on Sunday.

Barcelona make the trip to the Metropolitano with a two-point buffer over Diego Simeone's side, while Real Madrid are ready to pounce should either team slip up.

The Blaugrana have won their last four away LaLiga games against Atletico and could become the third team in the competition's history to achieve five consecutive victories at the Rojiblancos' home ground, after Athletic Club in 1955 (W5) and Real Madrid in 2013 (W6).

However, Atletico come into the clash having won their last seven LaLiga matches, only managing a longer winning run in January 2025, when they won eight games in a row.

Simeone's side have also won two of their last five games against Barcelona in all competitions (L3), one more victory than they managed in their previous 10 meetings with the Blaugrana (W1 D1 L8), and Flick is expecting another stern test for his players.

"Every game is important. There are three or four excellent teams in LaLiga that are tight in the standings," Flick told reporters.

"It's important to beat Atletico there. We'll give it our all, but there's a long way to go until the end of the season.

"They are a tough team with a fantastic coach. It will be a demanding match. The previous games are in the past.

"The important thing is tomorrow, and we know that they are a fantastic team, and we want and must win to be at the top of the table before the break."

Big stage. Big opportunity. https://t.co/rLSk2Boguj

— FC Barcelona (@FCBarcelona) November 7, 2026

Barcelona arrive in a rich vein of form, having returned to winning ways in the Champions League with a comfortable 3-0 victory over Unai Emery's Aston Villa.

Flick's side have scored 46 goals in 11 games in the 2026-27 LaLiga season, with only Athletic in 1930-31 (49) netting more goals after their first 11 matches of a single campaign in the competition's history (Sevilla also scored 46 in 1940-41).

And their form has been spearheaded by Raphinha, who has scored 15 goals in 10 games, becoming only the second player to reach this tally after his first 10 appearances of a campaign in the competition in the 21st century, after Cristiano Ronaldo with Madrid in 2014-15 (18).

The Brazilian put pen to paper on a new contract with Barcelona earlier this week, extending his stay in Spain until at least 2030.

Raphinha has made 190 appearances in all competitions for the club, scoring 94 goals in total as well as providing 55 assists, with no player recording more goal involvements (149) or chances created (347) for the club in all competitions since his debut.

Only Robert Lewandowski (120) has scored more goals than Raphinha in that time, with only Lamine Yamal (56) bettering his assist tally.

"I think it's the best situation for the club and for Rapha. I'm happy with his renewal, it's important for the future of the club. He's our captain," Flick said of Raphinha.

Tottenham ‘have confidence’ in VAR despite Bogle decision, says De Zerbi

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Roberto De Zerbi insisted Tottenham have "confidence" in VAR, despite the decision not to send off Jayden Bogle during their defeat to Leeds United.

Spurs saw their recent resurgence brought to an end at Elland Road, where they were beaten 1-0 following a run of three straight wins.

However, Leeds were fortunate not to be reduced to 10 men in the first half, when Bogle caught the back of Mathys Tel's Achilles.

The Spurs winger was forced off, but following a four-minute VAR review, Tony Harrington did not advise referee John Brooks to refer to his pitchside monitor for a second look at the incident.

De Zerbi was later incensed when Pedro Porro was booked for a less malicious challenge on Lukas Nmecha during the second half.

The Spurs boss was subsequently shown his third yellow card of the season, meaning he must now serve a touchline ban during their next game against Ipswich Town.

Nevertheless, De Zerbi was philosophical when reviewing the performance of VAR during his post-match interviews.

Thank you for your backing in West Yorkshire today, Spurs fans https://t.co/WeMmdU10Xh

— Tottenham Hotspur (@SpursOfficial) November 7, 2026

"No [the referee should not have been sent to his pitchside monitor for the Tel challenge], I have big confidence in the referee," he said. "Nothing changes if he goes to watch the clip or not.

"There was the VAR, and if they didn't take the right decision, I don't know, but we have confidence. We have to keep our eyes on the performance where we can improve.

"Pedro Porro was a normal tackle, a normal duel and was an incredible yellow card. The first situation of Mathys Tel, I didn't see, I can't say nothing."

It was only Tottenham's second defeat in 14 Premier League meetings with Leeds, while they also failed to record a fourth straight victory in the competition at Elland Road.

Spurs recorded 67% of possession during the second half, but registered just four shots to their opponents' 13, and De Zerbi felt his side were made to pay for that lack of a cutting edge.

"First half, I thought we played well, better than Leeds. We created two or three chances to score," he added.

"In the second half, we played badly, and Leeds played much, much better. They deserved to win. We didn't show what we showed in the Chelsea game, and the other games [that we won]."

Zlatan Ibrahimovic Named ‘Cowardly’ Icon as His Toughest Ever Opponent

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Defenders dreaded coming up against Zlatan Ibrahimovic because of his unpredictability, huge frame and ability to find the back of the net from pretty much anywhere on the pitch.

The Swede spent years traveling around Europe to destroy defences, and he spent most of his career in Italy, a league renowned for its defensive style. He relished battling the best warrior-esque centre-backs in Serie A during his era, whether that be for AC Milan, Juventus or Inter Milan.

Ibrahimovic even landed in the Premier League with Manchester United and showed just why he's regarded as one of the best target men in football history. While most of his opponents couldn't handle his power and dominance, he admitted that one did manage to ruffle his feathers and outsmart him physically and mentally.

Ibrahimovic not only went to war with defenders in duels on the ground and in the air but also played mind games with his rivals. It took a big personality to be able to deal with him and Italian football has birthed many gritty and determined centre-backs.

None more so than Giorgio Chiellini, who made life difficult for attackers with his persistence and never-say-die attitude. He made an impression on Ibrahimovic whenever they clashed as he explained back in 2016 (via The Score):

"For me Giorgio Chiellini is the toughest, and the roughest, marker that I have ever faced."

Being marked by Chiellini came as a nuisance not only to Ibrahimovic but many other forwards during the Italian's legendary career. With a six-foot-two inch frame, he combined old-school fashioned defending with physicality to boss the opposition.

Ibrahimovic added: "He is the one that I suffer against the most. It’s truly difficult to play against him, he does not give you space, he does not let you breathe."

When the Swede returned to Turin with Inter to face Juve, he ended up battered and bruised thanks to Chiellini. In his autobiography he recalled this while making an accusation: "He tried to provoke me, and then he tackled me from behind. That’s a cowardly thing to do. You don’t see the guy coming, and I went down in pain. I was in a lot of pain."

Chiellini spent the majority of his career at Juventus, winning nine Serie A titles among many other honours. He also enjoyed success with Italy's national team including winning Euro 2020 when he scored a vital equaliser in a final win over England at Wembley.

The 42-year-old retired in January 2024 while at MLS club Los Angeles FC and did so having cemented his legacy as one of the best Italian defenders of all-time. And he also is only one of a few who made life difficult for Ibrahimovic on the pitch.

Another former Serie A marksman who, like Ibrahimovic, didn't enjoy facing Chiellini is Edinson Cavani, who encountered the Juve legend while at Napoli. The Uruguayan became one of the most prolific forwards in the modern era of Italian football.

Cavani opined while at Manchester United in September 2021: "The most difficult defender I’ve come up against was Giorgio Chiellini. We’ve played against each other in many games, and we’ve competed against each other all over the pitch, so yes I can tell you that Giorgio Chiellini has been the toughest defender I’ve had to face."

That's two of the very best strikers to grace Serie A who both acknowledge Chiellini as their toughest opponent. Had he moved away from Italy to another top European league such as the Premier League, it would have been interesting to see how he fared.

Nicolas Jackson has emerged as one of the Premier League’s most dangerous attacking players this season, and his impressive performances highlight why Chelsea could eventually regret letting him leave.

The former Chelsea striker has rediscovered his best form and become an increasingly influential figure for his current side. His contribution in front of goal has been particularly impressive, with the £65 million-rated attacker responsible for half of his team’s goals so far this season.

According to Opta Analyst statistics via Daily Briefing, Jackson has scored five goals in eight Premier League appearances for Aston Villa, making him the club’s leading scorer in the competition. He has also found the net in consecutive league matches, suggesting that he is building considerable momentum under Unai Emery.

Chelsea allowed Jackson to leave Stamford Bridge in the summer, with Aston Villa securing his services in a deal reportedly worth £65 million, including add-ons. The decision could prove costly for the Blues, particularly given the Senegalese international’s ability to offer pace, movement and a consistent goal threat in the final third.

The 25-year-old previously struggled to convince everyone during his time in West London, but he appears to have found an environment where he can showcase his attacking qualities more effectively.

His previous experience working with Emery at Villarreal could also be playing an important role in his resurgence. The Villa manager knows how to get the best out of the forward, and their existing relationship could help Jackson maintain his impressive standards throughout the campaign.

Meanwhile, Manchester United will have to be particularly wary of the former Chelsea star when they face Aston Villa. Ruben Amorim’s side have conceded 15 goals in nine league matches without keeping a single clean sheet, leaving considerable room for improvement defensively.

Jackson’s current form makes him a major threat, and he will be determined to demonstrate exactly what Chelsea are missing.

For the Blues, watching a former attacking talent flourish elsewhere could become an uncomfortable reminder of their summer transfer decision.

Lately, Paris Saint-Germain has not been polished. I think during my coaching career we have been able to control that. There are bad times and good times, and I’m happy with what we’re doing. Injuries are part of high-level football and they are difficult to control”he confided at a press conference.

Worse Than Porro: De Zerbi Must Drop Kudus After Leeds 1-0 Tottenham

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Tottenham Hotspur may have felt that Jayden Bogle should have been sent off in their 1-0 defeat to Leeds at Elland Road on Saturday lunchtime, but it shouldn't deflect from what was a poor display from the in-form visitors.

Tottenham came into the clash in Yorkshire on the back of three successive Premier League victories, but in what was a pretty poor top flight game, they still looked second best compared to Daniel Farke's men for the majority of the game.

Spurs should have been full of confidence in the match before the two-week international break, but instead it was Noah Okafor's late winner that separated the sides.

The north London outfit will argue that they should have been playing against 10 men for the majority of the game after Bogle's first-half challenge on Mathys Tel, which went unpunished despite a four-minute VAR review.

That was a decision that former Premier League referee Mike Dean disagreed with on Sky Sports' Soccer Saturday, as he said it should '100%' have been a red card, especially after Jean-Philippe Mateta was sent off for something very similar last weekend.

A number of Spurs players simply didn't turn up at Elland Road, including Pedro Porro and Omar Marmoush.

The former struggled to deal with Gabriel Gudmundsson from a defensive point of view throughout the game, while he offered little threat in attack either.

The Spaniard was also fortunate not to see red following a challenge on Lukas Nmecha, having been harshly booked for an earlier tackle that led to De Zerbi being booked.

Meanwhile, Marmoush largely continues to flatter to deceive in a Spurs shirt following his summer move from Manchester City, and he offered little in the centre-forward position and on the left, where he moved after Tel had to be substituted.

Those two weren't the only poor performers on the day though, with Mohammed Kudus another to struggle badly.

While Porro and Marmoush were notably below-par in Yorkshire, Kudus produced one of his worst performances in a Tottenham shirt against Leeds.

The Ghana international started on the right wing against Farke's side, but he offered very little and was unable to provide the outlet they needed in the second half.

The 26-year-old was eventually replaced by Luca Williams-Barnett in the 83rd minute, and was handed a 3/10 rating by the Standard for his display.

Kudus is known for his ability on the ball but his dribbling was worse than normal, while he could only provide one accurate cross from seven attempts as Tottenham pushed for a goal.

To only win three out of the 13 duels he faced sums up the bad day at the office he had, and based on this display he will be fortunate to keep his spot in the XI against Ipswich after the international break.

While De Zerbi has made no secret of the fact that he is a big fan of Kudus having tried to sign him for Brighton previously, he can't continue to start matches if he performs like that.

The Italian boss has other options to pick from too with Luca Williams-Barnett, James Maddison and Dejan Kulusevski all on the bench on Saturday, while Savio could be back in action after the international break after injury.

Tel could well be missing for some weeks after leaving Elland Road on crutches, but it doesn't mean that Kudus will start again against the Tractor Boys.

Based on this display, he should be dropped regardless.

Tottenham have some strong youngsters coming through.

Ex-refs chief drops Dan Burn disallowed goal verdict as Newcastle footage re-analysed

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Dan Burn's goal for Newcastle against Fulham was rightly disallowed for Yoane Wissa's foul on Timothy Castagne.

That is according to former PGMOL chief and FIFA referee, Keith Hackett, who has exclusively told Football Insider that Rob Jones made the correct call to stop Newcastle opening the scoring in the first half.

Newcastle were held to a 1-1 draw at Fulham on Saturday as Rodrigo Muniz's flicked goal earned a point shortly after Jacob Murphy's stunning 80th-minute opener.

The visitors felt they should have been ahead in the 33rd minute as Burn struck from Murphy's dangerous cross.

Newcastle's captain had peeled away in celebration against his old club before Jones blew his whistle to signify a foul in the build-up spotted by an assistant referee.

Magpies fans flocked to social media following the controversial incident, but seemingly did not have any grounds to complain.

Burn was able to drift into the box and poke Murphy's fizzed cross past Bernd Leno at the near post just after the half hour mark.

He celebrated for around 10 seconds before it was clear that the goal was not set to stand, as Jones insinuated that an assistant had flagged for an offence in the box.

Replays showed that Wissa had tussled with Castagne and guided him closer to the goal line so he would play Burn onside from the cross.

Former referees chief Hackett has insisted that, despite Newcastle players and fans' protests, the DR Congo striker did enough to warrant being penalised for a foul on his opponent.

Speaking exclusively to Football Insider, he said: "Yes, the number nine has fouled his opponent, therefore, it was correct to rule the goal out for that foul."

The Premier League Match Centre's statement about the disallowed goal has provided more information for those involved to take in.

#FULNEW – 33’The referee’s call of no goal to Newcastle United was checked and confirmed by VAR – with it deemed that Wissa fouled Castagne in the build-up. AR2: “Defensive foul at the back post. Wissa holding at the back post”.REF: “Castagne held back by Wissa. It’s a…

Magpies manager Jaissle has admitted that he was surprised at the call and did not feel Wissa did enough for it to be ruled out.

Speaking after the game, he said: "I learned that word here, a 'soft' decision. In my eyes, it was not right. Last week we had the situation where Fabian Schar was bleeding in the face (before Everton scored). That is difficult for me to understand."

Napoli chief De Laurentiis: ‘Here is why I cancelled Allegri press conference’

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Napoli President Aurelio De Laurentiis confirms the report he cancelled Max Allegri’s press conference 10 minutes before it was due to begin, but insists ‘there’s no controversy.’

The squad was put into a training retreat from Friday to prepare for Sunday’s Serie A showdown with current leaders Lazio.

This was a response to the recent defeats to Juventus and FC Porto, capitulating 4-1 in the Champions League on Wednesday evening.

Allegri was all ready to speak in the press room this afternoon, with the journalists assembled and equipment set up when it was suddenly announced the whole event was cancelled.

“There is no controversy at Napoli,” wrote President De Laurentiis on his social media channels after reports he was the one who made the call to cancel.

“When I realised that we were about to hold yet another press conference, I preferred to block it. Why? Because I don’t think press conferences are necessary before every game, considering we play every three days.

“We are in a training retreat, and we must avoid any kind of distraction to take away the players’ concentration on the match. When we go into a retreat, there must be total protection.”

Inevitably, this will further fuel reports that De Laurentiis is losing confidence in Allegri, the coach he chose to replace Antonio Conte over the summer even after the tactician was sacked by Milan for missing out on the top four.

Napoli have struggled badly this season, emerging with five wins, two draws and seven defeats, scoring 23 goals and conceding 24.

Noni Madueke is a player transformed: one of his stats for Arsenal vs Hull is startling

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY NCTJ-qualified football journalist with experience at VAVEL, Hayters TV, SPORTbible, and Centredevils. FOCUS Premier League analysis, tactical deep-dives, and original opinion content across Arsenal, Chelsea, Tottenham, Manchester City, and Celtic. THE INSIGHT Evan utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified, high-speed reporting. He provides specialist tactical and analytical content to ensure fans get beyond the headlines and understand the full picture.

Noni Madueke enjoyed a fantastic performance as Arsenal narrowly beat Hull City 1-0 on Saturday.

The 24-year-old would ultimately prove key to the all-important breakthrough, which came when his shot was saved into the path of Martin Odegaard, who made no mistake.

The Gunners have begun to forge a gap on the top of the Premier League table, winning nine of their opening 10 league fixtures.

Madueke is likely enjoying the best form of his Arsenal career so far, having regularly impressed over the last month.

The winger, who was a £52million signing from Chelsea in 2025, has often failed to show why Mikel Arteta was so keen to bring him into the squad.

Throughout the match on Saturday, however, the England international's assets were clear for all to see.

Noni Madueke with a reminder to the rest of the Premier League 🔴🏆 pic.twitter.com/w7QYkP5qGk

From the first whistle, Madueke looked to create danger in and around the Hull box.

The winger gave his defender no respite, running at him time and time again, often beating him in the process.

He came close to scoring on several occasions, and really should have added to the score line when he went through on goal in the closing stages.

Regardless, Madueke remained Arsenal's driving force throughout. He had 13 touches in the opposition box - five more than anybody else.

He created four chances, took four shots - the joint-most - and delivered two accurate crosses.

Perhaps most impressive was the forward's dribbling. He made five successful dribbles throughout the game, at least three more than anyone else.

He was fouled twice, making four recoveries and winning seven of the 10 duels he contested.

Madueke never stopped getting up and down the pitch. His astonishing 21 sprints was at least seven more than any other player, and it would have made Arteta very happy indeed.

Arsenal's attacking signings under Arteta have been called into question on several occasions, and Madueke is often used as a stick to beat him with.

Yet the 24-year-old has looked like an entirely different player in recent weeks, and is proving that he has the ability.

"He's a great example for the rest of the group because before the first international break he wasn't playing," Arteta said after Saturday's game.

"And what he did is, instead of going on holidays, he went to Portugal and got somebody to train with him and got in better shape and condition and immediately he got back into the rhythm.

"You can see now he is full of confidence and trying things and getting past players and he's impacting games with goals and assists and we are very happy with that."

5 Qs, 5 As: Washington fans ‘hate’ the name Commanders

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Washington football team (not to be confused with the Washington Football Team) is on their fifth season of being named the Commanders. And it sounds as though fans of the team are no bigger fans of "Commanders" today than they were in 2022. In fact, the longer they go into the name change, the more they might want to change it again.

It's hard for me to imagine or accept that there is a team in the NFL named the "Commanders", which still feels like a made-up name for a fictional pro football league in a Keanu Reeves or Kevin Costner movie.

Even so, this is the name that they're stuck with today, and it is the team that the Los Angeles Rams will face on Sunday in a critical moment for both franchises:

If the Rams lose, all hell could break loose before the trade deadline Tuesday.

If the Commanders lose, head coach Dan Quinn may not be associated with the "football team" by Monday.

Washington is 2-4-1 and seeking their second win in a row since the return of quarterback Jayden Daniels. To get to know them — and their Football Team Fans — better, I sent five questions to Andrew Y at Hogs Haven. He sent me back five answers.

Q: The Commanders don't have a player in the top-30 for rushing yards or the top-60 for receiving yards. I feel like that would be very disappointing for fans, to not have one skill player so far this season who you can at least look forward to watching, win or lose. But those are stats, not necessarily reality. Who is the one healthy skill player on the team who Washington fans can look forward to watching each week?

A: I think the player most Commanders fans would point to is rookie WR and 3rd-round pick Antonio Williams. He's shown tremendous route-running nuance and short-area quickness to get open in the middle field and has been a consistent target getter, with 23 catches for 259 yards so far this season. Those simple stats don't rely do credit to his ability though, as he's generated a 96.7 passer rating when targeted and is PFF's second-highest graded rookie receiver. Dan Quinn made clear that with the loss of Terry McLaurin, he expects Antonio Williams' role to increase the most and indeed Williams led all receivers in snaps and routes last week against the Eagles.

Q: Five years in, how is the name "Commanders" sitting with fans? Do you ever long for the days of Football Team again?

A: Honestly, I think most fans hate the name Commanders. It's the last stinky gift given to us by former owner Dan Snyder that the new leadership hasn't been able to reverse. The names that seemed most popular with fans (and with me) were Red Wolves (a nod to the old name, but with a cool animal attached) or Warthogs (which has alliteration with Washington and could be shortened to 'Hogs to reference the famous Hogs offensive line of the glory days). There is a contingent of fans that actually liked the name Washington Football Team and many would probably still take it over Commanders, but I always suspected it was more driven by nostalgia for the old name and a feeling that if we can't have that name, then no name is better. Washington Football Team feels to me more like having no name and certainly doesn't lend itself to new branding. I'd rather have a new team name and branding, it just needs to be a good name and good branding.

Q: It's the end of 2026 and the beginning of 2027. No changes have been made yet. Dan Quinn is the head coach, Adam Peters is the GM, the roster is intact. If you can make one change to the Commanders team/organization to set them on the right path, what do you do?

A: Unless the rest of the season plays out very differently than it has up until now, I would fire Dan Quinn and let Adam Peters lead a search to choose the next head coach. I think Adam Peters has actually done a pretty good job of rebuilding the roster and giving the team enough depth to withstand an avalanche of injuries this year. However, they often look poorly coached, with the most yards erased by penalty in the NFL, slow playcalls and players taking too much time to get into position, and regular mistakes in situational awareness and game management that lose games.

However, one other change needs to be made. The biggest reason the team was able to go on that 2024 playoff run and has been unable to repeat is that our 2024 season was one of our healthiest ever, with few major injuries to core players in the regular season. That's a major outlier, as Washington has had the most Adjusted Games Lost (AGL) due to injury in the NFL since 2009 (!) and only the Giants have been in the same tier of AGL. This year is no different, with four of the team's highest-paid players already done for the season. To be one of the NFL's most-injured teams over that span of time is significant and unusual in that it covers multiple front office staffs, coaching staffs, and even medical staffs.

The one constant in that time has been the strength and conditioning staff, led by Chad Englehart. Englehart started as an assistant strength and conditioning coach in 2010 before being promoted to head of strength and conditioning in 2017. He's stayed on so long because he's extremely popular with players and rates highly in NFLPA surveys, but I'm convinced his staff are doing something that leads to Washington's players being more injury prone, perhaps by over-training them. An outlier injury rate over 17 years can't be simple bad luck. I would order a full external evaluation of the team's strength & conditioning program and make major changes there.

Q: Who are you happier to see fail this season: The Cowboys or the Eagles?

A: It's a delight to see both of them fail, but more so the Eagles. The Eagles have come to be seen by people around the NFL as the "class of the division" and their fans love to loudly reiterate that belief. It's great seeing them get taken down a peg with a nightmare season most didn't see coming. I've noticed there's a bit of an age gap in how people respond to this question. Most fans over 50ish years of age hate the Cowboys more and still vividly remember "we want Dallas" chants and the heyday of the rivalry from the 80s and 90s, but I think younger fans would generally prefer to watch the Eagles flounder.

Q: What's your happiest memory of the Washington team over the last 30 years? What's been the pinnacle of being a fan of this team since the mid 90s?

A: Honestly, my happiest memory in that time period was when Dan Snyder finally sold the team. His rotten ownership had been the millstone around the team's neck that prevented it from ever rising up and ensured it was always surrounded by negative drama. Most fans by that point knew we'd never be a serious football operation with Dan Snyder in charge, so the sale was the first real light after a long period of darkness that signalled things could finally change.

I wasn't prepared for how quickly that would happen though, because then followed the Commanders 2024 season with a dynamic rookie QB. That season blew out anything that happened under the Dan Snyder era: first time the team won 12 regular-season games since the '91 Super Bowl team, first time the team made the NFC Championship game since the '91 team, etc. In addition, so many of those games ended in exciting, last-minute wins that the team got the nickname "Cardiac Commanders." That season was the pinnacle of being a fan of the team since the mid-90s. The victory over the Lions in the playoffs was the single best game, because nobody expected us to boat race the Lions and advance to the conference championship like that.

→ Baca artikel asli

Saints release RB Kendre Miller

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Saints, on the last day before their first game since Week 7, have made a change.

Via Nick Underhill of NewOrleans.football, the Saints have waived running back Kendre Miller.

Miller arrived as a third-round pick (71st overall) out of TCU in 2023. He has 184 career carries for 656 yards, a per-carry average of 3.56 yards. He also has 29 career catches for 217 yards.

The Saints presumably tried to trade Miller but found no takers. He’ll now go to waivers. If he clears waivers, he’ll become a free agent.

Travis Etienne, who arrived in free agency, remains on injured reserve with a hamstring injury. The Saints currently have Alvin Kamara, C.J. Donaldson, and Audric Estimé on the depth chart. Estimé arrived recently on waivers from the Browns.

The 2-5 Saints host the 5-3 Browns on Sunday. The Saints hope to avoid their first 0-4 start at home since 1980 (not including the “home” game in Paris). That year, the Saints went 0-8 at home.

Aston Villa are reportedly close to a new contract with Unai Emery as the club looks to secure his long-term future amid interest from Manchester United.

According to a report from Football Insider, negotiations between the two parties have progressed significantly, with the Spaniard keen to continue his successful spell at Villa Park.

Emery has established himself as one of the most influential managers in Aston Villa’s recent history, transforming the club into a competitive force in English and European football. His achievements, including last season’s Europa League triumph, have strengthened his position at the club.

The Villa hierarchy are determined to reward his contribution with improved terms, especially as speculation about his managerial future continues to attract attention. His existing agreement runs until 2029, but the Midlands outfit are keen to extend that commitment.

Reports suggest Emery is satisfied with the club’s direction and has given the green light to discussions over a fresh deal. Both sides are now moving towards an agreement that would provide greater certainty over the club’s long-term plans.

Manchester United’s reported interest has added urgency to Aston Villa’s efforts to secure their manager’s services. The Red Devils have been linked with Emery amid uncertainty surrounding Michael Carrick’s position at Old Trafford.

However, the Spaniard has shown little indication that he wants to abandon the project he has built at Villa Park. Aston Villa are also confident they can reach an agreement, allowing them to put the speculation surrounding his future to rest.

Keeping Emery would represent a significant boost for Villa as they look to maintain their progress and compete consistently at the highest level. His experience, tactical expertise and ability to develop players make him central to their ambitions.

A new contract would therefore be an important statement of intent from the club.

He played for Wolves and Birmingham City - Then police made shocking discovery worth £160k

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bethany Patrick is a football writer based in Manchester, UK.

Her work focuses on the EFL, with particular interest in football governance, supporter culture, and fan experience and access.

Bethany is a director of the award-winning Sheffield Wednesday Women’s Supporters Group, where she works on fan engagement, strategy and campaigns for greater inclusion across the game.

She regularly represents supporters in governance spaces across Europe, including government panels and discussions around football regulation and the supporter's voice.

She has previously worked as a researcher for The Stretford Paddock and is a regular contributor to The Wednesday Week.

Michael Branch was once regarded as one of English football's brightest young prospects, earning comparisons with Robbie Fowler after breaking into Everton's first team as a teenager.

The former striker went on to represent Wolverhampton Wanderers, Birmingham City and Manchester City, as well as earning international recognition with England Under-21s.

However, his promising career eventually took a dramatic turn, culminating in a seven-year prison sentence in 2012 after police uncovered his involvement in serious drug offences.

Born in Liverpool in October 1978, Branch came through Everton's academy before making his senior debut against Manchester United at Old Trafford in February 1996, aged just 17.

His emergence generated considerable excitement at Goodison Park, with the young forward quickly attracting comparisons to Liverpool striker Robbie Fowler.

However, Branch struggled to fulfil the expectations placed upon him and gradually fell out of favour at Everton.

Loan spells at Manchester City and Birmingham City followed before he joined Wolves on loan in late 1999. After scoring four times in 11 appearances, the Midlands club made his move permanent for £500,000.

Branch initially featured regularly at Molineux but struggled to maintain his form, managing just six more goals in 67 appearances following his permanent arrival.

Further loan spells at Reading and Hull City followed before he departed Wolves in 2003, subsequently representing Bradford City and Chester City.

His professional career eventually came to an end in 2006, with spells at non-league Halifax Town and Burscough following as he attempted to prolong his playing days.

Following his retirement from professional football, Branch found himself struggling with financial difficulties and became involved in the criminal underworld.

On July 10, 2012, he was arrested by officers from the Serious Organised Crime Agency following an investigation into drug supply.

The investigation uncovered two separate incidents. In March 2012, Branch had handed over almost three kilograms of amphetamine to another man in a Liverpool car park.

Andre has revealed he turned down 'life-changing money' from Liverpool when he was at Fluminense, a year before he left to join Wolves.

Four months later, officers searched his home in Otterspool, Liverpool, where they discovered one kilogram of high-purity cocaine.

The drugs were reported to have an estimated street value of approximately £160,000, making for a shocking discovery involving a former professional footballer who had once represented his country at youth level.

Branch subsequently admitted charges of supplying Class A and Class B drugs at Chester Crown Court. On November 8, 2012, he was sentenced to seven years in prison.

He ultimately served three-and-a-half years behind bars, with his release coming in 2016.

However, Branch's story did not end with his imprisonment, with the former Everton forward working hard to rebuild his life following his release.

He joined Everton in the Community in 2016, initially working on programmes supporting former offenders as they attempted to reintegrate into society.

His experiences also helped him connect with vulnerable young people.

Branch has since taken on various roles within the charity, including outreach work supporting young people into education, training and employment.

More recently, he established Social Striders Liverpool, a free running group designed to promote positive mental health through exercise and conversation.

Having previously spoken openly about the difficulties he experienced following his imprisonment, Branch has also become an advocate for mental health awareness and rehabilitation.

Andoni Iraola suggests Liverpool superstar could now leave

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Andoni Iraola has confirmed a Liverpool superstar could be on the move.

The Spaniard knew what he was getting into when he took the reins at Anfield. It's a difficult period for Liverpool at the moment.

The Reds were once at the pinnacle of football. Right now, though, they are nowhere near.

There's an exciting squad in place with a lot of future superstars. However, the squad still has a fair few holes that need to be fixed before it is ready to compete against the very best teams on the planet.

Iraola is in charge of making sure this transition goes smoothly. He has to implement a playing style that is easy to comprehend and integrate the young players who will play a key role in the future of the club.

Meet Liverpool's next generationIt's fair to say Iraola has done a good job at this so far. Liverpool are definitely playing with a distinct style again, and there is a lot of promise we've seen in Iraola's philosophy so far. Ultimately, Liverpool's best players this season have also predominantly been the youngsters. Jeremy Jacquet caught the eye as soon as he arrived at Anfield. Lewis Koumas has been brilliant off the bench with his fervent energy and constant movement. More recently, in the last few weeks, Rio Ngumoha and Trey Nyoni have been Liverpool's best players on the pitch. Both of them have shone with their technical ability and footballing IQ. Both of them also have the potential to be world-class footballers. It's fair to say Nyoni, Ngumoha and Jacquet are the future of the Reds.

Ultimately, Liverpool's best players this season have also predominantly been the youngsters. Jeremy Jacquet caught the eye as soon as he arrived at Anfield.

Lewis Koumas has been brilliant off the bench with his fervent energy and constant movement.

More recently, in the last few weeks, Rio Ngumoha and Trey Nyoni have been Liverpool's best players on the pitch. Both of them have shone with their technical ability and footballing IQ.

Both of them also have the potential to be world-class footballers. It's fair to say Nyoni, Ngumoha and Jacquet are the future of the Reds.

But there is also a changing of the guard at Anfield. Liverpool have already said goodbye to a lot of key players in recent years and it looks like more could follow soon next summer.

Alisson, Virgil van Dijk and Joe Gomez, the last vestiges of Jurgen Klopp's golden era at the club, all have expiring contracts at the end of the season.

It was the former who Iraola was quizzed about in his pre-match press conference ahead of Liverpool's trip to Crystal Palace. When asked about Alisson's future, Iraola refused to rule out that leaving was not on the cards.

“I don’t know if this will be Alisson's last season. That is a question you have to ask him. I hope not, I hope he continues with us for more time," Iraola said.

“But, when you ask me about contracts and these situations, it is a matter for the players and the club.”

The fact that Iraola doesn't know at this stage is a confirmation that Alisson could leave the club. The final decision is up to him and Julian Ward.

Ex-Man City advisor drops verdict on Everton terminating Jack Grealish's contract 'for free'

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Jack Grealish's loan deal at Everton is unlikely to include any clauses to help send him back to Manchester City without a fee.

That is according to former Man City financial adviser Stefan Borson, who exclusively told Football Insider that the Toffees would have to pay to terminate the winger's contract.

Grealish was absent once again as David Moyes' side took on Coventry City on Friday, 6 November, claiming a 3-1 win at the Hill Dickinson Stadium.

Even without him on the left flank, Thierno Barry's brace turned the game around before Kiernan Dewsbury-Hall ensured all three points stayed on Merseyside.

Grealish's situation has raised some alarm bells, even if Moyes has publicly stated the player can return to the squad after a "breach in club discipline".

Football Insider understands that Everton are considering ripping up his contract ahead of the January window, but it will come at a cost.

Speaking exclusively to Football Insider, Borson has explained that, with Man City not wanting the player back, Everton will have to pay a fee to get rid of him.

"I'd be amazed if Man City had given Everton a release, effectively, even in this situation, or almost especially in this situation," he said.

"One of the reasons that there is an issue with the player is because he's obviously got some personal issues. Otherwise, he would be at City.

"We've known for quite a while that there are one or two things in his private life that impact his on-field performance.

"So I very much doubt there's a clause where Everton can say, well, if this happens, they can return him effectively for free.

"Of course, they can terminate the agreement and pay Manchester City to have him back or continue to pay his wages."

The Toffees do have a lot to consider, even if there is the possibility of Grealish being welcomed back into the squad this season.

Football Insider understands that terminating the player's contract would allow Moyes to add another key name to the squad in January.

The Merseyside club have already been linked with a couple of attackers, such as Kelvin Yeboah and Giorgi Kvernadze, and January is just around the corner.

It would appear that The Friedkin Group (TFG) have no desire to splash the cash while attempting to sell the club after just 21 months in charge.

Serie A | Parma 1-2 Bologna: Ducali’s Derby hope ruined by early red card

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Parma were down to 10 men for practically all of this local derby, yet were only beaten 2-1 by Bologna thanks to Arthur Theate and a Riccardo Orsolini penalty, a fourth straight victory.

This was an intriguing Emilia-Romagna Derby between two sides who already changed coach early in the campaign. Alberto Gilardino was celebrating his first victory on the Parma bench with a 3-0 away to Atalanta on Monday, but missed Adrian Bernabé and Dominik Drobnic.

Bologna were on a roll under Raffaele Palladino of three straight wins, even with Jay Enem, Emil Holm and Lorenzo De Silvestri sidelined, but were boosted by the return of Roberto Piccoli to the bench.

In the opening minute, Giovanni Daffara had to scramble the ball round the near post when Federico Bernardeschi’s snapshot took a double deflection.

The game changed after eight minutes when Abdoulaye Ndiaye and Jens Odgaard went down in a tangle just outside the penalty area, the referee originally waving play on to suggest they were fouling each other. However, he changed his mind following a VAR review, as Odgaard having a handful of shorts was considered insufficient to rescue Ndiaye from a red card for DOGSO (Denial of a Goal Scoring Opportunity).

Bologna started to pour forward against 10-man Parma, with a Juan Miranda volley charged down by Benja Cremaschi, and a couple of headers from Odgaard and Nicolò Casale just off target.

David Romero fell very awkwardly after a Casale sliding tackle and seemed to dislocate his shoulder, so had to be substituted.

Enrico Delprato’s header flashed wide of the far post on a free kick, but Bologna were also forced into a change for Miranda’s muscular injury.

Substitute Samuel Mbangula’s curler was palmed out from under the bar, then Daffara was tested by Odgaard at the near post, also parrying a Tommaso Pobega strike.

Federico Bernardeschi’s acrobatic volley on a Nicolò Cambiaghi cross forced another strong Daffara save at the dawn of the second half, while Cambiaghi’s free header was straight at the Parma goalkeeper.

The siege finally paid off when Cambiaghi stood up a cross from the right for the Theate diving header from six yards to break the deadlock.

Moments later, the referee pointed to the spot for a Mandela Keita foul on Bernardeschi, but VAR clarified the contact was just outside the penalty area.

Daffara needed quick reactions after a mad scramble in the six-yard box on a corner, yet Parma nearly equalised on the hour mark when El Bilal Touré started the run from midfield against the entire Bologna defence and was only denied by Lukasz Skorupski’s trailing leg.

Penalty appeals were initially rejected for an incredibly risky Touré tackle from behind on Cambiaghi, a decision that was changed following the VAR review which showed he made no contact with the ball. Riccardo Orsolini converted with power despite Daffara guessing the right way.

VAR got involved again at the other end when Giovanni Fabbian tried an audacious backwards flick from six yards on a set play and was caught by Martin Vitik in the process. A penalty was awarded following a review, and Vincent Sierro converted to keep 10-man Parma in the game.

So did Daffara, getting down to push a Bernardeschi free kick round the far post, so in added time Christian Ordonez saw his screamer fly just over the bar.

It was 10-man Parma who pushed to the bitter end, but were unable to turn the situation around.

Theate 53 (B), Orsolini pen 72 (B), Sierro pen 81 (P)

'I'm amazed' - ex-Refs chief can't believe 'wrong' Kai Havertz decision in Arsenal win vs Hull

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The decision to ask referee Farai Hallam to go to the screen to check a penalty in Arsenal's 1-0 win over Hull City was an unnecessary one.

That is according to former head of PGMOL and FIFA referee, Keith Hackett, speaking exclusively to Football Insider.

The Gunners ultimately won the game courtesy of Martin Odegaard's second half strike, after a first half defined by one odd moment.

VAR Michael Oliver asked Hallam to stop the game, sending him to the pitch-side monitor to look at an incident of shirt-pulling between Nobel Mendy and Kai Havertz.

But as he looked, it became clear that the German had been in an offside position when the offence took place.

Hallam announced exactly to the crowd, who appeared, understandably, bemused by the decision.

A frankly bizarre chain of events at the Emirates Stadium.Kai Havertz goes down in the box after a shirt pull from Nobel Mendy and after a check from VAR, the game is stopped and referee Farai Hallam is sent to the monitor.The referee announces to the stadium there is a… pic.twitter.com/e1KNhWOfvO

While Mendy's shirt tug on Havertz was a clear foul, there were other facets for the referee to consider.

The German had never been getting to the ball, with it having deflected well away from him.

And on further review, it became clear that, when the shirt pull took place, Havertz had been in an offside position.

Hassam was sent to the monitor anyway, and explained that to the crowd after coming away.

"It is clear that offside is the first offence and therefore the game should restart with an indirect free kick to the defending team," Hackett told Football Insider.

"I'm amazed that the offside consideration was not looked at immediately. The correct decision, albeit the wrong way to get to it!"

Interestingly, an argument that Arsenal should have had a penalty for the incident involving Havertz has emerged.

Made clear by BBC Sport correspondent, Dale Johnson, the German must be attempting to play the ball or challenging for the ball to be considered to have committed an offside offence.

Havertz did no such thing, with the foul coming before any other consideration. Whether that rule is appropriate is a different debate - Arsenal may well feel hard done by.

Had Odegaard not found the net in the second half, referees may have had plenty to answer for.

Spurs to make formal complaint over Jayden Bogle decision

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Tottenham Hotspur will make a formal complaint to Pro Ref over the decision not to send off Leeds United full-back Jayden Bogle for a foul that forced Mathys Tel off the pitch.

Bogle was penalised by John Brooks in the first half for treading on Tel’s foot before colliding with the French forward’s knee, but he was not shown either a yellow or red card. VAR looked at the challenge, which resulted in Tel coming off minutes later, but did not deem it serious foul play.

The explanation of the decision was played to the Elland Road crowd, which saw Leeds eventually win 1-0 through Noah Okafor’s goal.

“I’m confirming the on-field decision of no red card. There’s a lack of speed going into the challenge and he’s stepping into the tackle. Confirming a reckless challenge,” VAR Tony Harrington was quoted as saying on the big screen.

Spurs believe Bogle should have been sent off and will write to the refereeing body for an explanation, while head coach Roberto De Zerbi was upset with Brooks for booking Pedro Porro in the second half for a similar tackle.

Leeds head coach Daniel Farke said: “I haven’t watched the scenes back so I can’t give you a clear opinion, out of the game it looked a handball but the scene with Jayden was not too far away from my bench and it looked a very unlucky coming together.

“Fingers crossed for Mathys that he is back soon but it looked like there was not much speed in this action but Jayden Bogle is one of the most fair players in the country, he would never injure or foul a player on purpose because he is such a fair character.”

Tottenham Hotspur’s purple patch of form ended with a worrying loss. Wearing their “Spurple” kit for the first time, Roberto De Zerbi highlighted a lack of character and personality from his players and refused to blame the defeat on referee John Brooks’s decision not to dismiss Jayden Bogle for leaving one of his players injured.

Spurs’ away strip is based on a 1990s design, but this performance was more Christian Gross than Jürgen Klinsmann or David Ginola. Leeds United created the better chances and eventually broke through with 11 minutes remaining when their substitutes combined for Noah Okafor to score the winning goal.

After victories against Coventry City, Chelsea and Crystal Palace, this was a blow for De Zerbi after looking to move Spurs into the European places. Leeds had not scored in 298 minutes of football, nor had they won in five previous matches, yet the Tottenham manager could barely recognise the players who had won three matches in a row.

De Zerbi had warned his team they would face a hostile atmosphere at Elland Road, but instead of rising to the challenge, Spurs were passive until it was too late.

“We didn’t win any duels, we didn’t win any one-to-ones, there was space to play and we didn’t take the right position,” De Zerbi said. “We didn’t show personality, we didn’t show what we are or who we are. It is part of the journey for sure. It can be normal in football but we have to be honest, if you are not honest the future is not good. If you analyse our behaviour we can become a top team.

“We worked very hard this week. We spoke all the days about the intensity, the type of game we would have found in this stadium and the atmosphere, it was not a surprise for us. Sometimes it can happen that we didn’t play the kind of game we want.”

De Zerbi’s honesty shines a light on the amount of work that still needs to be done at Spurs. Even after three straight wins, he has refused to get carried away despite fans looking upwards towards the European places.

De Zerbi admitted the injury was “bad” and that Tel would now be scanned to determine the extent of the problem. The Spurs boss was upset that Pedro Porro was later booked for a similar challenge, while Bogle went unpunished – but he said the referee was not the cause of the defeat.

“I was surprised because in the same position in the second half there were two different fouls, the yellow card for Pedro Porro is not possible. I don’t know if it is a red card or not on Mathys Tel but the parameter of the referee was not good enough for this in both situations,” De Zerbi said.

It meant the home fans were singing their “I Predict A Riot” winning anthem for just the second time this season.

For Leeds, moving into the top half will lift their own hopes of challenging for Europe, such is the congested mid-table.

“Our final third, the quality was perhaps not there but second half there was no doubt just one team was pushing for that win. Another clean sheet another terrific defensive behaviour.”

Leeds United are heartbroken to learn of the passing of supporter Colin Westby at the age of 68. Colin suffered a medical emergency ahead of our Premier League fixture with Manchester United on Sunday 18 October at Elland Road. After being transferred to hospital, he was placed into a medically induced coma and sadly following a period of unresponsiveness, the decision was made to withdraw life support. Colin was a long-standing Season Ticket Holder at the club, attending matches for over 50 years. The thoughts of everyone at Leeds United are with Colin's family and friends at this sad time. We will pay tribute to Colin in the 68th minute of our next Premier League fixture with Tottenham Hotspur, on Saturday 7 November.

Good morning and welcome to live coverage of the Premier League match between Leeds United and Tottenham Hotspur at Elland Road. The home side, lions at night and mice in the morning, have the unenviable record of 21 top-flight matches with a kick-off time before 3pm without a win. All that extra TV moolah comes in handy but their home ground, a bearpit under the floodlights, is neutered when its denizens are yawning instead of being fired up by social lubricant.

Daniel Farke’s team started the campaign well but are now without a win in five, drawing three matches with teams beneath them in the table – Crystal Palace, Manchester United and Sunderland – and  losing to two above them, Arsenal and Bournemouth. In fairness they played well against the champions for an hour and were deservedly in the lead until Mikel Arteta turnd to his billionaire’s bench and changed the game. At Bournemouth last week they also made a decent start, had a goal disallowed for striking Dominic Calvert-Lewin’s arm and then James Trafford chucked one in.

They never recovered, found themselves increasingly bogged down when trying to unlock a low block and were mugged on the break in added time to lose 2-0. They are a robust side overly reliant on their wing-backs to create chances for their No 9 in the absence of the injured Noah Okafor who was unavailable to train with the team this week because of a groin injury but may be fit enough for the bench.

Spurs, by contrast, are buoyant following three wins on the bounce – over Coventry, Chelsea and Palace, two with clean sheets – that has taken them from 19th to ninth, three places above Leeds. They have won their last three visits to Elland Road, 0-4 under Antonio Conte, which proved to be the last match of Marcelo Bielsa’s glorious spell at the Peacocks, 1-4 under Ryan Mason, which sealed Leeds’ relegation, and 1-2 under Tomas Frank last year by virtue of goals from Mathys Tel and Mohammed Kudus as the home side struggled against their width back in the days before their switch to a back three.

Tel also scored in Spurs’ home game, a 1-1 draw in May, and then kicked Ethan Ampadu in the head to concede the penalty from which Leeds equalised. I wonder what kind of impact he will have today. Incidentally Roberto De Zerbi on his last visit to Leeds when manager of Brighton in 2022-23 had such a strop on the touchline when the home team equalised that he was within a gnat’s whisker of being sent off.

‘Hello darkness my old friend, soon you’ll be here by 5pm’ As Simon and Garfunkel never sang…

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The independent voice of Newcastle United since 1988

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Get your daily update and weekly newsletter by signing up today!

Back in August I contributed an article to The Mag which was entitled “The Phoney War”, it was about the lull before hostilities began at the start of the Newcastle United season.

In September I contributed “All Things Bright and Beautiful”, about our promising start to the season.

October’s article “Trick or Treat” arrived a little late last weekend. It raised doubts about how things are unfolding after a series of sloppy defeats.

My offering this month? Fire and darkness.

November starts with the dispiriting onset of darkness. As Simon and Garfunkel never sang, “Hello darkness my old friend, soon you’ll be here by 5pm.”

Darkness aside, there’s been more futility than fire from our footballing favourites. Maybe we’ll see a spark from Newcastle United in West London on Saturday.

Maybe we’ll see two bald men fighting over a comb. An increasingly dull, dreary and desperate attempt to win the coveted prize of being the fourth worst team in the league by the end of the season.

There’s always hope, of course, the thing that kills you.

We hope that Fulham will be even more wobbly than us. We hope that we can bag a fourth win of a mercurial season in the capital this weekend. We hope that the likes of Sunderland, Coventry, Ipswich, Palace, Fulham will finish below us by the odd point or with a less embarrassing goal difference. We hope that Santa will bring us a little stocking filler in the form of a relegation berth for Man City. If so, we hope that we won’t be one of the other two trapdoor teams. We hope that it’s going to be alright. We hope Matthias Jaissle will turn it around. We hope that the Newcastle United players will get it. We hope there’s something for them to get. We hope for a couple of signings in January. We know all about hope as Newcastle supporters. We know the sorts of things we say in our brighter moments. But November darkness doesn’t encourage hope.

There’s a beguiling song by an obscure folk singer Dave Goulder, entitled “The January Man”. It’s partly about the changing seasons, month by month. This month’s line is, “The poor November man sees fire and wind and mist and rain and winter air.” We can identify with that. So can our current manager, who looked like the poor November man at the end of Monday night’s match after shaking hands with the gargoyle Moyes.

If you want a giggle in November’s darkness, search up “David Moyes Dunfermline”. It should bring up an image of a Soccer Stars/Panini type sticker of a young Davey Moyes in his playing days. Be warned: it belongs on the Halloween clearance shelf. Unsettling. NSFW.

We might giggle but fly old Davey has demonstrated a certain brand of English football to MJ three times these last few months. As my Mam used to say, “that’ll learn him a thing or three.”

Aggregate score 7-2. Gesundheit, or something that rhymes with it.

Despite the hype of the Premier League being the world’s finest, we all know the reality: there’s an awful lot of beige football. Much of what is served up is tepid soup prepared by chefs like Moyes, using ingredients from the back of the fridge and the darkest corners of the cupboards. It sustains life but does not bring joy.

Welcome to Richard Masters’ soup kitchen. One bit of bread to dunk each, except for the slippery six. They’re on the “all you can eat” tariff, butter running down their greasy chins. No soup for them. They’re having steak, followed by a selection from the sweet trolley. Coffee. Brandy and cigars.

Here’s the recipe for Masters’ Beige Football Soup, a tried and trusted staple of the English football diet.

The main ingredients: – a pound of Pulis – five fat Sam fingers – a whiffling of Warnock’s whiskers – a measure of Moysie’s mustard – a handful of Hughes hoofball – a brace of Bruceballs. Lob in a few streaky rashers and a head of cabbage.

Method: Clag it all into a pan on a low heat until tepid.

Before serving, add the following to taste: – a dash of well-drilled defence – a rasher of rogue refereeing – a pinch of percentages – a layer of long-throw – a jar of “Wee Davey’s” jam – a measure of Monday night marinade – a can of barely concealed corruption – a scoop of Stoke soil, ideally harvested on a cold, wet midweek night.

Enough nutritional content and calories to keep you up. Best served lukewarm from a vat of vested interests into leaking, cracked bowls, just enough to accumulate points.

November: the month of fire and darkness. Maybe Matthias Jaissle can kindle fire in the hearts of our lads and us supporters. Maybe there’ll be a light in the darkness on Saturday.

Kepa Arrizabalaga set for emotional Athletic Club homecoming at San Mames as Arsenal backup goalkeeper is called up by Basque Country to face Venezuela

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Arsenal goalkeeper Kepa Arrizabalaga is poised for a poignant return to former club Athletic Club's San Mames stadium after earning a call-up from the Basque Country representative team. The shot-stopper has been named in the Euskal Selekzioa squad to take on South American visitors Venezuela during next weekend's international friendly in Bilbao.

Osasuna manager Jagoba Arrasate has included the Ondarroa native as one of the marquee names in his 22-man selection for the clash on Saturday, November 14. Currently serving as understudy to David Raya at Arsenal following previous loan stints with Real Madrid and Bournemouth, Arrizabalaga originally departed Bilbao for Chelsea in 2018. That €80 million move to Stamford Bridge remains the world-record transfer fee ever paid for a goalkeeper.

Joining Arrizabalaga in the squad as players plying their trade outside Spain are Udinese forward Unai Gomez and Bolton Wanderers attacker Gaizka Larrazabal, while Osasuna stopper Aitor Fernandez provides competition between the posts. Arrasate has once again leaned heavily on a familiar domestic core drawn from Athletic Club, Real Sociedad, Osasuna and Deportivo Alaves.

In defence, Athletic's Dani Vivian, Hugo Rincon and Johaneko Louis-Jean are joined by Sociedad trio Jon Pacheco, Igor Zubeldia and Aihen Munoz alongside Osasuna's Inigo Arguibide and Malaga's Einar Galilea.

Midfield options feature Athletic's Mikel Jauregizar, Sociedad's Benat Turrientes, Alaves pair Pablo Ibanez and Ander Guevara, Osasuna's Asier Osambela and Sevilla's Jon Guridi, while Athletic star Oihan Sancet, Osasuna's Kike Barja and Racing Santander duo Inigo Vicente and Asier Villalibre complete the attack.

Organised by Basque Football Federation president Xabier Goni, the fixture marks the representative team's first appearance since defeating Palestine 3-0 in front of a record 51,396 crowd on November 15 last year via goals from Unai Elgezabal, Gorka Guruzeta and Urko Izeta. Next Saturday's contest carries deep cultural weight as it celebrates the 90th anniversary of the first autonomous Basque Government formed under President Jose Antonio Aguirre in 1936.

Aguirre subsequently established the Euzkadi team in 1937 to tour Europe and the Americas during the Spanish Civil War. Venezuela also holds a special bond with the region after welcoming thousands of Basque political exiles fleeing Francisco Franco's dictatorship.

Arrrizabalaga will hope to earn rare starting minutes in familiar surroundings next weekend before returning to north London to resume his backup duties under Mikel Arteta after the international break. On the pitch in Bilbao, Euskal Selekzioa will look to preserve their 100 per cent historical record in what will be their fourth meeting against Venezuela. The hosts previously beat the South Americans 4-3 away in San Cristobal in June 2007, 3-1 at the old San Mames in December 2010 and 4-2 at Mendizorrotza in October 2018.

Why Footballers Wear Tiny Shin Pads

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Watch any professional football match closely, and you may notice something odd about some players' legs: socks rolled down to the calf, with holes cut out of the back and, tucked inside them, a shin-pad barely bigger than a credit card. Jack Grealish made the look famous, but the trend has since spread from the elite game down to Sunday league and junior pitches.

Shin-pads are compulsory under football's laws, so how are players getting away with protecting so little of their shins? And why would they want to flout a rule made to protect them and their most prized asset?

Shin-pads became mandatory in 1990 due to the increasing physical brutality and injury rates seen in Europe's elite leagues.

According to Law 4 of IFAB's (International Football Association Board) Laws of the Game, shin pads must be made of a 'suitable material' and must be 'of an appropriate size to provide reasonable protection'. However, it also states that players are responsible for the 'size and suitability' of their shin-pads, meaning that, ultimately, the decision is up to the individual.

IFAB told BBC Sport that referees are only required to check that some form of shin pads are being worn, meaning that even match officials are not able to force a player to change into bigger, more protective equipment.

An explanation has emerged after players have been spotted with cut holes in the back of their football boots

The majority of professionals who have been quizzed on their shin-guards cite comfort and freedom of movement as the main factor in opting for micro-pads. Given the speed and intensity that the modern game is played at, every gram less that their equipment weighs gives a minute boost to their range of motion and explosiveness.

Another reason is the recent trend of low socks that sit around the calf. According to Law 4, the shin pads must be covered by the players' socks. Therefore, if they were to use the more traditional, bigger shin-pads, players would have to wear longer socks.

The reason Jack Grealish wears his socks rolled down to his ankles and the smallest shin pads you're ever likely to see

Whilst there has not yet been an in-depth study into the effectiveness of mini-shin-pads compared to traditional ones, guidance written by the FA, and the actions of various amateur and semi-professional clubs, offer a damning verdict.

FA guidance written in 2024 states that "any part of the shin that is not covered is not as well protected", before adding that micro shin pads may "increase the risk of injury due to the smaller amount of shin covered." The statement continues by advising that "safety is prioritised by ensuring a good proportion of the shin area is covered and therefore protected."

Various amateur and non-league clubs have been keen to ban mini-shin-pads, including Ilfracombe Town, who prohibited the use of such equipment across all age groups and squads after forward Riley Malin suffered a double-leg break (tibia and fibula) in a 50/50 challenge.

According to the BBC, in August 2024, Penistone Church took similar action after teenager Alfie Collins also suffered a double-leg break, having switched his bigger shin-pads at halftime for a pair that were "no bigger than a credit card."

An increasing number of footballers are playing with holes cut in their socks - here's why.
