West Ham fans given 'dire' Burnley warning after Scott Parker appointment

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

West Ham United fans have been served a warning from a Burnley perspective following the club's decision to appoint Scott Parker in place of Nuno Espírito Santo.

The Hammers dismissed the Portuguese on Tuesday morning, with Parker swiftly installed in the East London hotseat ahead of Wednesday's dramatic draw at Cardiff City.

The Irons found themselves three goals down at half-time through Jack Moylan's hat-trick, but a comeback sparked by Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman's strikes saw them remain eighth, five points behind leaders Middlesbrough.

Parker's return to the club who he played 129 times for was met with mixed opinions given his managerial record, which is largely impressive at Championship level on the surface, although supporters of Fulham, AFC Bournemouth and Burnley can attest to post-promotion woes.

Football League World's Burnley fan pundit, Aaron Wright, is firmly in this camp, believing that the natives could soon get restless once more in E20, even if promotion is achieved.

Parker has won promotion to the Premier League on three occasions at his aforementioned clubs, twice finishing second whilst winning the 2020 play-off final with the Cottagers.

His immediate success at Turf Moor was met with criticism over the style of play, even if a concession of just 16 goals in 46 games in an all-time EFL record.

All three of his promotions have also come with newly-relegated teams, so when asked for his immediate thoughts on Parker's move to East London, Wright told FLW: "It's the most unsurprising news there could ever be that he's got the West Ham job. It's the biggest job he could get by an absolute mile.

"Let's be honest with his record, there was only one other job in the league he was going to take and that would be Wolves, because he's scared of every non-year one parachute team in the Championship.

"He has the best squad in the league and no doubt they will get promoted. No doubt. Whether it's winning the league, that's on Scott Parker. He's yet to do that. Yes, granted one on goal difference - it wasn't for a Plymouth defensive mistake to give Manor Solomon the ball, he would have won the Championship last time," Wright added.

"But there is also the caveat of if we hadn't have drawn 13 games, we'd have won the league. If we'd won one of them instead of drawing 13. It boggles my mind.

"You can't discredit him for also winning the number of games he had to win to cover for 13 0-0's and a few 1-1's in there. Marvellous consistency.

The new Hammers boss has generated plenty of talk for his fashion sense over time

Wright went on to admit that Parker is likely to guide West Ham back to the Premier League at the first attempt, such is the strength of the squad at his disposal, but also fired a warning to the supporters of Burnley's divisional rivals.

"West Ham will probably end up, maybe not walking this league, because I think Middlesbrough and Southampton both stand very good chances and I wouldn't want to face Birmingham in the play-offs currently. But they'll get promoted," he claimed.

"As a Burnley fan, I my thoughts are way past Scott Parker. We're having a relegation fight. I don't care what Scott Parker does now.

"But this is the biggest job he could get, if he can't get them promoted slash win the league, and if he can't then keep them in the Premier League, he needs to rethink pretty much everything. He is never getting a bigger job than this and his reputation as a player has helped him get this one. It's the most guaranteed promotion you will ever have.

"The West Ham fans won't enjoy it, but I think there'll be a good respect to it if it turns out how Burnley's did. 33 games unbeaten is something else.

"Let's not forget, this is a fanbase that saw 22 under Kompany and 23 under Dyche. To reach 33 is remarkable. We didn't lose a game from 5th November. That is insane mentality.

"But when you go up and you beat one team who was in the Premier League the year before, one, away from home because of a mad 6 minutes, we'd barely looked in a game past the end of October. We should have got better results," Wright stated.

"The football's dire. He will bore you to death, and he will cause a fanbase to turn through little snide little comments. He will cause the West Ham fanbase to turn on it. And that has happened at every single club consistently.

Burnley fans, do you agree with this viewpoint on Scott Parker? Comment below!

"There's a reason why Fulham fans and Bournemouth fans don't like him despite promotion. Burnley fans have a begrudging respect for the way he got promoted. 33 unbeaten.

"I don't know how many teams have done that at second tier level, but our defensive three and fullbacks- that three especially of Esteve, Trafford and CJ Egan-Riley -probably will go untouched for many decades in this league. And his football contributed towards that in both positive and negatives."

Given the fact West Ham are already the top scorers in the Championship, it is defensive improvements which need to be made first and foremost, with Parker's aforementioned feats at Turf Moor suggesting he could be the man to bring them to the London Stadium.

The 46-year-old was also a part of the last Hammers side to be relegated in 2011, so is also more than aware of the pressures that come with the fact a club of such stature is in the EFL to begin with.

However, that isn't the only pressure he is facing, as it was recently reported that if promotion isn't achieved in May, the board have the ability to relieve him of his duties at no cost.

'It's a big story to watch' - Keith Wyness: Burnley ‘spiralling’ towards ‘disaster’

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Burnley owner Alan Pace is desperately “searching” for a way to free up money to eat further into the club’s significant debt.

That is according to former Everton chief executive Keith Wyness, speaking exclusively to Football Insider, who believes that the Clarets are far from being out of the woods just yet.

Pace’s Velocity Capital company, who are the official owners of the club, recently saw their striking-off order discontinued, but the club remain in around £86million of debt.

However, Wyness believes that the team’s poor performances on the pitch are not “inspiring” investors to come to the table with financial aid.

Burnley were only relegated from the Premier League last season, but are already sitting inside the Championship’s relegation zone after the opening 14 games.

They have already sacked Nicky Hayen, and new boss Fabio Celestini appears to have a mammoth task ahead of him for the remainder of the campaign.

Everton’s former chief Wyness – who served as CEO at Goodison Park between 2004 and 2009 and now runs a football consultancy advising elite clubs – believes that Burnley are at genuine risk of suffering the “ultimate disaster” of relegation to League One.

That would provide a huge dent to their footballing reputation, while also being likely to heap yet more debt on the struggling club.

Speaking on the latest edition of Football Insider’s Inside Track podcast, Wyness explained how Pace will be scrambling to try and find a solution.

“It's not great for Burnley fans, they're not performing on the pitch and relegation would be an ultimate disaster for this club. Certainly, their position and their performances this season are not inspiring financial markets to come to their support,” he said.

“Pace will be searching for ways to try and release money from somewhere but sometimes the debt becomes due before you want it to be in football.

“It has an accelerator effect, and things can go wrong very quickly in a spiral. So it's a big story to watch for Burnley, as they’re a big, historic club.

“But I'm afraid we've got to pay real attention.”

Wyness also told Football Insider on Friday that Pace should consider selling Espanyol in order to improve cash flow at Burnley.

The American businessman owns both clubs at the moment, but could soon become open to the prospect of cashing in on the La Liga side.

Wyness believes that the deal to buy Espanyol appears to have been “incongruous” for Pace, and hasn’t helped when it comes to dealing with the ongoing issues at Turf Moor.

The Clarets’ latest accounts showed that they have already begun cutting into their debt, but they still have a lot of work to do moving forward.

Schalke will hope to end their abysmal run before the international break as they face fellow strugglers Hoffenheim away from home.

After three away games in a week, Christian Ilzer's men will be back at SNP Arena on Sunday night. The Sinsheim side failed to win those fixtures in three different competitions. The defeat in Bremen ended a two-game winning streak in Bundesliga, days after they were knocked out of the DFB-Pokal by 2. Bundesliga outfit Holstein Kiel.

And, on Thursday, they were very close to upset Europa Conference League winners Crystal Palace before an injury-time strike by former Eintracht Frankfurt midfielder Daichi Kamada undone them. However, Hoffenheim won their last two home games 3-0 against Lyon and Bayer Leverkusen.

3 players who Birmingham City should offload in January, including Lee Buchanan

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Birmingham City have enjoyed a fine season to date after a slightly different approach in the summer transfer window. BCFC made seven new signings, two of whom had already been at the club, so it was a window of evolution not revolution as opposed to previous squad rebuilds.

But, with Chris Davies‘ side firmly in the Championship promotion conversation, the January window offers a chance to strengthen. The Blues can shift out some fringe players to make way for additions who can push them over the line in the bid to make the Premier League.

With that in mind, we put forward three players who should be leaving Birmingham City in the winter…

Somewhat surprisingly, Scottish winger Scott Wright stayed at St Andrew’s in the summer. The 29-year-old spent the second half of last season on loan at Dundee and the strong competition for minutes on the Birmingham City wings meant minutes were bound to be limited.

That has proven to be the case with Wright making only two Championship matchday squads and playing just one minute of senior football in a brief substitute appearance against Swansea City in the Carabao Cup.

Wright sees his contract expire in 2027, but to get back to playing regularly, he should leave BCFC in the winter. A return to Scotland or drop to League One seem like his most likely options.

Another player who has been on the fringes at Birmingham City is Lee Buchanan, although his situation is a little different. There was talk of a potential summer loan move as the left-back rebuilt his fitness after a serious knee injury, but he ended up staying put.

Alex Cochrane‘s emergence in the starting XI and the signing of Kristoffer Lund have left Buchanan as third choice in his favoured position. The Blues have been eager to be careful with his minutes given how long he was out, but by the time January comes around, he should be ready to play more regualrly.

There may be hesitance to cash in right away, so a loan feels more likely for January. Then, his situation can be reassessed in the summer, as he has shown he can be a Championship option before.

Last but not least is Marvin Ducksch, who has made eight Championship appearances off the bench. The German striker showed signs of promise in his debut campaign at Birmingham City, but a drink driving charge brought doubts over his future.

A summer move failed to materialise, but it is still widely expected that the 32-year-old moves on this season. A switch back to Germany is most likely, and could see Ducksch return to impressive form given his record of 71 goals in 138 2. Bundesliga appearances, and 34 in 130 in the Bundesliga.

August Priske is the lead striker at BCFC with Jay Stansfield and Luis Vasquez as the other options. Another centre forward may be needed if Ducksch is sold, but that seems like a logical switch.

3 EFL managers Sunderland should eye as Regis Le Bris sack calls grow

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Sunderland are enduring a difficult start to the new season and Régis Le Bris’ position as head coach is under a lot of pressure as a result.

The Black Cats could turn to the EFL if they feel that a change in the dugout is needed, with the team bottom of the Premier League table.

The Wearside outfit miraculously qualified for the Europa League with a seventh place finish in the top flight last year, just 12 months on from winning the play-off final to earn promotion.

The Frenchman has earned a lot of praise for overseeing Sunderland’s rise, but that could all come to an end unless results turn around quickly.

A 2-0 loss at home to Dinamo Zagreb on Thursday night in the Europa League has further piled on the pressure after last weekend’s 3-1 loss to the newly-promoted Coventry City.

Here we look at three EFL coaches that the Premier League club must eye as the pressure mounts on Le Bris…

Kim Hellberg took over rivals Middlesbrough 12 months ago, and he has done a stellar job in that time.

While the team lost in the play-off final, he had the side immediately playing a distinguished and attractive style of play despite taking over mid-season, which will make him a particularly appealing option to the Black Cats right now.

And his Boro team are currently in the mix for promotion again this season, with star striker Will Lankshear earning a lot of plaudits leading the line for the Teesside outfit.

Is Kim Hellberg ready to manage in the Premier League? Comment your opinion below

He is getting the best out of the player, and that will catch the eye of Sunderland given the similarities between Lankshear and Brian Brobbey.

The Dutchman has scored three goals in the Premier League this season, but they all came in a 5-3 loss to Manchester City.

Getting the best out of the forward will be key to Sunderland turning around their form, and someone like Hellberg could be capable of coming in and unlocking top form from Brobbey.

Chris Davies has been under similar scrutiny at times at Birmingham City, but he’s still had an impressive few years at St. Andrew’s.

Firstly, he oversaw a record-breaking 111 point title victory to secure promotion from League One in his first season in charge.

He then led the Blues to a 10th place finish in the Championship in 2025/26, playing an assertive and proactive style of play.

The 41-year-old spent many years working under Ange Postecoglou, and it’s clear that the Australian was a big influence on his coaching style.

And he could be ready to make the step up to the Premier League, and if Sunderland come calling it’s difficult to see him turning it down.

Similarly to Hellberg, Davies has been able to get the best form from a big centre-forward in August Priske, which is another factor that could persuade the Black Cats to take a look at him.

Brian Barry-Murphy has a similar CV to that of Davies, having earned promotion with Cardiff City from League One.

He also worked under big name coaches, including Pep Guardiola, as a major figure at the academy level at Manchester City.

Jack Moylan's fine start to his Bluebirds career continued with a first-half hat-trick against West Ham United

The Irishman has earned a reputation for working well with developing younger players, which also makes him an attractive option for a club like Sunderland, whose model emphasises the development of young talent.

It would be a big ask for him to step up to the Premier League given his managerial experience so far, but it might just be a risk worth taking for the Black Cats, if Le Bris is dismissed in the near future.

Man City 'don't want Jack Grealish back' in Everton termination twist

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Manchester City have no desire to reintroduce Jack Grealish into the squad, leaving the winger "in limbo" at Everton.

That is according to former Manchester City financial adviser Stefan Borson, who exclusively told Football Insider that a return to the Etihad Stadium this season is unlikely for the winger.

This comes after Football Insider revealed that the Toffees are considering terminating Grealish's contract after a "breach of club discipline".

David Moyes is adamant that a return to the squad is not impossible for the loanee, but an appearance against Coventry City has been ruled out.

Grealish has now become a "headache" for both clubs, with Enzo Maresca not wanting him back at the Etihad after letting him leave in the summer.

With the 31-year-old's City contract expiring at the end of the season, his future as a footballer is looking rather bleak right now.

Speaking exclusively to Football Insider, Borson has stated that Man City would have no plans to bring Grealish back into the squad, even if his Everton deal were to be terminated.

"Man City don't want the player back. Speaking from a football perspective, there's no way City want the player back in the squad," he said.

"He's just a headache for them. He's out of contract in the summer. He's not going to play. I'm sure they just don't want him around the squad.

"So he's in a bit of a limbo. I just don't see it being realistic that Man City left a clause in there that gives Everton a way to return him. So it's a mess."

Grealish remains banished from the Everton squad for the clash with Coventry on Friday, 6 November. How this situation gets resolved is impossible to call.

Grealish was actually asked about his future as a Man City player right after his return to the Hill Dickinson Stadium was confirmed in the summer.

At an event back in September, he was asked the question, and he simply replied: "I don't know, honestly. I'll just have to wait and see what happens.

"I just want to be enjoying myself, and that's the main thing at the moment."

Sadly, it hasn't quite worked out for the tricky winger so far this season, and his off-field problems are becoming far too public as well.

PSG, DJ Snake: “I’ve been doing dry cleaning for Nasser for 1 year” for Ibrahim Maza

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

While the rumors around Ibrahim Maza and Paris Saint-Germain continue to agitate the media, the file has also caused reactions on social networks. Comedian Malik Bentalha publicly called for the young Algerian international to come to the capital. A position which quickly elicited a response from DJ Snake, also close to the Parisian club and particularly invested.

Indeed, in response to Malik Bentalha’s message, the famous French producer released a statement that was amusing to say the least: “I’ve been doing dry cleaning in Nasser for a year. » This demonstrates the enthusiasm generated by the profile of the 20-year-old attacking midfielder, while according to information from RMC SportPSG does not plan to recruit Maza this winter. Despite this enthusiasm, an arrival in January would therefore not be on the agenda.

LeBron held to single digits for only 10th time on Cleveland return

📅 Nov 07, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

LeBron James held to eight points as Philadelphia 76ers lose 119-95 on his return to Cleveland; the 41-year-old received smattering of boos from Cavaliers fans during first half; James spent 11 seasons over two stints with the Cavs

LeBron James was held to single-digit points for only the 10th time in 1,630 NBA regular-season games as his Philadelphia 76ers were beaten on his return to face his former team the Cleveland Cavaliers.

James was held to eight points as the Cavaliers routed the 76ers 119-95 in an NBA Cup group stage game.

The 41-year-old, a native of Akron, Ohio, who elected to sign with Philadelphia in the offseason, instead of returning to Cleveland for a third stint, was 3 of 12 from the field, including 0 for 4 on three-pointers, while he committed six turnovers in 31 minutes.

"Definitely not feeling a good rhythm offensively," James said. "We didn't look like much offensively. We had a couple spurts, but too many turnovers on my behalf."

James received a smattering of boos from Cavaliers fans every time he touched the ball during the first half.

And this time there was no video tribute to the four-time NBA champion - who won one of those titles with Cleveland in 2016. When visiting the Cavaliers last season, they showed highlights of him scoring 25-straight points during Game 5 of the 2007 Eastern Conference finals against the Detroit Pistons, which prompted him to tear up.

James spent 11 seasons over two stints with the Cavs. He left as a free agent in 2010 to join the Miami Heat, came back in 2014 and departed again in 2018 to join the Los Angeles Lakers.

"At the end of the day, nothing can get as worse as December 2, 2010, from a reception standpoint," James said. "I've seen them near the depths of hell when it comes to reception here all the way to the heavens above. So anything in between, it's easy money."

The 76ers' loss was James' second-worst in Cleveland as a visiting player. He is 9-5, but has been on the losing end in his last three trips. Philadelphia will return to Cleveland on March 1.

The Sixers came in with the NBA's best record but fell to 8-2. Head coach Nick Nurse was already looking ahead to Sunday's game at the Chicago Bulls.

"We were lacklustre and they were very, very good. And I think we expected them to be very good tonight considering that they had a couple tough losses in a row," Nurse said.

"We were not very good. Probably once in a while that's going to happen. Important thing is that we get over it and bounce back on Sunday."

Everton v Coventry City: Premier League – live

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

⚽ Premier League updates from the 8pm GMT kick-off ⚽ Today’s Football Daily | Live scores | Table | Mail Scott

54 min: VAR has a good look for offside, but there isn’t one. The goal stands. Coventry would have been fuming had another decision gone against them!

Rudoni dribbles right to left across the face of the Everton box. He lays off to Sakamoto, who feeds Gboho down the left channel and into the area. Gboho takes a touch and spins, putting Branthwaite on his backside before flicking a slow-motion shot across Pickford and into the bottom right. It had been coming.

51 min: Tell you what, that Mykolenko clip on Awoniyi was very close to being on the line. One angle looks like just outside, another the striker’s toe on the line. Who’d be a referee, huh?

50 min: Armstrong scuffs a shot goalwards from a tight angle on the right. It’s easy for Rushworth, but Everton’s first on-target effort for nearly 40 minutes. Coventry then counter through Dasilva, whose cross from the right only just evades Awoniyi in the middle.

49 min: VAR deems the clip on the ankle to be just outside the box. It looked on the line to the naked eye, but that vile jelly can play tricks on you. Be interested to see that again, mind. Everton very close once again to conceding a spot kick.

48 min: Torp slips a ball down the inside-right channel for Awoniyi, who enters the box and feels a light shove from Mykolenko on his back. Or is there a clip on his heel? Either way, he goes over, and VAR is definitely going to take a look at this.

46 min: Gboho immediately strides into space down the middle and, with options either side, opts to shoot himself. The shot’s deflected and bobbles through to Pickford.

Coventry get the second half started. Neither side has made a change.

On Sky Sports, studio pundits Jamie Redknapp and Callum Wilson both suggest James Tarkowski got away with a big one for tugging on Yirenkyi’s hair (39 min). Never mind planting his arm across Pinnock’s face in the same challenge! It should have been a penalty to Coventry, no question. No idea why VAR didn’t get involved.

Half-time advert break. Sign up now! Sign up now! Sign up now? Sign up now!

Everton started brightly, but Coventry ended the half much the stronger; the visitors have been the better side over the piece. A smattering of boos as Everton trudge off. Nothing super-dramatic, to be clear, a frustrated minority in a quiet stadium. But a smattering nonetheless.

45 min: Rohl shanks a right-wing cross out for a goal kick, and it’s the Coventry fans you can hear as they make fun of the Everton player. The home fans are pretty subdued at the moment. One minute of bonus first-half time to come.

44 min: … and so Torp sends a dipping long-range rake over the wall and towards the bottom left. Pickford deals with it. To be honest, it’s just as well the keeper makes the save, because Dasilva had quite a handful of Dibling’s shirt in the tussle that led to the free kick. Everton would have been livid had that decision led to a goal.

43 min: Coventry win another throw in Everton territory, deep down the left. It’s launched long and only half cleared. Everton can’t get out. Dibling and Dasilva grapple, 25 yards out, and Coventry get the decision. A free kick in a dangerous position, just to the left of centre.

41 min: Rudoni shimmies his way down the inside-right channel before unleashing a low bobbler across Pickford and inches wide of the left-hand corner. Goal kick. Not sure Pickford was getting to that. Everton were on top for a while back there; now it’s all Coventry, and the xG is now 0.28 for Everton to the visitors’ 0.57.

39 min: George and Torp collide in the midfield. Free kick to Coventry, whose player was taken out in mid-air. The resulting free kick is launched towards the Everton box. Tarkowski goes up with Yirenkyi, and gives the Coventry man’s hair a snide tug. He also manages to slap his arm across Pinnock’s startled face. VAR isn’t interested in looking at either part of that challenge; Tarkowski has got away with one two there.

37 min: … and now a long throw into the Everton box from the left causes minor bedlam. The ball drops to Rudoni, on the right-hand corner of the six-yard box … but he can’t sort his feet out to get a proper shot away. The home crowd getting a bit agitated now as Coventry get closer and closer.

36 min: Branthwaite’s poor clearing header is intercepted and sent back into the Everton box down the inside-right channel. Sakamoto shoots from a tight angle. Pickford parries. Coventry growing and growing.

35 min: Yirenkyi, Rudoni and Sakamoto combine well down the right to earn Coventry’s first corner of the evening. Rudoni swings it in. Pickford half-punches, half-slaps the ball clear, doing just enough. The visitors are getting on top.

33 min: Torp slides Awoniyi into acres down the right. He should stride into the box and have a whack, but tries to find Gboho in the middle with a low cross that the back-tracking Dibling cuts out. Torp and Dibling come out of this looking good; the lads either side of their contributions, not so much.

32 min: A bit of space for Torp down the inside-left channel. Hoick! Goal kick! Just beforehand, Sakamoto and Dewsbury-Hall traded misplaced passes. This has got a wee bit scrappy.

30 min: Yirenkyi crosses from the right. The ball’s deflected, so Pickford does extremely well to gather in the middle of a crowded six-yard box.

29 min: … so having said that, Rohl makes good down the right and, had he sent over a better cross, Coventry were in a bit of trouble with some blue shirts lurking. But the ball sails out for a goal kick.

28 min: Sakamoto nearly gets the better of Mykolenko down the right, but the full-back holds his position and draws a needless foul from the Coventry winger. The visitors suddenly looking a little bit more confident.

27 min: Now Dibling plays a loose pass in midfield, and suddenly a little bit of air has been taken out of the HDS. Everton squandering their momentum with some careless play.

25 min: Pickford skulls a dreadful pass upfield that’s easily intercepted. Groans. The keeper’s nearly punished severely when Gboho advances down the inside-left channel and shoots, but the ball sails wide of the left-hand post.

23 min: Everton probe down both flanks, calmly and confidently. No rush. Then suddenly Dibling picks up the pace down the right and shuttles a pass to Barry, just inside the box. Barry floats a cross that’s easy pickings for Rushworth. A disappointing end to a period of Evertonian play that was easy on the eye.

21 min: More space for Dasilva down the left. This time he’s able to whip a cross into the box. Rudoni is in a bit of space, ten yards out, but can’t adjust his body shape to steer a header goalwards. The ball slaps harmlessly wide left. That’s whistled Coventry’s xG all the way up to 0.04. Baby steps.

20 min: Dasilva and Gboho try to get something going for Coventry down the left, but their cute interplay, sprinkled by a spot of juggling, is comprehensively interrupted by Tarkowski. Attack over.

18 min: Everton now top the possession stats, having had 52 percent of the ball overall, which shows how much the momentum has swung in the last 11 minutes. The home side well on top.

16 min: George swings in a cross from the left. Torp attempts to head clear, but under pressure can only send the ball backwards and out for a corner on the left. The set piece is played short, then finally hit deep by Garner; Branthwaite can’t keep the ball in at the far stick. Goal kick. Meanwhile Richard Hirst, of sixth-minute assumptions fame, is back: “I well remember David Moyes’s first game as Everton manager: it was against Fulham and Everton scored in the first minute, going on to win 2-1. I’ve borne a grudge against him ever since, so it was a big effort on my part to send the previous message of support for Everton!”

15 min: This already doesn’t feel like it’s going to be a 0-0. The xG not quite telling that story: it’s currently 0.23 to a big fat zero in Everton’s favour.

13 min: George makes a nuisance of himself down the left and earns a free kick. Dewsbury-Hall swings the set piece into the mixer … at which point pinball-style bedlam erupts. The ball bagatelles in the six-yard box. Armstrong dinks over Rushworth and tries to head home from the closest of ranges … only for Torp to telescope a leg and hook clear. Barry also tries to force home, but Coventry hold firm and then the whistle goes for a handball by the striker. Well that was fun.

11 min: Dewsbury-Hall drags a meek shot wide right. Then Everton come again, Garner going long down the inside-right channel, finding Dibling free … but Dibling, making his first Premier League start of the season, can’t bring the ball down. Both teams are looking lively in attack.

9 min: Everton respond to that minor shock well. Dewsbury-Hall probes down the left; Mfuni closes the door. Pinnock then denies Barry as he probes on the other flank. Finally Mykolenko has a bash from the right-hand edge of the D, and Rushworth parries well.

7 min: Coventry have enjoyed 63 percent of possession so far. They’re looking confident. Yirenkyi drops deep on the left and sends a diagonal into the box hoping to find Awoniyi. Too much height, though the ball then nearly creeps into the bottom right, and it’s not clear Pickford had his bearings there. Goal kick. Inches away from embarrassment.

6 min: Mfuni passes back weakly and nearly lets Barry in. Coventry clear their lines just in time. Other than that, it’s been a quiet start in terms of action; both sets of fans are giving it plenty however. “I think we’re all with Mary Waltz on this one,” assumes Richard Hirst. “So come on Everton, for Mary’s sake; she deserves it, living under the orange one. Sorry, Coventry supporters.”

4 min: In other former-rival news, Awoniyi, erstwhile of Liverpool, tries to make good down the right. He doesn’t get anywhere. He never played a minute for Everton’s city rivals, either.

2 min: Coventry tonight become the 45th different club David Moyes has faced as a Premier League manager. Their fans are currently giving Dewsbury-Hall an ear-splitting what-for as a former Leicester star. The M69 representing.

Everton get the ball rolling. The Hill Dickinson loud. “Not only did the aforementioned David Speedie just miss out on the FA Cup and Charity Shield with Coventry, but also played for Liverpool between their league title in 1990 and FA Cup in 1992,” notes Tim Woods. “He then left Blackburn a little before their league title. Not the luckiest career, but a fondly remembered name for all football fans of that era.”

… so here come the players. No sounding of the usual siren, as a mark of respect on remembrance weekend. Everton in their royal blue, Coventry in second-choice white tops. We’ll be off once the Last Post has sounded. RIP the fallen.

Mr 592 II talks to Sky. “I’m really proud of [equalling Harry Catterick’s record] to be honest … it’s not easy for any manager to manage one club for 592 games … the world we’re in now, managers don’t get that long … so I’ve been really privileged to work under really good people who gave me the opportunity to manage this club … the first time here was a lot longer … this time has been just a couple of years but I'm enjoying it … Coventry are a good side … they won the Championship well … they started slow in the Premier League but they’re back in it now … I want to see if [Tyler Dibling] can do it on the main stage … see more from him … it’s a good opportunity to give him.”

Pre-match postbag: Evertonians Telling It As They See It edition. “Greetings from almost liberated California. The thrashing of a certain ‘orange’ gentleman has allowed me to have zero anxiety about today’s Everton tilt. I’m happy no matter what happens” – Mary Waltz

“I admire the optimism of Everton potentially going into fourth with a five-goal margin of victory today. I watch almost every single game and I struggled to recall the last time they scored five in the league; turns out it was the famous 5-1 win at Brighton in May 2023 that helped them stay up that season. At home, we have to go back to September 2020 when they beat West Brom 5-2 behind closed doors at Goodison. The last time in front of home fans? Their 6-3 win over Bournemouth in February 2017 when Romelu Lukaku scored four. Let’s just say Everton aren’t generally banging in goals for fun” – Matt Burtz

Coventry boss Frank Lampard - who having managed Everton 44 times, sits just 548 games behind the total David Moyes will be hitting tonight – speaks to Sky Sports. “I saw [the Hill Dickinson Stadium] in its early stages … it’s amazing to see the end product … what a stadium for a great football club … good to be here … but it’s more about the game … [Everton] are a strong team … [Coventry] are in a good moment … a good feeling [after the Sunderland win] … we have to show it consistently … reproduce good things … be on our game … [Jack Rudoni] believes in his talent … he’s got a lot of it … he’s said ‘give me the ball and I’ll show you what I can do at this level’ … hopefully that rubs off on other players.”

… and on that subject, kind of, here’s Andrew Beasley. He crunches the data, and explains why a Coventry win tonight would be a huge deal for Frank Lampard and his Sky Blues.

To the excited chatter of playgrounds up and down the country members of a certain jaded generation, this fixture is a re-run of the 1987 General Motors FA Charity Shield. Coventry, who had won the FA Cup just nine weeks previously, took 35,000 fans to Wembley, a record for the season curtain-raiser, while Everton supporters, making their eighth appearance at the grand old pile in four years, were comparatively thin on the ground. But the latter were the ones who got to celebrate, Wayne Clarke scoring the only goal just before the break, while Kevin Ratcliffe popped Cov’s new £750,000 record signing David Speedie in his pocket. All the post-match talk was of Everton retaining their First Division title: they ended the season in fourth; City, hoping to build on their breakthrough cup win, finished tenth. Both clubs were slightly disappointed with those returns back then; they’d deal on them for this season, right here, right now.

David Moyes = Harry Catterick. Well, not quite: Top Catt did win two league titles (1963 and 1970) and Everton’s first FA Cup in 33 years (1966) after all. But in another way: yes! Our man Tom Garry reports.

double quotation markWith David Moyes tonight set to equal Harry Catterick’s all-time record of 592 matches in charge of Everton across his two spells at the club, his colleague, the Everton women’s team head coach Scott Phelan, has spoken of his fond memories of his role as a ball boy on the day of Moyes’ first match at the helm at Goodison Park in 2002. Phelan, now aged 38, is a former Everton academy player and he said: “The opportunity to be a ball boy was a big thing. I think I was 14, 15 years of age. Just seeing the players close up, with the best seat in the house, was something I’ll always remember. The sense of occasion, the atmosphere and [Moyes] starting on a positive footing is what I remember. “What he is about to achieve is really important in his own career but, for this club as well, he’s brought stability in his first and second phase. I have massive respect for him and what he has achieved here. “He’s had an amazing career. Hopefully he can celebrate with a win.”

There’s no Jack Grealish in the Everton squad, by the way. But then did anyone really expect that?

Coventry City are in If It Ain’t Broke mode. They’re unchanged following last weekend’s 3-1 home win over Sunderland. Bobby Thomas is getting back up to speed after a calf issue but is not risked.

Everton are coming off the back of two victories over Newcastle United. They can’t play them every week, but they can near as dammit name the same starting XI, which is what’s happened in the wake of Monday night’s 1-0 win on Tyneside: Tyler Dibling comes in for Jake O’Brien, who drops to the bench.

Everton: Pickford, Rohl, Tarkowski, Branthwaite, Mykolenko, Armstrong, Garner, Dibling, Dewsbury-Hall, George, Barry. Subs: Travers, Maitland-Niles, Keane, O’Brien, Johnson, Norgaard, Alcaraz, Hackney, Graham.

Coventry City: Rushworth, van Ewijk, Mfuni, Pinnock, Dasilva, Yirenkyi, Torp, Sakamoto, Rudoni, Gboho, Awoniyi. Subs: Bentley, Grimes, Simms, Tchaouna, Kesler-Hayden, Latibeaudiere, Thomas-Asante, Hamer, Cherif.

Referee: Stuart Attwell VAR: Timothy Wood

If Everton win this match tonight, they go above Liverpool; if they win it big – five goals big – they leap over Chelsea into fourth spot. Things are going pretty well for David Moyes and his men, who are outperforming expectations that were set miserably low after an underwhelming transfer window. It’s one heck of a turnaround.

But then Coventry City will be pretty pleased with their efforts so far too. After a cold start, they’ve won three of their last five matches, and while nine points and 16th place isn’t exactly the most comfortable state of affairs, there’s been more than enough promise in performances, so spirits remain high as the Sky Blues embark on their first top-flight campaign for a quarter of a century.

Two clubs heading in the right direction, then; two teams and two support bases happy and hopeful. Everton go into this evening’s showdown as favourites, but Coventry have shown plenty of moxie away from home so far, with narrow defeats at Manchester City and Tottenham, plus a win at Nottingham Forest … and they won the last staging of this particular fixture, albeit in the days of Mustapha Hadji and Gary Breen, back on Boxing Day 2000. So they’ll be dreaming of three points too. It could be lots of fun. Kick-off is at 8pm GMT. It’s on!

The New York Knicks have released a statement supporting Taylor Swift's fandom after recent comments from reserve guards Tyler Kolek and Deuce McBride.

"Taylor Swift has been a Knicks fan for many years, has a long history with Madison Square Garden, and we are incredibly appreciative of her support of the organization," a Knicks spokesperson said.

Earlier this month, Kolek called Swift's fandom "a little performative" on an NBA 2K League video. Kolek and McBride were playing a game called "Deflected," where they have to compete in NBA 2K to avoid answering awkward questions. After McBride scored on the game, Kolek had to answer: "Who's the fakest celebrity fan you've seen sitting courtside in an NBA game?"

"At the Knicks games, I would say it's very performative," Kolek said. "Because they just want to be seen at the game; they're not necessarily fans. Maybe Taylor Swift?"

"I don't know if I've seen her at any other games," Kolek said.

"Swifties not gonna like that answer," McBride replied.

"No, I'm gonna get some hate from the Swifties," Kolek acknowledged. "I get she's got a lot going on, so she can't come all the time, but it's a little performative."

This is not the first time Swift's Knicks fandom has been questioned. "She's not a Knicks fan. Get outta here, girl," broadcaster Monica McNutt said in June after Swift sat courtside during Game 4 of the 2026 NBA Finals, which the Knicks won with a historic 29-point comeback. McNutt later apologized for the comments.

Swift was born and raised in Pennsylvania, but has been a Knicks fan since moving to New York over a decade ago. Here's an excerpt from an interview with Time Magazine in 2014:

You recently moved to New York. Are you a Knicks fan now?

I love them, though. Why does that matter? So you run into a bunch of different, interesting types of people at the Met Ball. I've gone the last four years. And the most normal people at that event, every single time, are Amar'e Stoudemire and his wife Alexis. I talk with them and hang with them every time I'm there. So I've always had this sort of love of the Knicks, just because Amar'e is so cool. And also I performed at the Knicks'—at Madison Square Garden's—Kids Talent Competition at halftime when I was 12 or 13. And ever since then I've had this kind of sparkly, magical opinion of Madison Square Garden and the Knicks, since they let me sing when I was a little kid.

Swift and Kansas City Chiefs star Travis Kelce were married at Madison Square Garden during a lavish wedding in July.

Judy Reyes To Debut Passion Project ‘Freestyle: A Love Story’ Theater Play In LA, Starring Justina Machado & James Martinez

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If you grew up in the 80s and early 90s, you may have listened to freestyle music, also known as Latin hip-hop, born after the disco era that dominated the airwaves from New York to Miami, Los Angeles, Texas, as well as Philly and Boston, to name a few areas. Among the genre’s biggest fans was an early-20s Judy Reyes, currently a series regular on ABC’s High Potential, who is pulling double duty on the network, reprising her fan-favorite character Carla Espinosa on the new Scrubs series.

Reyes and her husband George Valencia enjoyed going to clubs to dance to artists like Lisa Lisa and the Cult Jam, Expose, The Cover Girls, TKA, Judy Torres, Marc Anthony, and Brenda K. Starr. It’s worth noting that in 1988, Mariah Carey got her start in the industry, in part, as a backup singer for Starr. Related Stories News 'Scrubs' EP Aseem Batra On Dr. Cox And Carla's Season 2 Presence, Casting Changes, Featuring Fewer Interns In Each Episode & More News 'Scrubs' Revival: Who's Back (& Who's Not); How Many Episodes Judy Reyes, John C. McGinley Are In; How Many Kids J.D. & Elliot, Turk & Carla Have Now

While lamenting how short the freestyle era lasted, Reyes and Valencia had plans for a documentary that they would collaborate on with John Leguizamo. However, Covid put a damper on their plans, and places they pitched lost interest. Watch on Deadline

Full of determination, Reyes and Valencia pivoted to making a theater play. Tonight, Freestyle: A Love Story will make its Los Angeles debut at the Wilshire Ebell Theatre, with Justina Machado and James Martinez in the lead roles, which often rotate from city to city. Valencia is the creator, director, and co-writer, alongside Lidia Ramirez, of the production, with Reyes serving as executive producer alongside fellow thespian Ana Ortiz and Malcolm Lam.

Actors who have led this production elsewhere include Latino power couple Liza Colón-Zayas and David Zayas, EP Ortiz, who most famously starred in Ugly Betty, Luna Lauren Vélez of Dexter fame, Selena and Chicago P.D.‘s Jon Seda, Six Feet Under star Freddy Rodriguez, and Felix Solis, who recently joined the cast of Amazon’s Everyone In My Family Has Killed Someone.

“Justina and James Martinez will star this time around, and we’re really excited. It’s a reunion for them because they played husband and wife on One Day at a Time. It keeps the community strong and tight and motivated and inspired while continuing to tell our stories through the work. This is what we know,” said Reyes.

She added, “Our existence here is an act of political defiance, and the only way that we know how to continue to exist is to tell our stories, to each other and for each other. We’re fixated on that. At this age, while things are literally physically being torn away from us by time, technology, or politics, we’re doing everything we can to hold on to our history. Our history brings joy, and the joy brings it back to the people. So it’s a full circle, and it keeps us together.”

Tonight’s attendees will hear music performed by freestyle legends Torres, whose hit song “Come Into My Arms” is a favorite of Reyes’, and K7 of TKA fame for a completely immersive experience.

“People are encouraged to shout out, call out, laugh, sing along, dance, get up, woot woot, all of it. It’s completely immersive because we have dancing, we have a DJ, we have Judy, we have K, we have music, we have a screen with a little bit of documented history of what freestyle is.”

Proceeds from tonight’s performance of Freestyle: A Love Story will benefit the Sound of GOL Foundation and LA Watford Women Football Club, an all-Latina football team, whom they hope to send to the UK for two weeks of training with a professional football team.

“We want to give back to our community. For LA Watford, the goal is to build a place where they can learn each other’s strengths, learn what’s out there, and what they can bring to the community. We also want to share a little bit of who we are with them. We want them to know that this is for them.”

England squad: Three players who should and shouldn't be in Thomas Tuchel's group - there's no Cole Palmer

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Carter White | 06 Nov 2026 20:52

England manager Thomas Tuchel has named his 25-man squad for the upcoming November international break.

Practically ensured of a top-two finish in their UEFA Nations League group, the Three Lions are looking to leapfrog World Cup winners Spain to book a more favourable quarter-final tie in March.

Next Thursday, England welcome Croatia to Wembley Stadium before making the trip to the Metropolitano in Madrid on October 15, when La Roja will be looking to record back-to-back wins over Tuchel's side.

Here, Sports Mole discusses the German's selection for the two Nations League games, arguing for players who should consider themselves either lucky or unlucky.

A non-playing member of the group that reached the semi-finals of the World Cup in the summer, Jason Steele has been rewarded with back-to-back selections in the actual England squad in this Nations League campaign.

There is no doubting that the Brighton stopper's professionalism and attitude was a net positive for the Three Lions in North America, with the veteran helping Tuchel to nurture a feeling of togetherness.

However, as a clear second-choice option with his club and at the age of 36, there is no plausible route to England minutes for Steele, whose spot could be given to an up-and-coming keeper instead.

This one is controversial. There was serious outrage in September when Morgan Gibbs-White was left out of England's four-game squad, with the attacker's fiance leading the protests on social media.

The Forest sweetheart was the Premier League's most productive Englishman during the second half of last season, yet Tuchel still opted to leave the forward out of the World Cup squad in the summer.

The number-10 role for the Three Lions is already stacked with Jude Bellingham, Morgan Rogers and Eberechi Eze, with Gibbs-White unlikely to become an England regular - at least under Tuchel - until he is playing in the Champions League for an elite European club.

Can we be honest for a moment. By the time Harry Kane retires from international football, Dominic Calvert-Lewin will be playing in Championship, in a similar fashion to what Jamie Vardy is doing right now.

The 29-year-old is a limited international striker who would be more suited to another home nation such as Wales or Scotland, who would be extremely grateful for the player's top-flight experience.

Calvert-Lewin is fortunate that a certain Middlesbrough striker is struggling with injury, as the Leeds marksman would probably be sitting and watching a second-tier attacker in his space next week.

We are living in a world where Eberechi Eze and Gibbs-White are getting senior England call-ups, so it is entirely valid to argue that Everton's Kiernan Dewsbury-Hall should also get a crack at the Three Lions' whip.

The elephant in the room is that the former Chelsea man is playing for one of the poorest attacking outfits in the Premier League, with David Moyes's side really dimming the shine of the 28-year-old.

However, Dewsbury-Hall has still been impressive in recent top-flight matches for the Toffees and must be wondering what more he must do to receive a positive phone call from the England boss.

Nico O'Reilly has not set the world alight since the last international break, but the youngster is playing for an elite club team in Manchester City and regularly starts in the Premier League and Champions League.

With the ability to play left-back and in midfield, the 21-year-old is a versatile option for England and will feel unlucky to miss out this month, especially given the declining performances of Lewis Hall with Newcastle United.

Yet to reach anywhere near the prime years of his senior career, O'Reilly will surely not shed too many tears over Tuchel's November decision, targeting a comeback for the Nations League quarter-finals in March.

Netting 13 goals in his first 13 league appearances of the season, Will Lankshear is putting up Kane-like numbers in the Championship, striking fear into second-tier defences across the land.

It would be an eyebrow-raising decision for Tuchel to call upon a second-division player, however, the Boro striker's age profile and instinctual finishing ability would warrant a place in the squad.

Unfortunately, Lankshear picked up a slight knock in Middlesbrough's 3-0 success over Charlton Athletic on October 31, subsequently missing the defeat by Wrexham on Wednesday night.

FC Koln vs Bayer Leverkusen - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Lewis Nolan | 06 Nov 2026 20:50

Bayer Leverkusen travel to FC Koln's RheinEnergieSTADION on Sunday and will hope to avoid falling behind in the race for a spot in the Bundesliga's top four.

The hosts are 14th in the table with nine points from a possible 24, whereas Leverkusen are in sixth place with 13 points following their 4-2 loss against Stuttgart on October 31.

Koln came from behind to draw 1-1 with Union Berlin on November 1, extending their unbeaten record in all competitions to three games (W1, D2).

Rene Wagner's side are only two points above Union Berlin in the 16th-placed relegation playoff spot, while only three points separate them from 17th-placed Schalke 04.

The visitors are winless in their last three Bundesliga fixtures, losing twice in that time, and while they only conceded two goals in those matches, they scored just once.

A victory for the hosts would end a streak of two consecutive losses against Leverkusen, and it would be just the club's first success in six encounters with the visitors.

The Billy Goats come into Sunday's meeting undefeated in their five home contests this campaign, winning on three occasions.

Leverkusen produced arguably their worst defensive display of the season against Stuttgart, conceding goals from set pieces, counter-attacks and from poor marking in midfield.

Boss Carles Martinez has now overseen two losses from his last two games in the Bundesliga, and his team's 1-0 defeat in the Europa League on Thursday against Marseille means he has lost three of his past four matches.

Die Werkself had won seven and drawn one of the eight fixtures prior to those four contests, but while they are only two points from fourth-placed RB Leipzig, they could end the weekend five points from the top four.

Leverkusen were beaten 3-0 by Hoffenheim on October 25 in their latest away outing, whereas they had won three and drawn one of their previous four away clashes.

Martinez's side kept two clean sheets in their three most recent away trips, and they have netted at least two goals in six of their seven matches on the road this season.

Bayer Leverkusen form (all competitions):

Koln may not have any other choice but to start a three-man defence of Joel Schmied, Jahmai Simpson-Pusey and Tom Krauss due to the injury that Rav van den Berg is dealing with.

Ellyes Skhiri has been sidelined due to an ankle issue, meaning Paul Okon-Engstler is set to start at the base of a midfield three.

As for Leverkusen, the continued absences of Martin Terrier, Eliesse Ben Seghir, Christian Kofane and Nathan Tella means the visitors have limited options up front.

Striker Patrik Schick is certain to feature, and he could be supported by Lucas Vazquez, Ibrahim Maza and Afonso Moreira.

Edmond Tapsoba will be expected to lead the backline alongside Facundo Medina, while the duo are likely to appear behind Aleix Garcia and Ezequiel Fernandez.

Schwabe; Schmied, Simpson-Pusey, Krauss; Sebulonsen, Maina, Okon-Engstler, Moore, Sosa; Bulter, El Mala

Bayer Leverkusen possible starting lineup:

Flekken; Quansah, Tapsoba, Medina, Gutierrez; Garcia, Fernandez; Vazquez, Maza, Moreira; Schick

Leverkusen's form of late has been concerning, though they have been difficult to beat when playing on the road.

Koln have been strong at RheinEnergieSTADION, and while they may struggle to get the better of the visitors, they should have enough to earn a point.

WNBA Stars Paige Bueckers, Azzi Fudd Confirm Breakup

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Dallas Wings’ teammates Paige Bueckers and Azzi Fudd confirmed they broke up “over a year ago” in the WNBA rookie’s podcast Fudd Around and Find Out.

Paige Bueckers and Azzi Fudd pulled a pass fake on WNBA fans.

The Dallas Wings teammates—who confirmed their relationship in July 2025—shared they are no longer together, and haven’t been for over a year.

Welcoming Bueckers to the Nov. 6 episode of her Fudd Around and Find Out podcast, Fudd confirmed the news as the former couple laughed it off, quipping, “I don’t know how else to say that.”

Bueckers also confirmed that the duo—who first sparked romance rumors during their time playing basketball together at University of Connecticut—had been broken up for “over a year.”

In fact, it was well before they knew they would be stepping onto the court together.

“To share that now everybody’s gonna be like, ‘Oh my gosh they’re teammates. How is this going to work?’” Bueckers explained. “We were not together before she got drafted so all those narratives can go to bed. She was not drafted because she was my girlfriend. She was drafted because she deserved it.”

The 25-year-old emphasized that she and Fudd—who was drafted in April—remain friends.

“She’s an amazing human, an amazing basketball player,” she continued. “We’re still friends, we’re still cool. We broke up over a year ago, so we played an entire season together. We’re the utmost professional. We care about basketball.”

Bueckers also acknowledged that she wanted to give a “speech” regarding the fan’s obsession with their romance since it was confirmed.

“There’s a whole list and notes of things that I would love to address if I had the time or the energy but I think and Azzi can agree to this just to start by saying we don’t stand for hate of any kind,” the guard continued. “We don’t condone that. If you’re using our names and our image to spread that, that’s not who we are and that’s not what we’re about.”

Nodding to the negative messages Bueckers and Fudd’s family, friends and teammates received during their relationship, Bueckers acknowledged, “That’s never been something that we’ve stood for.”

Ultimately, both Bueckers and Fudd, 23, made it clear they only support each other—and only want support for each other from their fans.

“We’re still going to be great teammates,” Bueckers continued. “We’re going to be professionals. We are still going to have other friends. We will eventually move on.”

She also didn’t want any hate for their potential future partners joking, “I know we will continue to be outside and be lit and have a good time so we should not get punished for that as well.”

Fudd, for her part, concurred with everything Bueckers laid out, and confirmed they won’t be speaking any further on their relationship.

“We know you guys are probably curious and have a ton of questions,” she acknowledged, “but we’ve addressed everything that we need to and the rest will stay with us.”

For a deeper look into the love lives of WNBA stars, keep reading…

"I think the next time I'll post I'm in a relationship is when I'm married," WNBA standout Angel Reese shared during a January 2025 episode of her Unapologetically Angel podcast, explaining a newfound desire for privacy. "You might see me on a date. You gonna have to catch me though. Like, I’m not gonna post a man on my page."

Still, there have been definitive clues that the Bayou Barbie has found her Ken in Orlando Magic center Wendell Carter Jr. "That’s a sign," the Atlanta Dream power forward captioned a TikTok showcasing an impressive arrangements of roses, "that’s a signnnn." And it was certainly a big play when the Duke alum turned up to the 2025 WNBA All-Star game sporting a t-shirt baring Reese's visage.

The NCAA champ—set to appear on season two of The Hunting Wives—returned the favor, attending a December 2025 Magic game in a jersey emblazoned with the words "My Man."

Game recognizes game, and three-time WNBA champion A'ja Wilson has found her ace of hearts in Bam Adebayo.

The low-key couple first sparked dating rumors in the summer of 2024, and Wilson confirmed their romance with a bit of a pump fake when she showed up at NBA All Star Weekend in February 2025.

"My NBA All-Star is not here," the Las Vegas Aces center told People, acknowledging that the Miami Heat center hadn't made the roster. But Adebayo was busy "getting the rest he needs," she added, "and that's the best Valentine gift that I can truly give him, is just the rest."

And they're only heating up, Adebayo showing up to support Wilson—who made her fifth straight All-Star team—during the WNBA All-Star festivities in July 2025.

Months later, in her TIME Athlete of the Year cover story, she revealed they'd actually been huddled up way longer than fans realized, making that first move at the Tokyo Olympics in 2021.

Next up: Adding to their squad. "That is always a dream," she said of having kids with Adebayo. "This is my life partner. Honestly, what on earth was my world before you? That’s how much he’s impacted my life, my family’s life."

Sabrina Ionescu has racked up an impressive haul of trophies playing for the New York Liberty, WNBA All-Stars and U.S. Olympic team, but the day she married NFL player Hroniss Grasu is especially close to her heart.

"March 10, 2024. The day I got to marry my best friend, my other half," she captioned her Instagram wedding photo. "The best day of my life."

Marta Xargay Casademont & Breanna Stewart

Three-time WNBA champion Breanna Stewart met her love match with Spanish basketball player Marta Xargay Casademont. They connected as teammates on a Russian professional women's basketball team in 2018 and got engaged and married in 2021. They welcomed their first child Ruby in August 2021, followed by their son Theo in October 2023.

Xargay Casademont retired from the sport in 2021, but continues to support her wife's career, writing on Instagram after Stewart's New York Liberty won the 2024 Finals, "So freaking proud of you!"

Indiana Fever megastar Caitlin Clark has been dating basketball player and former Butler University assistant coach Connor McCaffery since April 2023.

The pair haven't been shy about celebrating their love on social media, with McCaffery posting a sweet birthday tribute for his girlfriend's January 2025 birthday: "You have an incredible ability to inspire and lift up those around you, and I am lucky to learn from you every day. Thank you for always being you. I love you."

New York Liberty shooting guard and 2021 WNBA All-Star Betnijah Laney is married to fellow basketball player Jordan Hamilton, who started for the Denver Nuggets, New Orleans Pelicans, and Houston Rockets during his NBA career.

In an Instagram tribute for their second wedding anniversary in 2023, Laney wrote, "Your arms are my favorite place to be."

After dating for eight years, Diana Taurasi married her Phoenix Mercury teammate Penny Taylor on May 13, 2017. Now both retired from the sport, the iconic WNBA couple have more time to spend with their children, Leo and Isla.

After a few weeks of speculation, Dallas Wings point guard Paige Bueckers confirmed in July 2025 that she was dating Azzi Fudd, her former teammate on the UConn’s women’s basketball team.

And with Fudd selected first overall by the Wings in the 2026 draft, the two were thrilled to pair up on and off the hardwood. "I'm excited to play again with Paige," Fudd told ESPN during draft night April 13. "She's an incredible person. An incredible player, and it's gonna be a lot of fun."

In November 2026, though, the couple confirmed they had broken up "over a year" prior.

"We’re still friends, we’re still cool," Bueckers explained during an appearance on Fudd's Fudd Around and Find Out podcast. "We broke up over a year ago, so we played an entire season together. We’re the utmost professional. We care about basketball.”

Prior to playing for the Indiana Fever in 2024 and re-joining the Seattle Storm in February 2025, Katie Lou Samuelson was busy with her life off the court. She married basketball player Devin Cannady in April 2023 and the couple announced the birth of their daugher Aliya in August 2023.

WNBA superstar Napheesa Collier can add devoted wife and mom to her long list of achievements (which includes two Olympic gold medals and 2025 All-Star Game MVP).

In October 2019, the Minnesota Lynx forward got engaged to basketball skills coach Alex Bazzell and they were married three years later. In 2022, they welcomed daughter Mila Sarah Bazzell.

Then-Chicago Sky teammates Allie Quigley and Courtney Vandersloot were married in December 2018 and share daughter Jana, who was born on April 8, 2025.

The winning pair are not only the first married couple to win a professional sports championship together (the 2021 WNBA Finals), but in May 2025, a month before Quigley announced her retirement, Vandersloot surpassed her wife's record as the all-time leading scorer for the Chicago Sky.

In March 2021, Alyssa Thomas shared on social media that she was dating her then-Connecticut Sun teammate, DeWanna Bonner.

The pair got engaged in 2023 when Thomas proposed during the WNBA All-Star Weekend. Bonner is also mom to Cali and Demi, the twins she welcomed in 2017 with ex-wife and former Phoenix Mercury teammate Candice Dupree.

Josh O’Connor Should Be James Bond

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Challengers star would bring a new energy—and a new brand of modern masculinity—to the world’s most famous spy.

WHEN THE NAMES of the rumored final contenders to play the next James Bond were floated in the press a couple of weeks ago—seemingly Callum Turner, Jack Lowden, Josh O’Connor, and a mystery third candidate, according to Deadline—I heard a lot of the same sentiment afterward. In more or less words, a lot of people seemed to say “I love Josh O’Connor, but he shouldn’t be Bond.” When I dropped the link into a group chat full of people who aren’t inhaling movie news like I am, there was another probably common response: “I don’t know who that guy is, but he doesn’t look like Bond.”

To me, that sentiment is exactly why O’Connor is the best—and, in some ways, the only—choice to be the next James Bond.

For those not familiar, O’Connor, a 36-year-old Brit who grew up in Cheltenham, has been a working actor for more than a decade, but his star has only really begun to shine in the 2020s. He won an Emmy for playing Prince Charles in The Crown, and more recently has made splashes in movies like the thrilling and sexy sports film Challengers, Rian Johnson’s third Knives Out film Wake Up Dead Man (which, ironically, he appeared in alongside previous Bond Daniel Craig), and this summer’s Steven Spielberg blockbuster Disclosure Day.

It’s not hard to understand why some people’s minds might naturally push against the idea of O’Connor as Bond. Where 007 is typically a classically handsome, devilishly smooth gentleman who almost looks like a video game simulation—seriously, look back at Sean Connery and Pierce Brosnan for just a second—O’Connor is a bit off-kilter. His hair is wavy; his ears are prominent; his smile is asymmetrical. There’s no beating around the bush—he’s been compared more often to Linguini from Pixar’s Ratatouille than the idea most people have of what a James Bond should be.

But it’s through those eccentricities that O’Connor shines. If you take the time to watch his performances, you’ll find the constant image of someone who brings an undeniable charisma to every project, and somehow manages to mold that into something different each time. In Challengers, O’Connor is the kind of guy you want to root against—he’s sleazy, not a particularly good friend, and unrefined. But he brings the perfect bit of steamy, compelling energy to a love triangle that wouldn’t be the same without him. Co-stars Mike Faist and Zendaya are deserving of credit (as are composers Trent Reznor and Atticus Ross), but O’Connor’s charm is what makes the whole movie work.

He pulls a similar magic trick with just about every other performance. In Wake Up Dead Man, he plays a former boxer-turned-priest who once accidentally killed a man in the ring. Throughout the course of the film, O’Connor goes from someone who wrestles with guilt, shame, and intrigue to someone who can believably talk someone struggling with grief off a ledge—all while both trying to solve a murder and possibly being suspected of that same murder at the same time. In Kelly Reichardt’s The Mastermind, O’Connor plays a less-than-competent, hardly aspirational art thief who stumbles his way through charged political times. Despite this, O’Connor is so magnetic on screen that it’s just about impossible not to like him.

But it’s not just the way he elevates sleazeballs and losers into undeniably watchable men (although that doesn’t hurt). O’Connor has shown a propensity to play traditionally enchanting as well. In La Chimera, from Italian filmmaker Alice Rohrwacher, he’s cool, sleek, and morally gray in a movie that may be the blueprint for what his Bond could potentially look like. He’s got a likable way about him that’s different from some of the manly men (like Connery), sleek operators (like Brosnan), or rugged action stars (like Craig) who’ve played the role before; His charm is distinctly more modern, oozing from nothing other than being a guy who knows how to talk to people, and do it really well.

O’Connor’s quirks and oddities, may, at first glance, be what seems to make him an unfit choice to play Bond. But the key is to look at O’Connor’s unique look and gifts as a feature rather than a bug. People might say Bond needs to look this way, and sound that way, and otherwise it doesn’t work. But for anyone who feels so strongly about that, there’s good news: There are 25 other canonical James Bond movies (plus 1967’s Casino Royale and 1983’s Never Say Never Again) where he almost always looks and behaves just like that. When better than now to try something new? Daniel Craig’s blond hair and untraditional look were also questioned at first, and it’s safe to say that risk turned out pretty well in the end.

The forthcoming Bond movie has Denis Villeneuve signed on as director, and Villeneuve has pretty much never failed when it comes to casting his movies. Can you imagine anyone other than Timothée Chalamet as Paul Atreides in his Dune movies? It seems like an obvious pick now, but the 30-year-old Oscar nominee had only just begun to break out when he was cast in the role back in 2018. Villeneuve has a clear eye for talent, and so it won’t be much of a stretch to trust in whoever ultimately lands the job—but it’s hard to not be excited about a director of his caliber linking up with an actor as ambitious and high-reaching as O’Connor.

Plus, it’s worth noting that the Bond franchise isn’t quite like other mega-series out there. Since the Craig era began with 2006’s Casino Royale, Bond movies have come out on average every three or four years. That’s spread out enough for O’Connor to continue taking on fun, exciting projects even outside of the role as everyone’s favorite super spy.

The other choices aren’t bad. Callum Turner gets a bad rap after his appearance in One Night Only, but he’s shown promise in projects like Green Room and Masters of the Air, and Apple TV’s new Neuromancer looks good. Jack Lowden has only played one of television’s most beloved spies for six seasons on Slow Horses—the fit would be seamless. Whoever Villeneuve picks (even if it’s the unnamed mystery actor) will be afforded a huge amount of goodwill.

But isn’t the fact that O’Connor elicits such a reaction—consistently in his on-screen roles and through the mere idea of him becoming James Bond—proof that he’s the right pick?

Leeds United and Newcastle United are reportedly preparing to step up their pursuit of Coventry City midfielder Jack Rudoni ahead of the upcoming transfer window.

Donovan Mitchell needs to shoot more, not less

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If you've been online recently, you may have noticed that Donovan Mitchell has… pissed off a lot of Cleveland Cavaliers fans.

Twitter is ablaze with criticism. People want him traded. Some regret that he was ever traded for. Mitchell has become the crux of all problems; the shot-chucking 'dictator' who will never be the primary option on a championship team.

But everyone has it backwards; Donovan Mitchell needs to shoot more.

Mitchell's field goal attempts and usage rate are at career lows to start the year.

His assist percentage and shot chart are nearly identical to previous seasons.

That means Mitchell has less control over the offense than ever, despite some insisting that he's grown into a ball-dominant tyrant.

How did we get here? And why are fans feeling such a disconnect?

Mitchell has always been a scorer more than a passer. He currently ranks in the 41st percentile in assist-to-usage ratio — a stat that tracks how often a player is ending their possessions with an assist rather than a shot attempt or turnover.

This means that roughly half of the league's players are more likely to get an assist on any given possession than Donovan Mitchell. That can lead to games where it feels like Mitchell is hogging the ball while leaving his teammates hanging.

That gung-ho play style caused some frustration throughout last year's playoffs, and the complaints have carried into this year.

Fans want to know why Evan Mobley and Jarrett Allen aren't getting the ball. Why do their leads disappear so often? And why haven't the Cavs gotten over the hump despite a roaring regular season?

Some have identified Mitchell's lack of playmaking as the problem.

Which, in fairness, is certainly a factor. Cleveland has lost big games in the past due to Mitchell's shot selection. He doesn't always make the right decision with the ball in his hands — and that can be difficult to watch as a fan.

But we should reframe this discussion. Let's start here.

Donovan Mitchell isn't a point guard. And maybe, he doesn't have to be.

Shai-Gilgeous Alexander was in the 29th percentile for assist-to-usage ratio when the Oklahoma City Thunder won the championship. He's in the 0th percentile now.

The truth is, championship-caliber players are allowed to have weak points. You are not disqualified if you can't do everything on your own.

Giannis Antetokounmpo never learned how to shoot. Nikola Jokic never became an elite defender. And the New York Knicks didn't win a championship last June because Jalen Brunson suddenly became an elite playmaker — they won because they delegated more playmaking responsibilities to Karl-Anthony Towns — leaving space for Brunson to do what Brunson does best… score the ball.

All of those teams identified their star's weakness, and built a roster around them that could insulate them.

The Cavs need a similar solution. They need someone who can take playmaking off of Mitchell's plate.

Darius Garland was the original fit. And, for a while, he would thread the needle beautifully. He sent the defense scattering into motion while Mitchell would catch the ball against a defense already on their back feet. That dynamic led to 64 wins — but unfortunately, Garland couldn't stay healthy.

James Harden was the next option. He's been more available than Garland, which was enough to help bolster the team to an Eastern Conference Finals run.

But Harden's style differs from Garland's. He's slower-paced, favoring a heliocentric style that inhibits stationary spacing around him. The result is fewer chances for Mitchell to attack in motion, nerfing his greatest strength as a slasher who can attack gaps.

All of this has culminated in the Cavs (and fans online) asking Mitchell to be more of a playmaker. But that is a fundamental misunderstanding of who he is — and how you win in the NBA by playing to your strengths.

I want to be clear that I am not advocating for Mitchell to go full Dion Waiters and ignore the other four Cavaliers sharing the court with him at any given time. He still needs to pass the ball — and he needs to choose the right moments to assert himself as a scorer.

You can't win by playing like a bull in a China shop. There has to be a delicate balance between scoring in bunches and releasing control of the offense to someone else. That's the key here.

Mitchell will never be a point guard. Any system that asks him to make reads at that level will learn the hard way. You can't force a square peg into a round hole.

You can, however, build a competitive team that understands who Mitchell is.

We know for a fact that Mitchell can go nuclear as a scorer in the playoffs. We've seen it multiple times.

But who can grab the reins and facilitate those high-scoring performances? Who is the Karl-Anthony Towns to his Jalen Brunson in this scenario?

The Cavs are still hoping that will be Harden. Additional support from Mobley would be welcomed. Whether that will be enough is certainly worth questioning.

But here's another cold, hard truth: Making it to the top of the mountain is hard. If we're being honest, it's damn near impossible. This franchise had the greatest player alive for 11 seasons and only made it to the finish line once.

Cleveland would need a myriad of miracles to win a championship with this group. Even if everything went right, they could still fall short. That's just the way it goes.

That's why sticking true to your identity is more important than anything. Being a bad version of someone else will always be inferior to being the best version of yourself.

I don't think Mitchell will lead Cleveland to a title through his playmaking. That feels like jumping the shark.

The Cavs do need him to be better defensively and to make better reads offensively, but that doesn't necessarily mean relinquishing his role as one of the highest-volume scorers in the league. That's who he is — that's who the team traded for.

Mitchell needs to be a world-class scorer. Nearly all of his attention on offense should be centered on getting his shot. Passing will always come second. Winning or losing comes down to Mitchell taking and making the big shots. That was the primary difference between him and Brunson last season.

Of course, any team would prefer a superstar who is capable of blending elite scoring with masterful playmaking and impactful defense. But, you work with what you have — and the Cavs have a dynamic scorer, not a floor general.

Accepting Mitchell's true nature and having the supporting cast insulate him with their playmaking is the most realistic path forward. It's the only path forward.
