Oldham Athletic vs Sheffield Wednesday - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

By Sam Varley | 06 Nov 2026 20:20, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 20:43

Dave Challinor will take charge of his first match as Sheffield Wednesday manager on Sunday in an FA Cup first-round tie away at Oldham Athletic.

Both teams enter at this stage, with the visitors returning to first-round action after suffering relegation to League One last season.

Oldham Athletic head into the weekend aiming to pull off a major upset at Boundary Park and advance to the FA Cup second round.

They have progressed past this stage in the last two seasons, beating Northampton Town in last year's first round before bowing out in a home loss to MK Dons.

On the league front, Micky Mellon are underway in a second season in League Two after National League promotion, currently sitting 18th following a 10th-placed finish last time around.

From their 14 games thus far, the Latics have managed 15 points, winning four and losing seven of those while scoring just 15 goals, but one of those wins did come last time out on Tuesday as they beat Northampton 1-0 away from home with Oliver Norburn scoring the only goal.

Yet to win consecutive league games this season, Oldham will now bid to build on that midweek victory and make a statement with a big home cup win at the weekend to book a second-round spot.

Their visitors, meanwhile, will begin under new management with Sunday's cup tie in their first season back entering in the first round.

Sheffield Wednesday dropped to League One last time around in a season to forget, ending on zero points having suffered deductions and administration before facing a fresh start under new ownership in League One.

The Owls initially enjoyed a quick rebound and a fast start in the third tier, but a dip in form in October left the club hierarchy unsatisfied and saw them dismiss Henrik Pedersen.

In temporary care following that exit, Matt Hamshaw was unable to inspire a turn in their fortunes, overseen a pair of draws to see a winless streak stretched to four matches having most recently led Blackpool through Davis Keillor-Dunn and been reduced to 10 men before being pegged back by a Jerry Yates equaliser.

With newly-appointed Challinor in attendance for that game, which saw his new team sit ninth but just two points off the top six, he will now hope to get off to a winning start and begin an upturn in form for Sheffield Wednesday.

Sheffield Wednesday form (all competitions):

Oldham Athletic will remain without Kai Payne and Kane Drummond, who continue to serve red-card suspensions on Sunday; the club lost appeals for both players after they were sent off in a defeat last weekend.

Elsewhere, Ryan Delaney, Keenan Appiah-Forson and Jack Stevens are still confined to the treatment room.

They may be unchanged from the midweek win, with scorer Norburn a key man in midfield while Calum Kavanagh and Mike Fondop are danger men in attack.

Challinor inherits a much-improved and deeper Sheffield Wednesday squad in 2026-27 but one hit by injuries with Joe Lumley, Liam Cooper, Tyler Onyango, Louie Barry, Billy Mitchell, Reece James and Ali Al-Hamadi among those sidelined, while Di'Shon Bernard is unlikely to be deemed fit to start.

Sil Swinkels will also miss out after his red card against Blackpool last week, while Jamal Lowe is back in contention after his suspension.

They may make some rotations to afford rests for the likes of Barry Bannan and Yan Valery, with Harry Gray, Kieran Morrison and Jordi Liongola all hoping to come in while Gabriel Otegbayo and Ricardo Santos will both compete to take Swinkels's spot at the back.

Oldham Athletic possible starting lineup:

Hudson; Sutton, Daniels, Monthe; Scott-Morriss, Norburn, Pett, Robson; Chilvers; Kavanagh, Fondop

Sheffield Wednesday possible starting lineup:

Maynard-Brewer; Palmer, Santos, Weaver; Fusire, Heskey, Slattery, Morrison; Burstow, Lowe, Gray

Oldham Athletic certainly have the tools to cause problems for Sheffield Wednesday, particularly considering the squad issues faced by the visitors, but we back Challinor to get more out of a squad with plenty of quality still available and see the Owls progressing at the weekend.

The Fifa hopeful hoping to take Infantino down with 'samurai spirit'

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Kozo Tashima is under no illusions it will be easy to take down Gianni Infantino - but the first serious challenger for the Fifa presidency says he will draw on his "samurai spirit".

First, the 68-year-old needs to get the endorsement of five member associations around the world.

The honorary president of the Japan Football Association said he has had "good replies" from those he has contacted since announcing his ambition to run in the March elections.

If he achieves the necessary endorsements, the next step is an altogether different proposition - toppling Infantino, who has been in charge of Fifa for a decade and told BBC Sport last month he believed he had the backing of the "vast, vast majority" of world football.

Tashima, who sits on the Fifa Council, acknowledged the scale of the challenge, but wants to bring "good change" to the organisation.

The former footballer says he is driven by disappointment in Infantino's recent actions, surrounding the cancelled suspension of United States forward Folarin Balogun at the World Cup and the abandoned plan to sell stakes in the World Cup to private investors.

In an exclusive interview with BBC Sport, he spoke of why he feels he cannot leave Fifa as it is.

"I know [Infantino] has a lot of supporters," he said. "Now I am alone and my budget is not enough but it is OK. My policy, my philosophy, I have to fight against him with my samurai spirit.

"I would like to make sure that the game of football is safeguarded and to be loved and enjoyed by everybody. We have to review the system in Fifa and build good governance.

"In the uncertain situation of the world, sports - especially football - can present good values like fair play, co-operation, hard work, integrity, and show the model of the society. So, first to review and rebuild good governance and make football accessible for all with the principle of football first."

Japan's Tashima to stand against Infantino for Fifa presidency

Fifa to share 'key documents' over World Cup sell-off plan

Infantino says he is backed by 'vast majority'

Asked how confident he was in his ability to get the five endorsements, he said: "I have confidence. I already asked to some of the member association and [received] good replies so far."

He laughed when asked if he realistically hopes to win the election in Morocco in March, before saying: "Of course, of course yes, I hope I will win. It is not easy."

Tashima's campaign will centre on his philosophy for the role of Fifa president, which he described as one based on the "values of sincerity, soundness, fair play, and integrity".

Regarding the Balogun red-card incident, which involved US President Donald Trump calling Infantino to ask for the ban to be reviewed, Tashima said: "I have never seen the red card and the next game who can play.

"This is, I hope, never happens in the world football. He didn't explain about this issue, how to come and how [it came] to happen.

He added: "Most of the things are not answered yet by Mr President Infantino.

"With my experience in Japan Football Association, Asian Football Confederation, and Fifa, for some more than 20 years, I would like to bring good change if I am elected."

Last month, former Tottenham Hotspur defender Ramon Vega announced his intention to stand as a candidate in next year's Fifa presidential election.

He told BBC Sport he had received "more than five" endorsements, but declined to give details of the identities of the member associations backing him.

All candidates must prove they have played an "active role" in football for two of the last five years. Vega has said he is confident he is eligible to stand for the presidency as a shareholder of a club.

Vega has attempted to run for the presidency three times before but each time has failed to garner the necessary support of five associations.

Tashima can therefore be considered the first serious challenger to Infantino.

Why Omarion Miller's breakout game for ASU could come against former team

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Towards the end of practice on Nov. 3, right after Arizona State senior wide receiver Omarion Miller finished talking to local reporters, there was a fun moment between Miller and his teammate, senior safety Adrian "Boogie" Wilson. Wilson is one of the more lively characters on the roster, and this moment was an example of that.

Pretending to be a reporter himself, Wilson began to ask Miller questions ahead of this weekend's home game against Colorado on Nov. 7. The first one was in jest, with Wilson asking, 'Who is your favorite teammate?' Miller responded, 'Probably Boogie Wilson.' Then Wilson got into the meat of his questions.

"'What're you most excited for playing your former team?'"

The answer was short but came with conviction. Miller is set to go against the same team he suited up for from 2023 to 2025.

While Miller did his best not to provide Colorado with bulletin board material, it's only natural to feel more pressure going against your former team.

"You're going to feel something, right?" coach Kenny Dillingham said. "People are going to take that and potentially twist that. They twist everything. But you can't say no. But you can't say yes. Every person is going to handle things different.

"I know he's been the same guy this week for us, same attitude, same everything. You wouldn't know the difference. But there has to be something in your head, right? That's human nature."

Miller still has a bunch of friends that he keeps in contact with in Boulder, but not for this week. It's a place where Miller saw himself grow up, maturing into a leader for younger players in his time with the Buffaloes.

"Man, I ain't gonna lie, this week I'm pretty excited going against my old team," Miller said. "A lot of emotions going on right now, for sure. I played there for three years, going against my former team; those were my guys. Anxious to get on the field and ball."

It's been an up-and-down season for Miller, who started off with eight receptions for 102 yards and one touchdown in the season opener — but that was against lower-level competition, FCS Morgan State. Miller, a priority target in the transfer portal for ASU, recorded five receptions for 85 yards and one touchdown against Baylor, although that came in a 55-19 blowout loss, one of the worst of the Dillingham era.

Miller just broke a three-game spell without a touchdown by hauling in a 20-yard score to cut the lead to 17-10 against BYU on Oct. 31. The senior wide receiver feels like he's on the verge of a breakout game — "Most definitely," he said with a smile — and so do his coaches.

"It's close," Dillingham said. "Last week there were multiple opportunities that (if) one of those balls is caught, that's his breakout game. There it is, just one of those four or five other opportunities. It's going to happen. It's just a matter of when, because we're all around it, we're dancing with it."

Heading into the season, Miller and fellow transfer Reed Harris, coming over from Boston College, knew it was unlikely they were both going to have huge seasons.

So far, it's been Harris (48 receptions for 804 yards and eight touchdowns) who has broken out and is gaining traction as a first-round NFL draft pick.

"Coming here, both of us knew we both couldn't be the guys," Miller said. "So we knew one of us was gonna have to be lower than the other one."

Scouting report: How Arizona State football and Jake Fette can beat Colorado

Many thought it would be Miller, who was ranked No. 2 in the transfer portal among wide receivers. But Miller has taken it all in stride and is happy for his teammate.

Miller said that probably wouldn't have been the case a few years ago.

"As a young guy, I probably would not be happy," Miller said. "That's part of learning as a college player. When it's all said and done, we're a team. I'm always happy for any one of my teammates. Whether they're doing good or bad, I stay motivated now, and they stay motivating me. We stay on each other so we can all do our best."

Defenses are starting to zero in on Harris, with BYU sending a safety at Harris at the start of each snap for most of the game.

Miller is hoping to use that to his advantage.

"They just want to double Reed," Miller said. "They forgot I'm still on the team."

It's been a quarterback carousel for ASU this season, with freshman Jake Fette now helming the offense after sophomore Cutter Boley began the year as the starter and then was replaced by senior Mikey Keene. A knee injury to Keene paved the path for Fette.

While it's early on, Miller can feel the connection between him and Fette growing.

"It's coming along really good," Miller said. "I feel like Jake knows the skill set of our receivers. I always tell him, 'Throw it in the air, and we gonna make you look good.'"

Miller's on the cusp of showing why the Sun Devils targeted him so quickly in the transfer portal, and it may poetically come against the team he left for ASU.

Logan Stanley is a sports reporter with The Arizona Republic who primarily focuses on high school, college and Olympic sports. Jesus makes seven changes in first Portugal squad since Ronaldo’s retirement

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Jorge Jesus has announced his first Portugal squad since Cristiano Ronaldo's retirement from international football, with seven changes made to their Nations League party.

Ronaldo brought the curtain down on his glittering international career earlier this week, leaving as the leading scorer and appearance-maker in the history of men's internationals.

The 41-year-old scored 146 goals in 234 games after making his debut for Portugal in 2003, while also captaining the team to their first-ever major trophy at Euro 2016.

He also lifted the Nations League trophy twice in 2019 and 2025, and after Portugal's round-of-16 exit at this year's World Cup, many expected Ronaldo to hang up his boots.

Instead, he was convinced to stay on by the FPF (Portuguese Football Federation), only to leave the Portugal camp during the last international break after being left on the bench during a win over Norway in the Nations League.

Ronaldo was subsequently given a 30-day suspension by the FPF, but ahead of this month's meetings with Denmark and Norway, he decided to walk away from the national team, with FPF president Pedro Proenca promising to celebrate Ronaldo's contribution in the future.

"No tribute will ever be enough to thank him for everything he has done for Portuguese football, for Portugal," Proenca said before the squad announcement on Friday.

"We will do everything we can to honour the legacy he leaves us. Everything he has ‌done deserves to be honoured and celebrated."

Os convocados para os próximos desafios da Liga das Nações 2026/27 #NationsLeague https://t.co/ANBq1844PQ

— Portugal (@selecaoportugal) November 6, 2026

Jesus has made seven changes to his Nations League squad, with Alberto Costa, Antonio Silva, Matheus Fernandes, Carlos Forbs, Geovany Quenda, Rodrigo Mora and Gustavo Varela all included in his 26-man group.

Francisco Conceicao misses out due to the three-match suspension UEFA handed him after his red card against Norway, while Paris Saint-Germain full-back Nuno Mendes is out injured. Goncalo Inacio is another absentee.

There are first senior call-ups for Costa and Varela, while Mora and Quenda will both be aiming to play their first match for Portugal, having previously been involved under Roberto Martinez, but failing to earn their first caps.

Portugal boast a perfect record in the Nations League, winning their first four games to guarantee a quarter-final place. They need just one point to clinch top spot in the group.

They face Denmark on November 14 before making the trip to the Cardiff City Stadium to face Craig Bellamy's Wales side three days later.

Iraola urges Liverpool to react positively to consecutive losses

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Andoni Iraola has called on Liverpool to respond to their back-to-back defeats when they travel to struggling Crystal Palace in the Premier League.

This time last week, Liverpool were on a five-game unbeaten streak, but that stretch ended with a 1-0 loss to Arsenal in the top-flight on Sunday.

It was then followed up with a 3-2 defeat away to Fenerbahce in the Champions League, with Liverpool twice giving up a lead to end their winning start in that competition.

With the international break now approaching, Iraola wants to see a quick response from his team to get back on track.

"I think that is the challenge, especially in a club like Liverpool," he said.

"One week ago, in this news conference [the talk was], we were on a run, everything was in a good place, and we were in a better mood.

"Now, after two defeats, it changes, and it has to be like this in clubs like ours. Now we have to react after two defeats. We need a win against Crystal Palace away.

"We have been in similar situations against Bournemouth before the international break and after the Brentford game. So, we have to go [to Palace], do a good job and win the game."

Next up: Crystal Palace (A) https://t.co/QfBd5VA6UN

Pierre Sage, meanwhile, is under vast pressure, with Palace sitting at the other end of the table in 18th, having lost each of their last three Premier League matches without scoring.

In all competitions, they have won just one of their last seven games (D2 L4), and they host Liverpool without the suspended Jean-Philippe Mateta after his red card in the 2-0 loss to Tottenham.

"As you know, JP was injured at the beginning of the season," Sage said. "There was the opportunity for Jorgen Strand Larsen and Eddie Nketiah to play.

"We have big confidence in the other players. They've shown they are able to play and they are able to compete.

"We need them in the play; we need them to press, we need them to score, to finalise the offensive play. We have some solutions."

Against Tottenham last weekend, Palace managed just two shots overall and only one on target, which came from Daichi Kamada.

The Japan international also salvaged the Eagles a late 1-1 draw with Hoffenheim in the Europa League, with that strike his fourth goal in his last 12 games for Palace, one more than he scored in his first 92 appearances for them.

Worcestershire trio aim to make more Cup magic

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

It's now 55 seasons since non-league Hereford United's 2-1 fourth-round replay win over top-flight Newcastle United entered English football folklore as arguably the greatest FA Cup shock of all time.

Elsewhere in the Midlands, though, there have been many other upsets and moments of cup magic too.

Telford United (in 1984) and Kidderminster Harriers (in 1994) both made it to the fifth round, and Worcestershire village side Alvechurch hold a record they will keep forever - unless the rules are changed again - after taking four replays to see off Oxford City in a qualifying round tie in 1971.

Worcester City were involved in a record-breaking penalty shootout against Scunthorpe in 2014 - the same year they famously beat Coventry City.

But, while the memory of that day at Hereford - Ronnie Radford et al - surpasses all other memories, across the Worcestershire border, it perhaps still irks a bit.

Accompanied by fallen 1970s non-league stars Stafford Rangers, Worcestershire clubs make up three of the four non-EFL Midlands sides to have made it through to the first round proper - the Harriers plus seventh-tier duo Alvechurch and Worcester, who all face EFL clubs. How will they fare this time?

5th in League One v 6th in Southern League Premier Central (Tier 7)

On paper, it looks a very tall order for Alvechurch. But not every non-league club has got an FA Cup pedigree like the Church's manager Kyle Storer.

The former Bedworth United, Tamworth, Hinckley United, Atherstone Town, Nuneaton Town, Kiddy Harriers, Wrexham, Cheltenham Town, Solihull Moors, Nuneaton Borough, AFC Telford United, Kettering Town and Alvechurch player is not just a walking advert for Midlands non-league football, he has got plenty of FA Cup "previous" to be taken into consideration.

"I've been very fortunate," Storer told BBC Midlands Today.

"I got to the fourth round with Kiddy Harriers, when we played at Sunderland in front of 40,000. And that was having beaten Peterborough over two games with a team that had Joe Lolley, Marvin Johnson and Chey Dunkley."

Kyle Storer (background) led Kiddy Harriers out for their 1-0 fourth-round defeat by Sunderland at the Stadium of Light [Shutterstock]

"And, when I was at Tamworth, we won at Bournemouth [Storer actually scored the winner], then took Stoke to a replay. And we got Norwich at home in third round the following year."

Dion Dublin and Darren Huckerby both netted twice in a 4-1 win but Storer had the last word by getting one back. "It was voted goal of the round too," he grins.

"I've had some amazing FA Cup runs but let's hope we can go and create another day to remember. Let's hope we can cause some damage.

"in terms of football prospects, it's a tough one. They're flying, aren't they? But, in financial terms, then it's amazing."

23rd in League Two v 17th in Southern League Premier Central (Tier7)

For the younger generations, ask any Worcester City fan what the FA Cup means to them and they will bring up the 2013-14 season.

City caused "Pandemonium at the Ricoh", as BBC Hereford & Worcester freelance reporter Dan Johnson excitedly put it, when they beat then League One side Coventry.

Then came that 14-13 spotkicks defeat by Scunthorpe United in that astonishing, record-breaking 30-penalty shootout.

City also made the first round the following season when, despite having Troy Deeney's brother Ellis in the side, they were well beaten 3-0 at Bramall Lane by Sheffield United.

This is their first appearance in the first round proper since then.

But, for the result that still counts as City's biggest FA Cup scalp, you have to go way back to January 1959 - and the arrival of Liverpool at their old St George's Lane home.

FA Cup highlights: Liverpool lose to Worcester City

It was 11 months before the great Bill Shankly arrived at Liverpool to create the legendary 'Mighty Red' machine which became kings of English football and conquered all of Europe in the 1960s, 70s and 80s.

At the time, they were still in Division Two - and not promoted until 1962 after eight years in the second tier - but it's a memory that then schoolboy, Roger King, will ever forget.

"The game should have been on the Saturday but it was postponed," Roger told BBC Midlands Today. "It was quite icy.

"I was at school then, Christopher Whitehead Secondary Modern. The game was rearranged for the following Wednesday afternoon and the only way you could attend was to get the ticket signed by my father, to take the headmaster and he gave you permission to have the afternoon off.

"I can't remember much of the game but I do remember the crowd. We got to the ground at one o'clock and didn't get in until gone two o'clock. St George's Lane was just a residential street and it was thronged with thousands.

"A lot of people talk about Hereford and their notable win, but we'll never forget our win over Liverpool."

Can City cause another upset on Saturday? Well, Danny Cowley's Colchester have not won since a 1-0 victory over Rochdale on 29 August - 12 games ago - and have slipped to 23rd in League Two.

It might not be Liverpool or Coventry, but it would be another significant scalp for Worcester. And it was in Essex 68 years ago that their Cup heroics all began, when they drew 0-0 with Chelmsford and beat them in a first-round replay.

13th in National League v 20th in League One

After early exits in the qualifying rounds over the last two seasons, when they were a sixth-tier side, regular cup fighters Kidderminster Harriers make ther first appearance in round one since losing 2-1 at home to Fleetwood in 2023.

After promotion in May, Harriers made a great start back in the fifth tier under new manager Paul Wotton. But they have now not won in eight league games - their only victory in that time coming at home to fellow Midlanders Solihull Moors in the fourth qualifying round.

Now they face Grant McCann's Doncaster, who have themselves won just three league games so far this season.

"You always want to win your next game," Wotton told BBC Hereford & Worcester. "And we're at home. And there's no pressure on us.

"I know the manager well. I've played against him many times and I've been assistant manager when he's been the manager on the opposite bench.

"We're full time, so we'll have no excuses fitness-wise but they're two leagues higher than us.

"It's still a big jump for us. We're a million miles from League One.

"But don't let anyone ever tell you can't do anything. We'll be as committed as we possibly can."

(17th in National League v 15th in League Two

(19th in League Two v 23rd in National League)

(2nd in Northern Premier League West Division v 6th in League Two)

Costacurta angered by ‘timid’ Milan performance vs. Ferencvaros: “One extreme to another”

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

After the defeat to Ferencvaros, the mood around AC Milan has worsened significantly, as you’d expect it to.

The win against Inter at the weekend has already been diminished in importance. It still means a lot, but the significance of it has been weighed down by yet another midweek defeat against – with respect – a lesser side.

Bournemouth, Bologna and now Ferencvaros, it feels like Milan are forced to recover with a strong performance after regular letdowns in midweek. Frustratingly, or maybe not depending on your viewpoint, they normally do the hard work, too.

The redemption seems to arrive regularly, but they always find a way to need it again. So, the importance of the derby win hasn’t become something to build momentum, but rather another sign of a two-tier squad.

Speaking after the game, Billy Costacurta – who has been nominated to enter the Hall of Fame – expressed his anger about the result, as he told Corriere dello Sport, via Football Italia.

“They were timid, one of the worst halves of the last four or five years. When you’re playing a European match, you have to go out there and do your best. The three that Amorim changed in the first half cannot start like that. How can you afford to step onto the pitch with that attitude?”

“After the derby, I did say that the difficult game would be tonight’s.

“There are good teams hiding in disguise in the Europa League. But Milan’s attitude was crazy. We’re going from one extreme to another. Rabiot also didn’t play well tonight.”

Giants’ offense vs. Eagles’ defense: The Vic Fangio factor

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The New York Giants emerge as winners from their Week 8 BYE.

They're (relatively) rested and still riding the emotional wave of a stunning overtime win in Week 7. They return to action this week as 3.5-point favorites (per FanDuel) on the road against the Philadelphia Eagles. The Eagles have been in a tailspin over the last several weeks, losing the last six games. They've lost each of the last three games by at least 11 points, including a deflating 27-10 loss to the Washington Commanders last week.

The Giants are looking to build on the momentum of their upset win over the Houston Texans, while the Eagles are just hoping to stop the skid.

What can J.J. McCarthy and the Giants' offense expect from the Eagles' defense?

The last six (counting overtime) quarters of Giants' football have turned former 10th overall pick J.J. McCarthy into a national story. Most had written McCarthy off after his rookie year was upended by injury, his sophomore year was a disappointment, and he fell to QB3 in his third year.

Now? McCarthy has catapulted himself back into the "QB1" conversation with an electric 11 minutes against the New Orleans Saints and a game full of explosive plays against the Houston Texans. This of course, begs the question: Can he do it again?

The Eagles now have about a game and a half worth of tape on the McCarthy-led Giants. That isn't much to go on, but it may be enough to form a more effective gameplan.

The good news is that McCarthy also has two more weeks in the Giants' offense and more tme practicing with the Giants' starters. Not to mention, McCarthy will get to enjoy the presence of a (hopefully) healthier Andrew Thomas and Jermaine Eluemunor. It seems likely that Eagles defensive coordinator Vic Fangio will look to contain the explosive passing plays that defined his performance against the Texans. McCarthy excelled at throwing deep against Houston, delivering some legitimate "WOW" throws. He'll need to capitalize on the opportunities to do so against the Eagles, but can't count on them being regular occurrences. Instead, the Giants' intermediate passing game will need to be more consistent on a down-to-down basis while they work to create opportunities for explosive plays.

If there was a blemish on McCarthy's game against the Texans, it's that his passing success rate* was 25th out of 25 qualifying quarterbacks.

*Success rate is defined as the rate at which a play that gains a percentage of the yards needed to convert a first down. A "successful" first down picks up 40 percent of the yards to convert, a successful second down picks up 60 percent of the yards to convert, and a successful third down picks up 100 percent of the yards to convert.

Explosive plays are great and go a long way toward making it more likely that a team scores on a given drive. However, a team can't rely on explosive plays to consistently move the ball down the field. While the Giants did an excellent job of scheming 1-on-1 opportunities and good receiving windows downfield for McCarthy, it's not something they can live on. McCarthy will need to be more consistent in hitting underneath passes to keep the offense moving and on schedule. That will be particularly true if the Giants' run game struggles with both Cam Skattebo and Tyrone Tracy injured.

The Giants finally got their run game going against the Texans. While the Texans' defense isn't what it was in 2025, they still filed one of the NFL's best run defenses, which makes the Giants' 180-yard, 3-touchdown performance all the more impressive.

The Eagles' run defense has decidedly mediocre this year. And their high Run Stop Win Rate but average YPC and high rushing EPA allowed (+11.45, 28th in the NFL) suggest that they're prone to giving up explosive plays on the ground. That would be good news for the Giants in light of their performance against the Texans, but there are suddenly doubts about the Giants' backfield.

Tyrone Tracy landed on injured reserve with a hamstring injury suffered early in the game, while Cam Skattebo is currently not practicing with a hamstring injury of his own. Skattebo's injury was suffered at the end of his final carry of the game, the 42-yard run which set up Devin Singletary's 30-yard touchdown run to win the game. Skattebo ended that run early when he felt his hamstring tighten, and while he didn't seem overly concerned after the game, he has yet to practice.

That leaves Singletary and the newly-added Greg Desrosiers as the Giants' healthy backfield, with practice squad backs Phil Mafah and Eric Gray as possible additions.

The Giants offensive line will need to play well. While the Eagles' defense has struggled this year, the trio of Jalen Carter, Jordan Davis, and Moro Ojomo remains dangerous.

The Giants will also need to depend on McCarthy's play to help unlock their running game. Not only will more consistent passing success help take the pressure off the run game, but the threat of McCarthy's legs will help unlock the running game. The Giants used a slow mesh to simulate a read-option play on Tyrone Tracy's touchdown, freezing a key Texans' defender. The Giants could make (judicious) use of the read-option with McCarthy in this game — as they did with Jaxson Dart in Week 1 against Fangio acolyte Christian Parker — to help supplement their rushing attack.

The Giants could also make use of Darnell Mooney as a pseudo-running back with sweeps and screen plays. Mooney has been disappointing as a receiver, but his athleticism and explosiveness have been apparent when he gets the ball in his hands. Doing so on schemed touches could be a way the Giants look to augment their rushing attack and adding an explosive element on the ground.

The Giants have spent most of the 2026 season facing defenses we've called "Fangio Adjacent". That is, defenses designed and called by coaches who have either been inspired by Vic Fangio, or his acolytes.

We've already been over the iterations enough to know the general shape of the "Fangio" defense.

It's based around a 4-2-5 nickel formation under a quarters coverage. He makes heavy use of pattern matching principles to add man coverage to his zone schemes, as well as using post-snap rotations to create ambiguity and doubt in quarterbacks' reads. While his defenses frequently start out in a Cover-4 look, they often rotate to Cover 3, adding a defender to the box, or a hybrid coverage like Cover-6 or Cover-8* to give them a numbers advantage to the passing strength.

*I go into more detail on Cover-6 and Cover-8 in our Week 1 preview of the Cowboys' defense.

Fangio doesn't blitz all that often, just 22.5% of the time (26th in the NFL), instead trusting his defensive front to get home.

While the details may shift with different coordinators and defensive rosters, the basic shape of the "Fangio" defense remains consistent and has become one of the most popular philosophies in the NFL. However, the biggest advantage that Fangio himself has always been that it's his. More than any other coach, Fangio not only knows how is defense is able to attack offenses, but is also intimately aware of it's weaknesses and how offenses go about attacking his scheme.

We saw that first-hand last year when Fangio deviated from his scheme on Thursday Night Football. That game he opted to blitz Dart heavily in his second start. Fangio has typically eschewed the blitz, instead trusting his defensive front to get pressure while playing coverage behind them. While the decision to blitz a rookie is typically correct, it proved disastrous against Dart. The weaknesses were easily exploited by the athletic young quarterback, and it stressed the defense in ways that it wasn't designed to sustain.

Two weeks later, Fangio went back to his roots and played his usual coverages and didn't blitz Dart. This time, the rookie struggled to identify the post-snap rotations and had one of his worst performances of a rookie year that was largely otherwise promising.

McCarthy was excellent when the Houston Texans employed MOFC (middle of field closed) looks. Whether it was Cover-1 (man) or Cover-3 (zone), single-high safety looks created defined passing lanes for McCarthy and he took advantage. While it's notable that the Giants' rushing offense finally got going against the Texans, their explosive passing attack helped carry the day when Houston's defense got them off schedule.

We should expect Fangio and the Eagles to be well aware of this and try to stick to their usual two-high shells against McCarthy.

The good news for the Giants is that the Eagles' pressure rates have been simply bad this year, as they've bled talent from their Super Bowl roster of two years ago. They might be forced to try and blitz McCarthy if he's too comfortable in the pocket, which could create weaknesses which the Giants could exploit.

We should also expect the Giants to make a concerted effort to run the ball and attack the second level of the field with Isaiah Likely, Malachi Fields, and their running backs. Doing so effectively could also help to force the Eagles out of their two-deep shells and into more vulnerable looks.

Cavs vs. 76ers: How to watch, odds, and injury report

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Cleveland Cavaliers open up their NBA Cup schedule as they host LeBron James and the Philadelphia 76ers.

This will be James's first game in the arena he called home for over a decade, since he spurned them in free agency last summer. James has received a hero's welcome in each of his returns when he was wearing purple and gold. That reception isn't guaranteed this time around.

The Sixers have shown early on why James chose them over the Cavs. Philadelphia has amassed the most wins so far by putting together the second-best defense in the league — an area that many people thought they would struggle with. The offense has been a work in progress, but they have enough individual shot makers to eke out enough points in the clutch to come away with victories.

The Cavs, conversely, are struggling. They've lost two games in a row and are just 1-4 against teams over .500.

This is also an NBA Cup game. The Cavs haven't advanced out of the group stage in this tournament yet. If they want to do so this season, they'll likely need to win this game, or at the very least, not lose by much.

What NBA Cup games are on Friday? 5 matchups to watch

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The second batch of NBA Cup group play games will take place on Friday, Nov. 6, with nine games on the schedule.

The schedule includes heavyweight matchups, with Western Conference contenders such as the Los Angeles Lakers facing the surprising Portland Trail Blazers, while the defending conference champion San Antonio Spurs look to maintain their strong start against the lowly Sacramento Kings.

The East slate of games features a stacked and balanced Philadelphia 76ers team taking on the Cleveland Cavaliers, plus an intriguing matchup between the Washington Wizards and Atlanta Hawks to start the evening.

MORE: Who is making a run at Doncic, Wembanyama? Latest NBA MVP rankings

The Luka Doncic train keeps rolling, as he's scored 40+ points in four of his last five games. Portland has the best record in the Western Conference, but will miss Ja Morant, sidelined for at least a month with a hamstring strain.

Can Victor Wembanyama possibly top his 52-point, 24-rebound performance in his last outing against the Dallas Mavericks? It's possible against the 1-7 Kings, who ranked 28th in defensive efficiency.

Oklahoma City has surprised despite a rash of devastating injuries, but most contain Grizzlies guard Ty Jerome, who has become a walking bucket over the first weeks of the season, with two 40-point games to his credit.

A possible Eastern Conference final matchup as Cleveland looks for revenge after blowing a 29-point lead to Philadelphia on Oct. 22. The 76ers have three 20-point-per-game scorers and have gotten off to an 8-1 start.

The Celtics have done just fine with Jaylen Brown, as Paul George has picked up the scoring slack from a formidable duo with Jayson Tatum. The Hornets have struggled, losing their last five games and are the only team in the league that doesn't average 100 points a game.

Washington Wizards at Atlanta Hawks (East Group C) — 7 ET, NBA League Pass

Boston Celtics at Charlotte Hornets (East Group C) — 7 ET, NBA League Pass

Philadelphia 76ers at Cleveland Cavaliers (East Group B) — 7:30 ET, Prime

Utah Jazz at Dallas Mavericks (West Group A) — 8 ET, NBA League Pass

Oklahoma City Thunder at Memphis Grizzlies (West Group B) — 8 ET, NBA League Pass

Toronto Raptors at Milwaukee Bucks (East Group A) — 8 ET, NBA League Pass

Minnesota Timberwolves at New Orleans Pelicans (West Group B) — 8 ET, NBA League Pass

Portland Trail Blazers at Los Angeles Lakers (West Group C) — 10 ET, Prime

San Antonio Spurs at Sacramento Kings (West Group C) — 10 ET, NBA League Pass

The 30 NBA teams are split into six groups of five (three in the Eastern Conference and three in the Western Conference, playing four group-stage games (two home, two away).

Each tournament game counts as a regular-season game, except the championship game on Dec. 11. Each team will play the other four teams in its group once, and its record in those games will determine whether it qualifies for the Knockout Rounds.

Eight teams will advance to the Knockout Rounds: the six Group Play winners and one wild card team from each conference.

Manchester United’s nine original ‘Fergie’s Fledglings’ & how they fared

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

While everyone remembers Manchester United‘s iconic Class of ’92, they weren’t the first group of young players that Sir Alex Ferguson worked with at Old Trafford.

Ferguson had some talented young players on his books in the late 1980s and they were the first group to be dubbed ‘Fergie’s Fledglings’ by the press.

We’ve taken a look back at Ferguson’s first batch of fledglings to see how they all fared.

After winning United’s Young Player of the Year award in 1987-88, Martin replaced Arthur Albiston and became their first-choice left-back over the following two seasons.

He only scored two goals for the club but one of them was the winner in the 1990 FA Cup final replay against Crystal Palace as Ferguson secured his first trophy at United.

But the defender struggled with injury problems after that FA Cup final win while Clayton Blackmore and Denis Irwin established themselves as United’s first-choice full-back pairing.

Martin was sold to Celtic in January 1994 and then had a spell at Bristol Rovers before seeing out his playing career in non-league football.

“If you’d told me at 16 I would play 110 games [for United] and score the winner in the FA Cup final I’d have snapped your hand off,” Martin told the Sun.

“I was a United fan, I still am, and it was just a great time. We all felt it was the start of a new team and to play my part in it is something I’m so proud of.”

Lee Martin scored in the #FACupFinal replay against Crystal Palace to secure Sir Alex Ferguson his first trophy as Manchester United manager. pic.twitter.com/dKaXvzuNX7

— Cantona Collars AKA Larry (@Cantona_Collars) May 17, 2021

Gill was handed his first-team debut early in Ferguson’s reign, filling in for the injured Bryan Robson during a 1-1 draw away against Southampton in January 1987.

“I’d never played in central midfield at United before so it was a surprise and I imagine very much a gamble for the manager,” Gill told Retro United.

“I was wearing Bryan Robson’s No.7 shirt and just so happened to wear New Balance boots at the time so I got a bit of stick in the dressing room along the lines of who did I think I was.”

But an Achilles tendon injury derailed Gill’s progress, keeping him on the sidelines throughout the 1987-98 season.

He started to become a first-team regular in 1988-89, only to suffer a broken leg and a shattered ankle during a game against Nottingham Forest in March 1989. Gill tried to recover but never played professional football again, retiring at the age of 22.

Ferguson wrote in 1992 that he would “undoubtedly have made it” if it wasn’t for injuries.

Alongside Martin and Gill, Wilson was part of the youth team that reached the 1986 FA Youth Cup final, but he made just six substitute appearances for United’s first team.

After loan spells at Lincoln City and Charlton Athletic, the midfielder was released by United at the end of the 1990-91 season.

He spent the next two seasons with Bristol Rovers before moving abroad, playing for teams in Finland and Sweden until his retirement in 2006.

Beardsmore made an immediate impact in United’s first team, registering a goal and two assists in a 3-1 win against Liverpool on just his second league start for the club.

He made a total of 73 appearances for the Red Devils but failed to live up to the initial hype and that Liverpool game ultimately proved to be the zenith of his United career.

The winger wasn’t named in the matchday squad for the 1990 FA Cup final or the 1991 European Cup Winners’ Cup final and failed to make a single Premier League appearance in 1992-93.

He joined Bournemouth on a free transfer in 1993 and made over 200 appearances during his five years at the club before a back injury forced him to retire at the age of 29.

Fergie's first fledglings of Lee Sharpe, Mark Robins and Russell Beardsmore combine to make it 3-1 against Liverpool on New Year's Day 1989

A lovely jinking run on the left by Sharpe and a wonderful volley from Russell Beardsmore past Mike Hooper #MUFC pic.twitter.com/miLt5BnSaD

— 80s&90sFootball ⚽ (@80s90sfootball) March 28, 2022

United fought off interest from Tottenham, Chelsea and Manchester City to sign a 17-year-old Brazil from Irish side Rivermount Boys in March 1986.

The right-back later became captain of United’s reserves but made just two cameo appearances for the first team before going on loan to Oldham Athletic and Swansea City.

“We were all looking over our shoulders at the Class of ’92 because we’d go and watch them play,” he told the 42. “They were only Under-16s, maybe, at the time but you’d sit there and watch and think, ‘Who are these boys?’ So we knew that was coming.

“And I think Fergie knew too and he almost used us as a template in many ways. A lot of us left because of the likes of those boys coming through.”

After spotting Gary Neville’s progress, he decided to join Cardiff City on a permanent deal in 1992 and went on to make over 100 appearances for the club.

Robins scored 17 goals in 70 first-team appearances for United, including the winner in an FA Cup third-round tie at Nottingham Forest which reportedly saved Ferguson’s job.

“I had no idea that goal against Nottingham Forest would become so important in the history of Manchester United,” Robins would later reflect in an interview with The Telegraph. “How could I at the time? Nobody knew it was a pivotal moment for Sir Alex Ferguson.”

But the striker failed to usurp Mark Hughes and Brian McClair and was deemed surplus to requirements following Dion Dublin’s arrival in the summer of 1992.

After joining Norwich City, he scored two goals in a 4-2 win against Arsenal on the opening day of the inaugural Premier League season and was also part of the side which famously defeated Bayern Munich in the UEFA Cup.

The 52-year-old left the Canaries in 1995 and then embarked on a nomadic career, playing for 11 different clubs over the following 10 years before hanging up his boots.

Despite being a prolific goalscorer in the youth teams, Graham made just four first-team appearances for United and scored his solitary goal for the club in an FA Cup tie against QPR.

The striker completed a £50,000 move to Barnsley in August 1991 but failed to make much of an impact at Oakwell, scoring just two goals in 42 appearances.

Short spells at Preston, Carlisle United, Stockport County, Scunthorpe and Halifax Town followed before he dropped into non-league football.

Unlike the Class of ’92, Fergie’s original fledglings didn’t just feature United academy graduates. Sharpe started his career at Torquay United before moving to Old Trafford shortly after his 17th birthday.

The winger quickly became a fan favourite at United due to his flair and won the PFA Young Player of the Year award in 1990-91 but was later usurped by the emerging Ryan Giggs,

While he didn’t live up to his potential, Sharpe still won three Premier League titles, two FA Cups, the League Cup and European Cup Winners’ Cup. He also won eight England caps, making him the only player on this list to have represented their national team at senior level.

Leeds United spent a club-record £4.5million on Sharpe in 1996, but he struggled with a series of injury problems and played just 35 times in three years, scoring six goals.

The 51-year-old later had stints at Bradford City, Portsmouth, Exeter City and Icelandic side Grindavik before retiring at Garforth Town.

Maiorana joined United from Eastern Counties League side Histon in a £30,000 deal after initially going on trial and impressing Ferguson in a friendly game against Birmingham City in 1988.

The winger showed glimpses of his raw talent during eight first-team appearances, but he had a turbulent relationship with the manager and was banished to the reserves.

“[Alex Ferguson] used to belittle me in front of people,” he told FourFourTwo. “Darren Ferguson would have his head in his hands, just shaking his head.

“Once, I went up to him and said, ‘What the f*ck has your dad got against me? What have I done to him?’ He didn’t know what to say. He was embarrassed. That happened on numerous occasions.”

After picking up a knee injury in a reserve game in 1991, he would only play five more first-team games in his entire career and they all came for Ljungskile SK in the Swedish second tier.
